# TextMouth architecture

This document explains how TextMouth is put together and, above all, which part of the app owns each kind of state: who keeps it, who may change it, and how everything else finds out about a change. It describes main as of October 10, 2026. When a change moves the ownership of some state, update its entry here in the same branch.

README.md covers building, testing, the files in the data directory, and keeping the library in the cloud. Paths below are relative to the TextMouth source folder unless they start with the name of another top-level folder, and names in the text are the names of Swift types, functions, and properties.

## The parts of the app

- One SwiftUI application. TextMouthApp (App/TextMouthApp.swift) declares three kinds of window:
  - Library windows (LibraryWindowView, ContentView, and LibraryView). The first opens at launch, and Command-N opens more.
  - Reader windows, one per book, identified by the book's ID (BookReaderWindowView, in the scene whose ID is BookReaderWindowView.windowID). Window restoration is off, so none reopen at launch.
  - The Settings window (SettingsView), with nine tabs, one for each SettingsTab case, at the fixed SettingsView.windowSize. SettingsTabContent matches each tab with its view, and SettingsTabPage draws every tab but Text in the grouped form style, which scrolls and fills the window's width, keeping checkboxes and bordered fields (SettingsTab.scrollsAsAWhole); the Text tab's rule list scrolls by itself. SettingsTabFitTests finds every AppKit control of each tab where the window shows it or a scroll view reaches it, and measures each tab's height and width without a window (TODO A10: in the columns style, the Shortcuts tab was 4,113 points tall in the 540-point window, and 223 points wider than any width it was given).
- Two more kinds of window are AppKit's, so that closing one can ask first. The Record window, titled New Recording (RecordingWindowView, in an NSWindow that RecordingWindowController.shared makes, one at a time, with the identifier "new-recording" and restoration off): TextMouthApp attaches the LibraryViewModel recordings are saved into as a Library window appears, and File > New Recording… and Library > New Recording… show it, as does the first Library window's appearance when recordings were left behind (takes left behind, below). And the audio editor's windows, one for each book being edited, titled "Edit Audio:" and the book's title (AudioEditorView, in NSWindows that AudioEditorWindowController.shared makes, with the identifier "audio-editor" and restoration off), which Edit Audio… opens through AudioEditorLauncher (the editor window, below).
- App-wide objects, created once by TextMouthApp and given to every window as environment objects:
  - LibraryViewModel: the library in memory, and every operation on it.
  - SettingsViewModel: the global settings, and the config.json mirror.
  - KeyboardShortcutManager: every action's keyboard shortcuts.
  - SoundEffectService: the sounds that playback events play.
  - LibraryWindowRouter.shared: which window takes each app-wide action.
- AppDelegate (App/AppDelegate.swift) starts the router before Finder can hand over a file, owns MenuVisibilityManager, turns off window restoration, and holds a quit until nothing would be lost: it stops a data-directory move that is copying, has the Record window ask about a take it holds (RecordingWindowController.holdQuit) and then each audio editor window about its unsaved edits (AudioEditorWindowController.holdQuit), asks when changes are unsaved (UnsavedChangesTerminationGuard), and then lets the feed downloads keep their place, for at most three seconds (FeedQuitHold). At quit it cancels a transcription under way, asking nothing, and ends the session lease.
- Each reader window has its own ReaderViewModel, which has its own SpeechService, AudioPlaybackService, and NowPlayingSession. Readers share SleepTimerService.shared, ReadingStatsService.shared, TextSubstitutionService.shared, ReaderAutoplayRequests.shared, ReadingListOpenRequests.shared, and SpeechRenderCoordinator.shared, and, through ReaderCloudServices, the installed book storage, BookCloudStates.shared, and ReaderWorkingCopies.shared.
- The source folders:
  - App: the entry point, the delegate, the menu commands, the build identity, the process runtime (TextMouthRuntime), and the check made before quitting.
  - Models: value types and small rule tables, such as Book, BookInfo, Library, BookContentIndex, SettingsPolicy, StoredPreference, and the wording helpers.
  - Parsers: one reader per format, each turning a source file into DocumentContent (chapters, headings, and an index), plus shared readers for archives, packages, and text encodings.
  - Services: everything that owns a file, a queue, or a system resource. Services/Feeds holds the feed engine, whose FeedCenter.shared LibraryViewModel starts and offers as feeds, Services/AudioEditing the audio editing engine, which works without a window (audio edits, below), and Services/MP3 the MP3 encoder over the LAME library that project.yml builds from Vendor/lame (an MP3 file TextMouth writes, below).
  - ViewModels: LibraryViewModel and ReaderViewModel, each split into extensions by topic, plus SettingsViewModel, BookSearchModel, and LibraryBookUpdates.
  - Views: the SwiftUI views of the library, the reader, settings, the Record window, the audio editor window (Views/AudioEditor), the Export dialog (Views/Export), transcription's sheets, dialogs, and settings group (Views/Transcription), and the feed reader's interface (Views/Feeds), plus VoiceOverFocus.swift, which places VoiceOver's focus in new windows and sheets, and ViewBodyCounter.swift, which counts body evaluations for the hosted tests.
- Concurrency: the view models, the window router, the settings mirror, the sleep timer, the statistics, the books' cloud states, the books kept on this Mac, the list cache, the live reading positions, and the voice list run on the main actor. Disk work runs on queues that each own their work: serial queues for the library index and for package records (LibraryPersistenceService), for imports (SerialImportQueue), and for logs (LogWriter), and at most two workers for cache updates (TextCacheUpdateQueue). Each import, text extraction, and book analysis on those queues runs through StorageBoundary.perform, which a data-directory move holds back and waits for, so none runs across the move's publication; the move then copies the data directory off the main actor, while every Library window's sheet keeps the library from changing. Rebuild Directory and the folder edits made from the library's sheets hold storage work the same way; a folder edit moves its packages and rewrites their records off the main actor, then publishes the new rows on it, keeping any change made to the library meanwhile (performLibraryTransition). Work that can wait on a drive or the network never runs on the main thread. The list's cloud refreshes and commands call the cloud services from detached tasks, and work that holds its thread for as long as it takes runs on a global dispatch queue (ReaderViewModel.offMain, OffMainWork) rather than on one of Swift's cooperative threads: the reader's requests to the book storage and its reads of a book, the check of a folder chosen for the library, the count of books only in the cloud before a move, a move's copy, and a folder edit's moves. The audio editor's level measurement reads its composition on a Thread of its own for the same reason: a reader may block for as long as CoreMedia takes, and on October 6 a few cancelled measurements blocked there starved the cooperative pool. A read that must never download runs inside DatalessFiles.withoutDownloading, which leaves dataless files in the cloud for its own thread only. Other work leaves the main thread too. The look-ahead prepares the speech of a chapter too long to render ahead on a global queue, which ChapterSpeechInputs allows by being values and prepare() by being pure, and the main actor keeps the result only while the chapter's inputs are unchanged. Rendered speech is fed to the engine's player node from RenderedSpeechFeeder's own serial queue, which every call on the node goes through, so a busy main thread never leaves the node without audio, and a completion handler of the node never waits on the feeder. The installed voices are listed on a detached task (VoiceCatalog), launch finds the books its cache batch must rebuild on a utility queue, where its metadata refresh reads the files of the rows an older reader read too, and Storage settings ask whether the data directory exists off the main thread. Transcription runs on an actor of its own (TranscriptionService), one book at a time, with its blocking reads on a global queue (OffMainWork); the main actor's TranscriptionJobCenter saves each transcript on a global queue too, a window's TranscriptionPresenter deletes one there, and its Clean Up sheet reads the cleanup's rules, cleans a transcript up, and undoes a cleanup there. The Record window's capture backend (CaptureSessionRecordingBackend) is an actor running on a serial queue of its own, since a capture session blocks while it starts and stops. The feed engine runs on the main actor; a feed's fetch, its parse, and a first merge run off it, its records, this Mac's fetch details, and its imports are written on queues of their own, and the files its after-playing sweep and newest-N rule remove go on the imports' queue (FeedDownloadManager.runBesideImports(_:)), once the records and the feeds' indexes are written on the main actor. Audio editing reads, renders (LAME encoding an MP3 within a render), and deletes originals off the main thread, as storage work (StorageWork.audioEditing), each change of a book's edit history under one lock (AudioEditHistoryStore), and an installed ffmpeg runs as a child process, never on the main thread; the editor window's model runs on the main actor, measuring the edited audio's levels off it, and plays through an AVPlayer. Every target builds in the Swift 6 language mode, set once in project.yml (TextMouthTests/Package.swift matches it), so concurrency diagnostics are errors, and `make check` fails on any Swift warning and on a Release app larger than 50 MB (11 MB on October 3, 2026, once `make build` stopped compiling with code coverage and the symbols were stripped into a dSYM that `make install` keeps for the last five installs, and 15.4 MB on October 4, with that day's features and LAME's encoder). The mode adds runtime isolation checks to `@objc` methods of main-actor classes and to main-actor closures handed to Objective-C APIs; each of the app's checks runs on the main thread, and VoiceWarmUp's buffer callback relies on AVSpeechSynthesizer calling back on the thread that called write(). SpeechService handles every callback of its direct synthesizer on the main queue, so its announcement of a text the speech engine refused, made only when no reader listens, asserts the main actor there (endDirectSpeechRefused(at:refusing:spanning:)). A render's RenderCancellationObserver, called on whatever thread the synthesizer uses, only sets a flag in an OSAllocatedUnfairLock and signals the semaphore the render queue waits on; it is Sendable, as the SDK makes every synthesizer delegate (AVSpeechSynthesizerDelegate is NS_SWIFT_SENDABLE), and the compiler checks it, with no `@unchecked`. Speak Info's LibraryInfoSpeechDelegate ends an announcement at once when called on the main thread, and otherwise on the next turn of the main queue, and the reader's WhereAmIDelegateHelper hands each finish and cancel of an announcement to the main queue. UncheckedConcurrencyPolicyTests requires a comment naming a lock, queue, actor, or thread on the three lines above each `@unchecked Sendable` or `nonisolated(unsafe)` declaration and pins their counts (26 and 14, the fourteenth the record of each feed's index on disk, LibraryPersistenceService.feedIndexesOnDisk, confined to the save queue since FR11's branch A1).

## Launch

- TextMouthApp's initializer installs the real cloud services (CloudServices.installSystemServices) before anything else runs: SwiftUI creates LibraryViewModel before applicationWillFinishLaunching, and the library's launch check uses them.
- AppDelegate.applicationWillFinishLaunching starts the router before any window exists, so a book Finder hands over waits for the first Library window instead of opening a second.
- The data directory's first location is decided when anything first asks for it (StorageBoundary.shared): TextMouthPaths.root(for:) names the folder the preferences hold, or the default location. When a custom location is missing but its bookmark leads to a folder holding this library under another path, as after a rename or a remount under another name, that folder is the data directory, and the preferences name it from then on.
- LibraryViewModel's initializer copies a sandboxed release's data on first launch (through DataDirectoryMigrator, keeping the original), starts listening for the data directory's loss, and checks the data directory (checkDataDirectory), reading metadata only. Nothing is created or loaded until the check finds the library: then startLibrary creates the data directory's folders, records the library's identity, removes leftover archive extraction folders, loads the library index (loadLibraryIndex), sweeps away the originals of recordings and edited audiobooks whose time is up (sweepAudioOriginalsInBackground), clears what the audio editor left in this Mac's caches when TextMouth last ended with editors open (removeStaleAudioEditCachesInBackground), starts the background work below, and begins the session lease. Otherwise every Library window shows Library Not Available, and the Try Again that first finds the library runs startLibrary.
- Loading reads library.json and every feed's own index, the feeds' rows onto the library's feed shelf (feed items, below), and repairs the index against the disk (migrateOldBooksIfNeeded): every feed item's row waits on the shelf meanwhile, books removed with Remove from Library Only stay out, the books the checkpoint journal names take the newer reading positions their records hold (adoptNewerRecordedPositions), equivalent folder rows merge, stale file paths are fixed, legacy books move into packages, packages that two books shared are separated, misplaced packages move into their folders, packages missing from the index are adopted, the feed items whose files are here join the books (joinFeedItems), and the result is saved. Every check and read of the repair leaves dataless files in the cloud, and nothing is moved, rewritten, adopted, separated, relocated, or taken out on the strength of a check that met a dataless folder or a read that was refused; one MAINTENANCE_SKIPPED_IN_CLOUD line counts the books left as they were. The look for a removal marker, which is almost always absent, stops at the first folder above it that is there and not dataless, the package itself, rather than asking every folder up to the data directory. An unreadable index skips all of this and enters recovery instead (below).
- LibraryViewModel then brings every book up to date in the background (updateContentCachesAndStatistics): after the metadata refresh, the words of places saved before anchors (captureMissingTextAnchors, T06's branch B), then every book's text cache, and after that its statistics, leaving books whose files are in the cloud as they are. The batch finds the books whose caches need work off the main thread (booksNeedingCacheUpdate, on a utility queue), and starts on the main actor once it has them. Once that maintenance has ended, and only when the statistics were read from their file (ReadingStatsService.statsReadFromFile; over stand-ins for a missing, unreadable, cloud-only, or unavailable file nothing is moved, recorded, or logged, and a later launch makes the move), the library puts each book Books Finished counts whose row reads 100 percent (99.5 or more, rowsHundredPercent) exactly at its end, once on each Mac (moveFinishedBooksToTheirEndsOnce, P07; the preference library.finishedBooksAtEndVersion, kept on this Mac alone): it reads each book's content index off the main thread, with dataless files left in the cloud, and saves the end through persistReadingPosition(bookID:position:markingOpened: false), as a reader saves a place but keeping the book's lastOpened in its row and record (Library.updateReadingPosition(bookID:position:markingOpened:), whose default dates a reader's save as an opening). A book whose record or index is only in the cloud, or that a reader has open or opens meanwhile, keeps its place, and the move is recorded as made only once none waits, so a later launch makes it then; a book whose index disagrees with its row's measure is left. One FINISHED_BOOKS_AT_END line counts what it did.
- startLibrary also starts the feed engine in the background (startFeeds, FeedCenter.start(library:)): it moves the feed settings that the List and download choice retired to the choice, once, before anything reads it (FeedSettings.moveToItemPolicyIfNeeded(in:)), reads every feed's record off the main thread, moving a feed's retired kept-entries number to a choice of its own (moveKeptEntriesOverrides()), reads this Mac's fetch details, and sweeps away what interrupted downloads left; then, outside a process hosting tests, it sends the hidden .removing- folders a Delete Download cut short left in the feeds' own folders to the Trash, or deletes them, as Removal Behavior says (sweepRemovalHolders(), through FeedItemPackage.removalHolders(in:); it runs only while feedLibraryIsComplete, so never while the library index is recovered; FeedItemPackage.removalHolder(for:beside:) names holders .removing-<book ID>-<UUID>, and sweepHolder(_:puttingBackInto:remove:) puts back what a still-downloaded row's package lacks (packageWaitingForItsFiles) and sends the rest through FeedEnvironment.removeItem folder by folder (HolderSweep); FEED_HELD_ASIDE_SWEPT, or FEED_HELD_ASIDE_LEFT for one left for the next launch), asks again for the downloads under way or waiting when TextMouth last quit (resumeDownloadsSavedAtQuit), deletes the played downloads that are due, runs the newest-N rule's first pass over every feed (the List and download choice, under Feeds, below), watches feedItems and feedNewestCount for the rule (itemPolicyChanged(for:)), refreshes every feed when feedRefreshAtLaunch is on, and refreshes them again every feedRefreshIntervalHours, never holding the library back. Once the records have loaded, the library moves the downloads made before FR11 into their feeds' own folders, returns to the library, as books, the downloads of each feed whose folder is here while its record is gone, and marks downloaded again the items an older TextMouth marked otherwise whose files are on this Mac (moveFeedDownloadsOnceRecordsLoad(); feed items, below).
- SettingsViewModel's initializer applies config.json, reconciles it with the preferences, and starts ConfigService watching the file. Before it applies the file (applyFileAtLaunch), the first launch whose preferences hold no record of the shortcuts the user edited makes one from the shortcuts saved before (KeyboardShortcutManager.moveSavedShortcutsIfNeeded; keyboard shortcuts, below). While the data directory is unavailable, ConfigService reads, writes, and watches nothing, and it reconciles the file once the library is back.

## Who owns each kind of state

Each entry names a kind of state, then its owner, who may write it, how others observe it, and the files involved.

### The library index

- The library: every book's row, the folders, each location's sort, and the reading list (below).
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel.library, published on the main actor. It is the live copy; library.json, with each feed's own index (feed items, below), is its saved form. Each location's sort is kept in folderSortSettings under the location's key (LibraryLocation.sortSettingsKey): a folder's path in Folders, and in every other category a reserved key (LibraryCategory.sortSettingsKey: __allItems__, __unopened__, __recent__, and __readingList__, under which nothing is saved), which no folder may take as its name (BookFolder.reservedNames, made from those keys and the former No Folder category's __noFolder__).
  - Who may write it: LibraryViewModel and its extensions, on the main actor. Each change is followed by save(), which hands a snapshot to LibraryPersistenceService.save(_:). That waits half a second on the service's serial queue, and only the newest snapshot is written, so an older snapshot never lands after a newer one. A reading checkpoint goes through saveReadingCheckpoint(_:of:writingWithin:) instead, which lets its snapshot wait up to five minutes (readingCheckpointDelay), since the book's record holds the checkpoint meanwhile: any ordinary save writes it sooner, and writePendingIndexSoon() brings it forward when a reader closes (unregisterOpenBook) and when a record cannot take its checkpoint, being only in the cloud or failing to be written. Until an index write holds every checkpoint, ReadingCheckpointJournal names the books whose records are ahead in reading-checkpoints.json, noting each before its record is written, and launch's repair gives each book it names its record's newer position and last-opened date (booksWithCheckpointsAhead() and adoptNewerRecordedPositions). flushPendingSave() writes a waiting snapshot before the app quits. Every write replaces library.json atomically, as compact JSON (encodedIndex(_:)), after writing each feed's index whose bytes changed or whose file is gone (feed items, below).
  - How others observe it: SwiftUI views observe LibraryViewModel. A reader takes a copy of its book when it opens and reports back through LibraryViewModel: updateLiveReadingPosition (live reading positions, below, which change nothing in the library), persistReadingPosition (the row and the package record at once, library.json within five minutes), and updateContentIndex. Every change of library is offered first to LibraryViewModel.listCache (the lists Library windows show, below), then to LibraryViewModel.bookUpdates, which keeps the copies of open readers current (a reader's copy of its book, below). No timer republishes the library: nothing a Library window shows depends on the clock, and a value that ever does should use a TimelineView rather than a tick that redraws every window. Every window, menu, and list reads a location's sort through Library.sortState(at:), from Library.sortSetting(at:) (Models/LibrarySortState.swift). At the Reading List, which shows an order of its own (LibraryLocation.keepsItsOwnOrder), that is always FolderSortSetting.readingListOrder, shown as manual order, and userChangedSort(_:at:) saves nothing there. Anywhere else it is the first of these that exists: the setting saved under the location's key; in Unopened alone, which took No Folder's place on October 7, 2026, the setting saved under No Folder's key, __noFolder__, unless its field is manual order, which was the arrangement of the root's books alone; and the category's default (title ascending; last opened descending in Recently Opened; date added descending in Unopened, FolderSortSetting.unopenedDefault, since no unopened book has been opened). Nothing is written under __noFolder__ again, so a sort chosen in Unopened, saved under __unopened__, takes that one's place for good. Outside the Reading List, userChangedSort(_:at:) saves only a sort that LibrarySortState.applying(_:) changed: choosing the field shown again returns the sort unchanged (A11), so nothing is saved, and only LibrarySortChange.reverseDirection, the menu's Sort Ascending or Sort Descending, reverses a direction.
  - Files: Models/Library.swift, Models/LibrarySortState.swift, Models/BookFolder.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift and its extensions, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, Services/LibraryLoadResult.swift.
- Live reading positions: where readers are in their books between checkpoints, for the rows and the summary bar.
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel.livePositions, a LiveReadingPositions on the main actor, holding one LiveReadingPosition for each book, kept for the session.
  - Who may write it: readers, about once a second while they play, through LibraryViewModel.updateLiveReadingPosition; a book no longer in the library changes nothing. When openBookIDs changes, a book no reader has open drops its entry (keepOnly), its reader having saved its position to the library as it closed.
  - How others observe it: a book's row (BookRow) observes its own book's entry, so a live position redraws that row alone, and the summary bar (TimeRemainingBar) observes the store; the library model publishes nothing. A live position is shown in place of the row's own only while it is the newer (ReadingPosition.lastUpdated), so a checkpoint, or a position another window saved, takes over at once.
  - Files: ViewModels/LiveReadingPositions.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+BookOperations.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift.
- The lists Library windows show: each location's books in its sort, narrowed by a search, and the book counts of folder trees.
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel.listCache, a LibraryListCache on the main actor, which derives each list once and keeps it, at most LibraryListCache.entryLimit lists, until a change it depends on. It publishes nothing, so a window may derive and store a list while SwiftUI draws.
  - Who may write it: the library itself, which offers every change of library to listCache.libraryDidChange(from:to:) before anything reads it. A change of membership (which books there are, their order in the library, their folders, and their feed links), of the reading list's books or their order (LibraryListCache.readingListMayDiffer, which its options never change), or of a field a sort or a search reads (the title, the author, the dates added and last opened, the manual order, and the book's file) drops every list; any other change, such as a word count or a voice setting, keeps every list's order and refreshes only its books' values. A reading checkpoint stamps the book's last-opened date, so it sorts the lists again. Folder trees are counted in one pass whenever the lists are dropped, from the library's own books, and the sizes a File Size sort reads are measured once per book, until its file changes. A list's key (LibraryListCache.Key) holds, for a feed's folder in Folders, the feed whose items it lists, so the list is derived again when that changes although no book did.
  - How others observe it: a Library window reads its list through displayedBooks(at:matching:) and bookCount(inFolderTree:) as often as it draws: the first read after the lists were dropped derives its list again, and the first after any other change refreshes the list's books. Before sorting, a location lists LibraryViewModel.booksForCategory's books: a folder's in Folders, with its feed's items for a feed's folder (feedItems(listedIn:)); every book of the library's own in All Items (Library.libraryBooks); in Unopened every book of its own never opened, from every folder (Library.unopenedBooks(), the books Book.isUnopened names, those whose lastOpened is nil, as a record's BookState.isNew says too); in Recently Opened the ten rows opened last, books and feed items alike, of each feed's items only the one opened last, which takes one of the ten places (LibraryBookSorter.recent(_:limit:)); and at the Reading List the list's books in its order (readingListBooks), which displayedBooks(at:matching:) never sorts, a search narrowing that order. A feed's items are listed nowhere else (feed items, above). Opening a book stamps its lastOpened, which the lists depend on, so the book leaves Unopened as the list is derived again. LibraryLocation.manualOrderFolder is nil in All Items, Unopened, Recently Opened, and the Reading List, whose books come from many folders that share no one order. What dragging and Move Book Up and Move Book Down rearrange is LibraryLocation.reorderTarget(sortedBy:), a LibraryReorderTarget: a folder's manual order in Folders, the root included, while the list shows it, or the reading list at the Reading List, always. LibraryViewModel.moveBook(_:direction:in:) and reorderBooks(in:from:to:) take a move or a drag there, and each move made from the Library menu, the keyboard, or a row's context menu is announced alike (ManualBookOrder.MoveResult.announcement, as in "Moved Bestiary above Almanac"). A row's Move Up and Move Down VoiceOver actions go through LibraryView's moveSayingPlace(_:direction:), which moves through moveBook(_:direction:in:) as well and announces the book's new place instead (MoveResult.placeAnnouncement, "Dune, 3 of 12", MoveResult.count being the order's length as the list shows it), or, when the list changed after the row offered the move, ManualBookOrder.cannotMoveAnnouncement ("Dune can't move up.").
  - Files: ViewModels/LibraryListCache.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+FolderManagement.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+SortAndOrder.swift, Models/LibraryBookSorter.swift, Models/LibrarySortState.swift.
- The reading list (FR09): books from any folder, in an order the user arranges, and what a book opened from it does when it ends.
  - Owner: Library.readingList, a ReadingList (Models/ReadingList.swift), saved in library.json with the rest of the index: the books' IDs in reading order, each once, and two options, continuation (ReadingList.Continuation: playBookBelow, the default, playBookAbove, or stop, saved by its raw value, never by the words the options bar shows) and skipsFinished (true until cleared). It is read leniently: an ID that can't be read is skipped, one listed twice is kept where it first appears, an option missing or unknown reads as its default, and a list that can't be read at all is left empty, so it never makes the index unreadable.
  - Who may write it: LibraryViewModel+ReadingList, every change through editReadingList(_:), which edits the list as the window shows it, any ID naming no book left out, and makes a change one change of library and one save, and a request that changes nothing no save: addToReadingList and removeFromReadingList, whose announcing forms serve a row's, a selection's, the edit-mode bar's, and the Library menu's commands and say what they did, or why nothing changed, in ReadingListWording's words, each through LibraryView's changeReadingList(_:with:), which at the Reading List also takes the books taken off the list out of both selections, so the edit-mode bar counts the books still shown; moveInReadingList and reorderReadingList, for Move Book Up and Down and a drag; and setReadingListContinuation and setReadingListSkipsFinished, for the options bar. An import made at the Reading List (ImportRequest.addsToReadingList, set through the addingToReadingList: parameter of importFiles, importDAISYFolders, importZipAsync, importSingleFileAsync, and createBookFromText) adds its books to the list's end at every place its books join the library, a ZIP batch's together, through addImportedBooksToReadingList(_:request:), which announces ReadingListWording.importedAnnouncement(added:folderPath:), naming the folder the books went to, or LibraryFolderOption.rootLabel; a book already in the list stays where it is, and nothing is said when none joins. A duplicate's Replace calls ReadingList.replace(_:with:) before removeDeletedBook, so the new copy takes the old one's place before the old one leaves; a Replace at the Reading List of a book not on the list adds the copy to its end, as Keep Both does. Library.removeBook(id:) and removeFolderAndContents(id:) take a removed book off the list, so every way a book leaves the library ends there, the books Remove from Library Only retired included (retirement markers, below). The list's order is its own: moving a book in it never touches a folder's manual order.
  - How others observe it: LibraryCategory.readingList, the Library window's fifth category, lists readingListBooks, which a search narrows and nothing sorts. A book row carries ReadingListCommands (Add to Reading List for its books not in the list, Remove from Reading List for those in it, each counting a selection's books), as context menu items and as its last VoiceOver actions, and, in edit mode, EditModeBar shows ReadingListCommand.editBarCommands(for:inList:atReadingList:) beside Move: those a selection's row would offer, or, with nothing selected, the one the view suggests, acting on no book and so dimmed (Remove at the Reading List, Add elsewhere), worded by ReadingListWording.editBarTitle, editBarLabel(_:count:), which counts the books as Move's label does, and editBarHint, and run through LibraryView's performReadingListCommand; a book row also carries, outside the Reading List, the accessibility value "In reading list" (ReadingListWording.inReadingListValue), the empty string otherwise, so the row keeps its identity, and VoiceOver its place on it, when its own command changes the book's membership. At the Reading List, ReadingListOptionsBar shows the options, the Sort menu's one item is ReadingListWording.orderName, checked, the window is titled "Library: Reading List", and an empty list shows ReadingListWording.emptyList as a row. Speak Info says a book's place in the list in every view, the brief summary and the full alike (BookInfoFacts.readingListPlace, a ReadingListPlace, which LibraryInfoWording.briefParts(of:) says through readingListNote(_:) after the place note, "In reading list, 3 of 12"). LibraryOrderCommandsSupport (Views/Library/LibraryView.swift) takes the Library menu's Move Book Up and Down and Add to and Remove from Reading List, which post addToReadingList and removeFromReadingList, for the Library window that takes the command (WindowCommandRouting); ShortcutAction.addToReadingList and .removeFromReadingList are in the Library group, with no default keys. readingListNeighbor(of:direction:skippingFinished:) and readingListContinuation(after:) answer what a reader of a book from the list asks (reading from the reading list, under the reader, below). A book counts as finished by LibraryViewModel.finishedBookIDs, what Books Finished records (ReadingStats.finishedBookIDs), which tests replace: a deliberate record, unlike a position near the end (P07), kept when the book is read again.

- Feed items (FR11): the episodes and articles a feed downloaded, their packages, and each feed's own index.
  - Owner: the library model, with each feed's index on disk. A feed item's row is a Book whose feedLink (Models/FeedBookLink.swift: the feed's ID and the item's) names the feed and item it came from. The link travels with the row's file location (Book.FileLocation, so a reader following a moved row finds the package) and with the package record (BookMetadata.feedLink, which makeBookInfo() and Book(record:filePath:folderPath:tmbDirectoryName:) carry both ways, a save writing the row's link or else keeping the record's), and it makes tmbDirectory place the package in the feed's own folder, Feeds/<feed ID>/, beside the feed's record (Book.packageDirectory(inDataDirectory:); Services/Feeds/FeedPaths+Items.swift: itemsFolder(of:in:), indexFile(of:in:), packageDirectory(named:of:in:), and feedID(ofItemsFolderNamed:), which takes a folder named by a feed's ID alone, so FeedStore never takes the folder for a record). Each feed's index is Feeds/<feed ID>/library.json (FeedIndex, Services/Feeds/FeedIndex.swift: its layout version, 1, the feed's ID, and a row for each package, as library.json would hold the row, its link included), which FeedIndexFiles.encoded(_:) writes as compact JSON with sorted keys and its rows by date added, so one index is always the same bytes. Reading one checks its layout version first (FeedIndex.init(from:), a missing one read as 1), and one of a later version throws FeedIndexNewerLayout. While TextMouth runs, a feed item's row is in one of two places in the Library: among books while its file is here, so the reader, positions, bookmarks, settings, the reading list, and Stats reach it as they reach any book; or else on Library.feedShelf (FeedShelf, Models/LibraryFeedShelf.swift: the rows held apart, which no list, count, or pass meets, among them, since FR11's branch A2, each row kept after its file went, holding the listener's place; and indexedFeedIDs, the feeds whose indexes the library keeps, so a feed whose last row left gets an empty index and a feed no library named is never written; and indexesLeftAlone, the feeds whose index files no writer touches while TextMouth runs: one only in the cloud, one of a later layout, and a gone feed's while its downloads return to the library, leaveIndexAlone(of:) and forgetIndex(of:); and keepIndex(of:), for a feed whose index is written though its last row left, as Move to Library… leaves it). Library.libraryBooks and libraryBookCount are the library's own books, every row without a link, and Library.feedIndexRows is each feed's rows, wherever they are, leaving out the feeds in FeedShelf.indexesLeftAlone, for every writer. Each feed's rows by the identity of the item each came from are FeedItemRows (downloaded, among the books, and kept, on the shelf), which the model's FeedItemRowLookup works out once for each change of the library, keyed by the list cache's content revision, for feedItemRows(of:) and feedBook(withID:), so a feed's counts, an item's state (FeedCenter.state(of:in:) and downloadedRow(of:in:)), whether it is new, and a download restoring a kept row each find a row in one step rather than by a scan.
  - Who may write it: a download publishes its package into the feed's folder (FeedEpisodeImporter.destinationPackageURL, which makes the folder), and LibraryViewModel.addFeedBook(_:) adds its row with its link and no library folder (downloads, under Feeds, below). Every save goes through LibraryPersistenceService.writeIndexFile, the one write path of library.json, which first writes each feed's index from Library.feedIndexRows (writeFeedIndexes(of:), through FeedIndexFiles.encodedIndexes(of:placedIn:), each row placed where its package is by FeedIndexFiles.placed(_:in:of:), and FeedIndexFiles.write(_:in:fileManager:)), only an index whose bytes differ from what this process last read or wrote of it, or whose file is gone (feedIndexesOnDisk, confined to the save queue), and then library.json, whose encoding (Library.encode(to:)) writes only libraryBooks, so no writer of library.json, a save, a checkpoint, the quit flush, a retry, Rebuild Library Index, or the data-directory move, can hold a feed item's row. A feed's index that can't be written throws FeedIndexWriteError, naming its file, before library.json is touched, and is reported and retried as a failed library index write. Reading library.json clears any link a row holds (Library's decoder, through Book.placedInLibraryFolder()), as orphan adoption and a package import do for a package found in the Library folder, whose record keeps the link: only a package in its feed's folder is a feed item's. At launch, LibraryPersistenceService.load() reads every feed's folder onto the shelf (loadFeedShelf(fromPackages:reader:), FeedIndexFiles.readAll(in:fromPackages:reader:), reading each index without downloading it through an injectable reader), each row placed where its package is, so moving the data directory or opening the library on another Mac rewrites no index first; an index that can't be read is preserved beside itself (SavedFile.preserveUnusable, FEED_INDEX_UNREADABLE) and its rows rebuilt from the packages' records, and a package the index doesn't list is read from its record too (FEED_INDEX). An index only in the cloud, whose download is asked for (FEED_INDEX_IN_CLOUD), or of a later layout (FeedIndexNewerLayout, FEED_INDEX_NEWER) is left alone (FeedIndexFiles.Contents.isLeftAlone, kept in FeedShelf.indexesLeftAlone): its rows come from the packages' records, no writer writes it while TextMouth runs, and a library.json write meanwhile keeps the checkpoint journal. The startup repair (migrateOldBooksIfNeeded) shelves every feed item's row first (Library.shelveFeedItems()), since its repairs reconcile the library's own books with the Library folder, and at its end joinFeedItems(journaled:access:skips:) takes the newer positions the checkpoint journal names from their records for every shelf row, and moves the rows whose files are here, or in the cloud, to books; a library.json row sharing a joining row's ID gives way to the feed item's, whose package is the one here, and a shelf row sharing a book's ID that stays is dropped. While library.json can't be read, the provisional library takes each feed's rows from its packages' records, and Try Again and the data directory's return keep the session's (carryingRecoverySessionState(from:into:), through feedItems(of:over:), in LibraryViewModel+IndexRecovery.swift). Once the feed engine's records load (moveFeedDownloadsOnceRecordsLoad()), LibraryViewModel+FeedItemMove.swift moves the downloads made before FR11 that a feed's record names as downloaded (FeedItemState.bookID), subscribed to or not, into their feeds' folders (moveDownloadsIntoTheirFeeds(records:), holding the storage work as moving books between folders does), leaving a book where it is when its row names no package the library manages (FileImportService.namesManagedPackage(_:), FEED_DOWNLOAD_MOVE_REFUSED): each package moves in one rename, its row takes its link and its record is written there (moveIntoItsFeed(_:link:root:), through relocatePackage(of:as:root:)), and LibraryPersistenceService.saveAndWait(_:) writes the feeds' indexes and then library.json at once, so a crash between any two steps is mended at the next launch (FEED_DOWNLOADS_MOVED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_MOVE_FAILED). It then returns the downloads of each feed whose record is gone (below), and marks downloaded again each item an older TextMouth marked not downloaded, when its file is on this Mac (markFeedItemsDownloadedAgain(access:), FEED_ITEMS_DOWNLOADED_AGAIN); a row whose file is not goes back to its feed's shelf as kept (FEED_ITEMS_KEPT). Move to Library… (FR11's branch D; ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+MoveToLibrary.swift: moveToLibraryReturningOutcome(_:toFolder:), for the rows booksMovableToLibrary(_:) keeps, each an item's download whose record says it is downloaded as that very row (.downloaded(bookID:)), so no two moves mark one item, or else a MoveToLibraryError) makes a feed's download a book of the library's own in an order whose every stop keeps the book. It marks each item FeedItemState.inLibrary(bookID:), the listener's from then on, and waits for the record (FeedStore.flush(), before it holds the storage work, since a record's write is storage work itself), moving nothing when the record isn't saved (FEED_MOVE_TO_LIBRARY_PUT_OFF): that write is the move's commit point, ahead of the rename where the addendum had it after, so a crash never lets the launch's move of downloads carry the book back into its feed. Then, holding StorageWork.movingBooksBetweenFolders, it renames each package into the chosen folder (moveIntoTheLibrary(_:folderPath:root:), through relocatePackage(of:as:root:), refusing a row that names no managed package, namesManagedPackage(_:), the row losing its link while its record keeps it), telling readers (bookUpdates.packagesWillMove and packagesDidMove), makes each row a book of the library's own in that folder, the feed's index written without it even when it was the feed's last (FeedShelf.keepIndex(of:)), and saves and waits (saveAndWait(_:); FEED_ITEM_MOVED_TO_LIBRARY); a package that can't move has its item's mark put back (unmarkInLibrary, FEED_MOVE_TO_LIBRARY_FAILED), and a move whose packages moved only in part is a failure worded by FeedWording.partlyMovedToLibrary(moved:noun:folderPath:stayed:), what moved and then what stayed, grouped by reason, so the sheet stays open saying both. A stop before the rename leaves the item In Library with its download in its feed, which markFeedItemsDownloadedAgain(access:) heals at the next launch; a stop after it leaves the package under Library/, which the startup repair adopts under its own book ID (joinFeedItems(journaled:access:skips:)); and the launch's move of downloads passes over an item In Library, reading only FeedItemState.bookID (FeedMoveToLibraryOperationTests). A feed whose record is truly gone, lost outside TextMouth or deleted by an Unsubscribe of a build before branch D, gets its downloads back as books at launch, a stopgap of REV10's item 3 (LibraryViewModel+FeedDownloadReturn.swift: returnDownloadsOfFeedsWithoutRecords(knownFeeds:absence:removingWith:), through returnDownloads(ofFeed:toFolderID:removingWith:), holding the storage work as the move does): each package moves to the library's top level in one rename (moveIntoTheLibrary(_:folderPath:root:)); library.json is written and waited for while the feed's index, which still lists them, is left alone (FeedShelf.leaveIndexAlone(of:)); only then do the packages kept for removed downloads go, through FeedEnvironment.removeItem, and, once no download stayed, the feed's folder with its index (forgetIndex(of:); FEED_DOWNLOADS_RETURNED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_RETURN_FAILED). LibraryViewModel.feedsTakenForGone(knownFeeds:absence:) chooses those feeds, never one the listener unsubscribed from, which keeps its record: each feed whose folder was read while no record of it is loaded, only in the cloud, or in the Feeds folder, a sync service's placeholder of one counting as one (feedsWithoutRecords(knownFeeds:), FeedRecordAbsence.placeholderName(of:)); at once in a data directory outside a cloud location, and in a cloud one only when a note this Mac left at an earlier launch that found the record missing is in the feed's folder (FeedRecordAbsence: .record-absent- followed by this Mac's ID, LibrarySessionLease's, and the launch's; FEED_RECORD_ABSENT as one is left and FEED_RECORD_BACK as the record's return takes this Mac's notes away, both storage events), since a feed subscribed on another Mac can arrive before its record. returnDownloads(ofFeed:toFolderID:removingWith:) does nothing, holding no storage work and saving nothing, for a feed that left nothing (leftSomething(feedID:)). Removing a feed item's file (FR11's branch A2), by Delete Download, the library's Remove of a feed item (FeedCenter.deleteDownload(ofBook:in:)), or Delete Episode, goes through LibraryViewModel.removeFeedFiles(of:keepingPackages:remove:), and by the engine's own rules, the after-playing sweep and the newest-N rule (FR11's branch B), through its two halves, readyFeedFilesForRemoval(of:keepingPackages:) and settleFeedFileRemovals(_:failures:), the files removed between them off the main thread (removeDue, under Feeds, below), all in the order that never loses the listener's place: a package holding the listener's state (FeedItemPackage.holdsListenerState(_:package:): opened, or a place, playback times, bookmarks, a setting of its own, a field edited by hand, or files of the listener's, a transcript, a copy of one, or a copy Edit Text kept, which a package that cannot be listed is taken to hold, holdsListenersFiles(_:)) has its record written at once (writePendingBookInfoNow) and its row moved to the shelf, while a package holding none, or any package when Delete Episode passes keepingPackages false, goes whole; then the indexes are written and waited for (LibraryPersistenceService.saveAndWait(_:), which returns whether every index reached the disk); and only then does the caller's remove closure remove the file and what it made, keeping the record, the content index, content.md, the transcript's files, and Edit Text's copies, whatever the format (FeedItemPackage.staysWithoutTheFile(_:) and removeWhatTheFileMade(of:from:)), though Delete Download and the library's Remove take an article's file and its content.md together, moved by rename into a hidden .removing- folder beside the package and removed as one item through FeedEnvironment.removeItem (FeedItemPackage.removeFileAndText(of:in:remove:)), so the Trash holds one item, a folder holding both, whose own place is gone once the holder is removed (a refusal puts both back in the package, and anything that can't go back stays in the holder, FEED_DOWNLOAD_HELD_ASIDE); or the whole package. A failed index write throws FeedItemPackageError.indexNotWritten before any file is removed: the library is put back as it was and saved again, and only the records written first, which hold the rows' own state, have changed; a file that can't be removed puts its row back; a removed row leaves the reading list; and every package is checked against its feed's own folder before anything in it goes (FeedItemPackage.packageForRemoval(of:in:)), never the Library folder, so a download made before FR11 that has not moved yet is refused (FeedItemPackageError.notAFeedPackage). Remove from Library Only refuses a feed item (staysInItsFeed), since retiring its package would delete its record. Delete Download, the library's Remove, and Delete Episode remove the file, with an article's content.md, which may hold an edit, as one item, or the whole package through FeedEnvironment.removeItem, which follows the Trash setting (FileImportService.removeItemRespectingTrashSetting(at:)), and delete anything else the file made for good (removeWhatTheFileMade(of:from:)); the sweep and the newest-N rule delete for good (FeedItemPackage.removeForGood(_:in:), on the imports' queue), keeping an article's content.md in a kept package. Downloading an item whose row is kept restores the same book (FeedEpisodeImporter.restoreBook, FeedDownloadManager passing the kept row from feedItemRows(of:)): the same ID, package name, date added, place, bookmarks, and settings, with the new file. The publish step puts the new package in the kept one's place (FeedItemPackage.putInPlace(_:of:swap:)): in one step, renamex_np with RENAME_SWAP (swapItems(_:_:)), or, when the swap fails, as on a volume that cannot swap two folders, with one FEED_PACKAGE_SWAP_FAILED line giving the errno, the kept package set aside beside it as .replaced- and its name (setAsideURL(of:)) while the new one is published, removed once it is, and put back should publishing fail; what a crash in between leaves, the item's next download puts right (FEED_PACKAGE_PUT_BACK, FEED_PACKAGE_SET_ASIDE_REMOVED, FEED_PACKAGE_SET_ASIDE_LEFT). An article's text kept by the rules is replaced by the new download's, and kept beside it as an .edited- copy when the two differ (FeedItemPackage.keepEarlierText(_:in:), through BookTextEditSessions.keepCopy(of:bytes:); REV11); a copy that can't be written fails the download, the kept package staying as it was. addFeedBook(_:) then takes the kept row off the shelf as the restored row joins the books (FEED_DOWNLOAD_RESTORED). A transcript of other audio, as when a host inserts other advertisements, is kept, and found stale as the episode opens. FeedReconciler.kept keeps every played item and every item whose row is kept (keepingRecordsOf:, from FeedCenter.keptRecordItemIDs(of:)), whatever number of entries the feed's listing keeps (the List and download choice, under Feeds, below).

the question offers no Remove from Library Only (RemovalChoiceWording.feedDownloads, a FeedDownloadRemovalWording, and offersLibraryOnly), and what the removal did is reported through VoiceOver (LibraryViewModel.removalCompletion(_:mode:kept:), naming only what each kept package holds, FeedDownloadRemovalWording.completionMessage(kept:)), once the question has closed (VoiceOverAnnouncer.announceAfterSheetCloses(_:from:)), or at once when no question was asked. - Index-write suspension: what protects an index that cannot be read.
  - Owner: LibraryPersistenceService. The flag lives on its save queue, beside the waiting snapshot.
  - Who may write it: load() suspends index writes when library.json exists but cannot be read, after LibraryIndexQuarantine has preserved its bytes as library.json.unreadable- followed by the date and time. Only resumeIndexWrites() (Try Again, once the index reads) and replaceUnreadableIndex(with:) (Rebuild Library Index, which first preserves whatever is there) end it. LibraryViewModel+IndexRecovery drives both.
  - How others observe it: LibraryViewModel.indexRecovery is set while recovering, and the Library window shows the recovery banner and its Recovery Options…. beginIndexRecovery gives the provisional library the arrangement recoveredArrangement(session:unreadableIndex:) finds (FR09): each LibraryIndexArrangement part, .readingList and .sortSettings, comes from the session when it holds one, as when the data directory came back with an unreadable index, and otherwise from SalvagedLibraryArrangement.read(from:) over the preserved bytes, or over the unreadable file itself when no copy could be made, each part read on its own and the sort settings entry by entry. LibraryIndexRecovery.arrangementNotKept records what neither gave; the .rebuilt and .savedProvisionalLibrary outcomes carry it as notKept, and LibraryIndexArrangement.notReadSentence(in:) words it for them and for the recovery sheet's Rebuild choice (LibraryIndexRecoveryText.rebuild(savedCopy:notKept:)). A reading list ID that names no book is kept, and dropped at the list's next change, as ever. Snapshots taken meanwhile are dropped, never kept for later, and no feed's index is written either, since each is written with library.json (feed items, above). areIndexWritesSuspended makes data-directory moves refuse. Package records are still written, so reading positions are not lost.
  - Files: Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, Services/LibraryIndexQuarantine.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+IndexRecovery.swift, Views/Library/LibraryIndexRecoveryViews.swift.
- Retirement markers: books removed with Remove from Library Only.
  - Owner: the marker file in the package, removedFromLibrary.json (RemovedFromLibraryMarker), together with LibraryPersistenceService's set of retired packages on its metadata queue.
  - Who may write it: LibraryPersistenceService.retirePackage(of:) writes the marker first. Only once it is on disk does it cancel the writes queued for the book, remember the package, and delete bookInfo.json, a courtesy to older versions of TextMouth. reinstatePackage(of:) deletes the marker when the package is deliberately taken back, by importing it again or adopting it.
  - How others observe it: every package record write checks the set and drops writes into a retired package; orphan adoption skips a package whose marker names its record's book; launch's repair and Rebuild Directory remove retired books from an index saved before their removal, through RemovedFromLibraryMarker.removeRetiredBooks(from:sparing:), which takes each out through Library.removeBook(id:), so it leaves the reading list too (FR09); the repair spares, unread, a book whose marker is only in the cloud, which a read would download.
  - Files: Models/RemovedFromLibraryMarker.swift, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, Services/FileImportService.swift, Services/FileImportService+Packages.swift.
- Duplicate books: what Find Duplicate Books, in Storage settings, groups and removes.
  - Owner: DuplicateFinderView's @State, for as long as the sheet is open: the criteria checked (DuplicateFilter: md5Hash, isbn, title, and author; Identical File (MD5) is checked at each opening, and nothing persists the choice), the groups the last scan found (DuplicateGroup), the page of them shown (pageIndex), the books checked for removal, on every page, and the ISBNs read from EPUB records when Same ISBN is first scanned. The criteria combine: DuplicateFinderEngine.findDuplicates puts a book in a group only when every checked criterion has a value for it, and books share a group only when every value matches, so the former titleAndAuthor case is gone, and a book without an author is left out while author is checked. DuplicateGroup.matchDescription names the criteria with ListFormatter ("Same Title and Same Author"), and its matchKey gives the values shared, a title and author matched together as "Title by Author"; the two make one heading.
  - Who may write it: the sheet's checkboxes and its Scan for Duplicates, which runs the engine over the library's own books, never a feed's items (LibraryViewModel.booksForDuplicateScan, FR11). DuplicateRemoval.firstStep(removing:preferences:) decides from RemovalPreferences whether DuplicateRemovalButton asks first, and DuplicateRemoval.remove(_:from:) deletes the checked books through LibraryViewModel.removeBooksAndDeleteReturningOutcome, to the Trash or not as the preference says.
  - How others observe it: DuplicateFinderWording makes each row's label (title, author, format, folder, and the date added) and the scan-result phrase (scanResult(groupCount:), through CountWording), which the results carry as their accessibility label and VoiceOverAnnouncer announces after a scan the user asks for (runScan()), not after the rescan that follows a removal (runScan(afterRemoval: true)), whose outcome is announced instead, and which keeps the page now holding the group that headed the page shown (DuplicateGroupPages.page(keeping:of:orElse:)). The groups are shown a page at a time (DuplicateGroupPages, REV13): whole groups in the scan's order, listing at most rowsPerPage (40) books, or one larger group alone. While there is more than one page, DuplicateGroupPager shows DuplicateFinderWording.pageLine(groups:of:), "Showing groups 1 to 5 of 116" or "Showing group 12 of 116", with Previous Groups and Next Groups, which announce it as they turn the page; the list is made anew for each page, so it opens at its top.
  - Files: Views/Settings/DuplicateFinderView.swift, Views/Settings/StorageSettingsView.swift, Models/CountWording.swift.

### Package records

- bookInfo.json and contentIndex.json: each package's record of its book (BookInfo), in two files. bookInfo.json holds the metadata, the reading state, and the bookmarks, about a kilobyte, pretty-printed with sorted keys; contentIndex.json, beside it, holds the content index alone, compact with sorted keys, since it can run to hundreds of kilobytes and changes only when the book is imported or analyzed.
  - Owner: LibraryPersistenceService's metadata queue, for every save the running library makes. The record is built from a Book by Book.bookInfo(replacing:contentIndex:revisionFloor:).
  - Who may write it: saveBookInfo(for:) and saveBookInfo(for:contentIndex:), which hold each book's newest snapshot for half a second and write only it; saveContentAnalysis (below); and flushPendingSave(). A save of a book's state writes over the package's record (BookInfo.saveRecord(to:)): a record carrying an index writes contentIndex.json first, so bookInfo.json never names an index that is not on disk yet, and one without leaves contentIndex.json as it is, so a reading checkpoint writes about a kilobyte. The record such a save replaces is read as a StoredBookRecord, bookInfo.json and whether the package keeps an index, never reading contentIndex.json. A package written whole, by an import or a repair that separates a shared package, replaces both files (BookInfo.save(to:)), removing contentIndex.json when the record has no index; an import writes its new package's record so and reads it back (FileImportService.writeVerifiedRecord). Folder moves, migrations, and data-directory moves write records directly with Book.saveBookInfoThrowing, under the same revision rules but outside the queue's order; a move writes each staged copy's record as a save of its book does (saveBookInfoThrowing(contentIndex:inPackageAt:)), so the contentIndex.json it copied stays as it was, byte for byte. A record that is only in the cloud, or whose package or removal marker is, is neither read nor written by the queue, since reading it would download it: the book's saves go to library.json alone until the record is on this Mac again, with one BOOK_RECORD_IN_CLOUD line, and an analysis result for it is dropped.
  - How others observe it: BookInfo.load(from:) reads bookInfo.json with the index from contentIndex.json. An index an older version kept inside bookInfo.json wins, and the next save moves it into its own file, while an older version reading a record written since finds no index and builds one once. A missing or damaged contentIndex.json leaves the record without an index, which the book's next load builds afresh, and a read refused because either file is only in the cloud is passed up rather than downloading it. Content analysis reads the saved index and fingerprints from the record (Book.storedBookInfo()), and saveContentAnalysis reads it back as a Book (Book(record:filePath:folderPath:tmbDirectoryName:)) to merge an analysis into. Orphan adoption, which also builds the provisional library while the index cannot be read, and importing a .tmb package (FileImportService.bookFromPackageInfo) rebuild library rows from records with that initializer too, each without the feed link its record may hold (Book.placedInLibraryFolder(), FR11), since a package in the Library folder is a book of the library's own; a feed's index rebuilds its rows the same way, keeping the link (feed items, above).
  - Files: Models/BookInfo.swift, Models/Book.swift, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift.
- Revisions.
  - Owner: BookState.revision, in each record.
  - Who may write it: every write stores one more than the record it replaces, and never less than one more than the highest revision this process has written for the book, so a book's revisions only rise, across restarts too.
  - How others observe it: no decision rests on a revision. Whether an analysis still applies is decided by the book's content (below).
  - Files: Models/BookInfo.swift, Models/Book.swift, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift.
- Analysis-owned fields.
  - Owner: the content analysis. Book.ContentAnalysisFields (the word and character counts, the audio duration, the content index version, the cache format version, and the text cache fingerprint) travel together with the content index.
  - Who may write it: LibraryPersistenceService.saveContentAnalysis merges a finished analysis into the book's latest durable state, on the metadata queue: the snapshot still waiting there, or else the record on disk. Only the analysis fields and the index change. A result is dropped, with one CONTENT_ANALYSIS_DROPPED log line, when that state describes other content: another MD5 hash, format, or file path, or another book's record. A save that carries no index keeps the stored index and its fields, so neither kind of write undoes the other. The reader commits a fresh index of its own through LibraryViewModel.updateContentIndex.
  - How others observe it: a library row takes the committed fields and keeps its own reading state, settings, and metadata. The text cache batch and the statistics pass hand each finished book's fields to LibraryViewModel.analysisSummaries (ContentAnalysisSummaryBuffer), which gives the rows all that arrived in one change of library and one save, at most half a second after the first of them (flushInterval), when a pass ends, and when TextMouth quits; a summary a crash loses is recovered from the book's record by the next launch's batch. The batch's progress is an object of its own, LibraryViewModel.textExtraction (TextExtractionProgress), which only the Library windows' overlay and Storage settings observe (ProgressObserver, the generic observer the import overlay uses too), so a finished book redraws only them.
  - Files: Models/Book.swift, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+BookOperations.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift, ViewModels/ContentAnalysisSummaryBuffer.swift, ViewModels/TextExtractionProgress.swift.
- The metadata reader's version: which reader last read a book's file for its title and author.
  - Owner: ImportedFileMetadata.readerVersion ("1.0.0"), the version of the reader every import uses (ImportedFileMetadata.read, which opens EPUB, Word, PDF, DAISY, and audio files and never a plain text, Markdown, or HTML file: readsFile(of:)), stamped on the row as Book.metadataReaderVersion and, with the book's next save, in its record (BookMetadata.metadataReaderVersion), so a library rebuilt from its records reads again only the books saved before that. SemanticVersion.isOutdated(stored:current:) judges it as it judges the text cache's format version, so a stamp a newer TextMouth left in a shared library is never undone. An EPUB's author is EPUBParser.authorName(in:): the dc:creator with the author role (EPUB 2 opf:role, EPUB 3 a meta refining it), else the first dc:creator naming anyone, else a dcterms:creator meta element, else a dc:contributor with the author role, the name trimmed, and an element holding only the placeholder Book.unknownAuthor naming no one; parseOPF, readMetadata, and the editor's write (updateOPFMetadata, which writes into that creator or adds one) share the choice.
  - Who may write it: an import stamps the row it makes (importFileWithReport). The launch's metadata refresh (LibraryViewModel.refreshOutdatedMetadata, in LibraryViewModel+MetadataRefresh.swift) reads the file of every row an older reader stamped, or none, but a feed item's (FR11), whose title and author come from its feed, off the main thread with the same reader, each read as storage work a data-directory move waits for (StorageWork.metadataRefresh), and stamps every row read. Each file is read with an empty fallback title (ImportedFileMetadata.read(from:format:fallbackTitle:)), so a file that names no title names none, into a MetadataReading of the values it names for the fields the refresh may replace (Book.MetadataField.refreshable: the title, the author, and an EPUB's publisher and publication date), an author only when it names someone. refreshedValues(of:from:), asked on the main actor of each row as it is then, so a field edited by hand while the pass ran stays, decides what the row takes: never a field its record of hand edits names (hand edits, below); every other refreshable field whose value differs, for a row whose record has been kept since its import (Book.HandEdits.sinceImport); and for any other row, whose unrecorded fields may hold edits nothing recorded, only an author still at the placeholder, and only with a name. Since October 7, 2026, FileImportService.importFileWithReport begins such a record on every book it makes (hand edits, below); every other row can take nothing but an author in the placeholder's place. The refresh records no edit of its own, and a row with a field replaced has its record written as any edit of it would. A book whose files are in the cloud is left unstamped and counted in the pass's CloudSkips (MaintenancePass.metadataRefresh; maintenance in a cloud library, below); a book whose format keeps no metadata in its file, or whose content is a folder of tracks, is stamped without a look.
  - How others observe it: updateContentCachesAndStatistics runs the refresh first, then the text cache batch, then the statistics; the rows take what was read in one change of the library and one save (applyMetadataReadings), and one METADATA_REFRESH line in the debug log counts the books read, the fields replaced, and the rows they were replaced on (metadataRefreshLogLine(read:replaced:rows:)). A reader follows a new author as it follows any edit (ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates).

- Hand edits (FR06): which of a book's metadata fields the user edited by hand.
  - Owner: Book.handEdits, a Book.HandEdits (Models/Book.swift, under Hand Edits), on the row and, as BookMetadata.handEdits, in bookInfo.json: the Book.MetadataFields edited (title, author, publisher, publicationDate, audioAlbum, audioGenre, and audioYear), kept in MetadataField.allCases order, each once, so a record is always written as the same bytes, and sinceImport, whether the record has been kept since the book's import, so that it names every hand edit. nil means nothing was recorded: every row saved before October 7, 2026, and every book since that the file import did not make, until an editor's save begins its record. library.json decodes it leniently (LenientFields.optionalValue), a record that can't be read counting as none; a field name a later TextMouth writes is dropped, and a record without sinceImport reads as begun by an edit. makeBookInfo() and Book(record:filePath:folderPath:tmbDirectoryName:) carry it both ways, and Book.EditableMetadata carries it, so a reader's copy takes the row's through libraryFields.
  - Who may write it: LibraryViewModel.updateBookMetadata(_:of:contentHash:), the one save every metadata editor goes through: the Book Info sheet of a plain text, Markdown, or HTML book (LibraryBookInfoSheet); the EPUB editor, the library's and the reader's (ReaderViewModel.saveEPUBMetadata(_:openedAs:writeToFile:writer:)), through LibraryViewModel.saveEPUBMetadata(_:openedAs:for:writingToFile:writer:); and the audio editor, the library's directly and the reader's through saveAudioMetadata(_:openedAs:). It takes a Book.MetadataEdit: the values of the fields the editor shows, as it opened with them and as they were saved. Its changes (MetadataEdit.changes) are the fields saved with a value other than the one the editor opened with, a field it opened without counting as opened empty. An editor shows a value nothing knew as empty (Book.storedEPUBMetadata and storedAudioMetadata, which Book Info shows for a book only in the cloud), so such a field saved empty is no change and stays nil, and the book's file gives its value once the book is downloaded. Book.applyMetadataEdit(_:) applies the changes alone to the row as it is at the save, and marks exactly those through Book.markEditedByHand(_:), which begins a record with sinceImport false on a row without one; nothing removes a field. Every other field keeps the row's value, so a change another window or the launch metadata refresh made while the editor was open is neither put back nor marked, and the row's record keeps every field it gained meanwhile. A save with no change and no new content hash writes nothing. Every editor keeps what it opened with in @State, since whoever shows it may make it again with newer values, as the reader's Book Info does when the library changes the book: MetadataEditorView hands openedEPUBMetadata or openedAudioMetadata to its save beside the edits, and LibraryBookInfoSheet keeps its title and author trimmed as Save trims them (values(title:author:)), from which edit(from:title:author:) makes its edit, or nil for a blank title. LibraryViewModel.saveEPUBMetadata, asked to write the library's copy of the EPUB file, writes it with the row as the edit leaves it laid over the editor's metadata (mergingEditableEPUBMetadata(_:)), and passes the written file's hash as contentHash. The save returns the row as saved, whose record the reader's copy takes; the reader's saves then show the saved row over the edit, and its audio save of a book no longer in the library changes nothing and says so (EPUBMetadataSaveWording.notInLibrary(title:)). HandEdits.noneSinceImport is the record FileImportService.importFileWithReport begins on every book it makes (each single-file format, a DAISY book's folder, package file, or navigation file, a text-only DAISY archive, and each book of a ZIP batch), beside the metadata reader version it stamps, since that reader read its title and author in the very file the launch refresh reads again. New from Text (importText), recordings (importRecording), a feed's downloads (FeedEpisodeImporter), and archives of audio tracks or of a DAISY audio book (stagedAudioBook) begin none, and a package keeps what its record carries (bookFromPackageInfo, orphan adoption; reconstructedBook has none). An episode's or article's Book Info while it is in its feed (Book.hasReadOnlyBookInfo, FR11's branch C) has no editor and saves nothing: LibraryView.bookInfoSheet(for:) and the reader's ReaderBookInfoSheet show FeedItemBookInfoSheet (Views/Library/FeedItemBookInfoSheet.swift), whose details (FeedItemBookInfoWording.details(of:info:feedTitle:), each a BookInfoDetail) FeedInfoValue draws as one element whose value can be selected, with a spoken value where it differs, as a duration does; LibraryViewModel.feedTitle(of:) names the feed, for Book Info and Speak Info, and the reader's Book Info button is labelled by readerButtonLabel(readOnly:).
  - How others observe it: the launch metadata refresh never replaces a recorded field (the metadata reader's version, above). MetadataEditorWording.fieldLabel(_:for:in:) (Views/Reader/MetadataEditorView.swift) adds MetadataEditorWording.editedByHand, "edited by hand", to the accessibility label of a recorded field's control, as in "Book title, edited by hand", in MetadataEditorView, which takes handEdits:, and in LibraryBookInfoSheet, which reads its book's. The Library window passes handEdits: to both of its editors, and so does the reader's Book Info, ReaderBookInfoSheet (Views/Reader/ReaderView.swift), which observes the reader and passes its copy's record, which every save of the row, the reader's own included, brings up to date; for an episode or article in its feed (Book.hasReadOnlyBookInfo) it shows the read-only FeedItemBookInfoSheet, and for any other plain text, Markdown, or HTML book LibraryBookInfoSheet, which reads the reader's copy.

- Saved places' text anchors (T06's branch A): what a reading position, a bookmark, and a skipped heading keep of the text around them, so a place can be found again by its words when the book's text is read anew.
  - Owner: the library row and the package record. ReadingPosition.anchor and Bookmark.anchor are each a TextAnchor (Models/TextAnchor.swift): up to quoteLength, 64, UTF-16 units of the chapter's reading text before and after the place, cut on composed-character boundaries, a place inside a composed character counting as at its start; the place's share of the book, counted as Book.restsAtEnd counts a place, so an anchor captured at or past the book's total marks its end; the text cache's and content index's versions it was measured in (currentMeasure, as "3.0.7/1.0.0"), which nothing decides by; and its format version. Book.skippedHeadings, and BookState.skippedHeadings in the record, hold a SkippedHeading for each skipped heading: its level, label, chapter, offset, and the anchor at its start. Every field is read leniently, an anchor that can't be read counting as none, and written only when set, so a book with neither writes exactly what it wrote before.
  - Who may write it: ReaderViewModel.makeCurrentReadingPosition() gives every position its anchor (anchorToSave(at:)), keeping the opened place's saved anchor (openedPlaceAnchor) until the cursor moves; both bookmark adds pass one to LibraryViewModel.addBookmark and Library.addBookmark; toggleHeadingSkip saves SkippedHeadings with anchors through updateSkippedHeadings(bookID:headings:) and Book.setSkippedHeadings(_:), which writes skippedHeadingIDs from them. Book.skippedHeadings is kept in step with skippedHeadingIDs, which older builds read: made from the IDs where a row or record has none, and following every change to them, keeping each still-named heading's anchor (SkippedHeading.inStep(with:keeping:)). Places saved before anchors take theirs at load (captureMissingAnchorsAtLoad()) and in the launch pass captureMissingTextAnchors(access:then:), run by updateContentCachesAndStatistics after the metadata refresh and before the cache batch (TEXT_ANCHORS_BACKFILLED); both use CapturedPlaceAnchors.capturing and Book.adoptCapturedAnchors(_:), which gives words only to places still saved where they were read. The pass reads each book's content.md off the main thread, writes nothing else, skips books only in the cloud, held by an Edit Text session, or open in a reader (they wait for a later launch), and changes no place's date and no lastOpened. A place still saved past its chapter's end is quoted at that end; a place in a chapter the book lacks, and a skipped heading whose ID names no current heading, get no words, since quoting where a skip's old place falls now could make it skip the wrong section. measuredIn is TextAnchor.measure(ofTextCachedBy:) of the content.md's recorded cache version, nil when the book records none. DAISY text books (.daisy) take words like any text book; DAISY books with audio are audiobooks and keep places in time.
  - How others observe it: TextAnchor.stands(at:in:) is the direct check, the anchor's text standing exactly around the saved numbers, compared unit for unit. TextAnchorFinder (Models/TextAnchorFinder.swift), a pure Sendable value built once per book's text, off the main thread and only when a direct check fails, finds a place again by its words, read as fine words (FineWords and BookWords, Models/FineWords.swift: runs of letters or digits, ending where letters meet digits and where a small letter meets a capital, compared in lower case, each keyed by a 64-bit FNV-1a hash, the one word rule every way of finding a place shares). The end of the book stays the end; then the direct check; then every spot where the first four words after the place, or the last four before it, stand, scored 2 for each word after that agrees, in order, and 1 for each word before, a candidate needing 8; equal best spots are settled by nearness to where the place was, and a best spot far from it (more than 5% of the book, and more than 3,000 units) yields to a near one scoring 8 or more, both silent best guesses; then a tolerant second search, lining the quote's words up with the book's around every spot where three of them stand together; and last the same share of the place's chapter, at a word start, when the chapter's old length is known, or else its saved offset held within the chapter (for a chapter the book no longer has, its share of the whole book). findHeading finds a skipped heading by its level, label, and words, or returns nil so the skip waits. Each result says how the place was found (TextAnchorFinder.Finding: unchanged, found, or guessed, with its reason).
  - Moving them across a rebuilt text (T06, branch C): PlaceRelocator (pure, Sendable) moves a book's reading position, bookmarks, and skipped headings from its old text (the old content.md as this build reads it) to its new one. It lines the two texts' fine words up once by a patience diff (PatienceDiff). For each place it: keeps it when its anchor still stands or marks the end of the book (TextAnchorFinder.settled); finds it in the old text, at its numbers or else by its words; carries it by the diff's rules (solid matches, a place keeping the words that follow it, like-for-like stretches, removals slid back, moved text followed into text the diff found put in, the end to the end); else asks the saved-words search, then guesses silently. A place goes to the new text's end only when it was at the old text's end (TextPlace.restsAtEnd, which Book.restsAtEnd also uses); a place at its chapter's end stays at that chapter's end; a place in a chapter without words goes to the matching chapter without words between its neighbours; a reading position at the very start of the book stays at the start; a place saved without words in a lost chapter is held as the reader opens it (TextPlace.opening, which ReaderViewModel.openingPlace also uses).
