A skipped heading is found first in the old text: by the heading its ID names, else by its level and label when one old heading has them (of several, the one its saved words find for certain), else at its place where its saved words still stand exactly; never by saved words alone or at stale numbers. That heading's start is carried like a place, and a new heading there takes the skip only on the diff's proof: no rival old heading that lent it words, or whose section continues in it, has a label more like it (or as like, with its own section continuing there instead), and its label is the skip's, or the skipped section continues in it, or most of the skipped heading's words went into it. Among several, the label decides, then the level, so a re-levelled heading keeps its skip. Failing that, the one new heading of its level with its label takes the skip unless the diff makes it another section; otherwise the skip waits, unchanged. The relocator takes both texts' headings together, or neither (the initializers for places alone let every skip wait). relocate(_:movedAt:) takes the time of the move, which a moved position takes as its date, and returns the places to save (anchors captured anew), how each was found, and the counts for the PLACES_RELOCATED line (Counts.logLine(title:)); the findings and counts cover only the skips kept. It shares BookWords.landing and TextAnchorFinder.settled and onlyBestSpot with the finder.
  - Moving them when a rebuild writes the text (T06, branch D): MarkdownCacheService.writeCache(for:) copies the old content.md and the book's places aside before it writes (PlaceSnapshot.keep, in MachineLocalPaths.placeSnapshots), and after writing moves the places with PlaceSnapshot.movePlaces(to:at:), through PlaceRelocator.relocate(_:from:to:movedAt:), which builds a relocator only when some place does not stand. Nothing is copied for a book with no places, one in an Edit Text session, one whose content.md is only in the cloud, or one whose new text is unchanged. The moved places travel in GeneratedCache.movedPlaces and BookContentAnalysisService.Result.movedPlaces. The batch, Redo Text Extraction, the statistics pass, and the reader commit them with LibraryPersistenceService.saveContentAnalysis(…movedPlaces:), guarded by Book.takeMovedPlaces(original:moved:), which compares each place as stored (the record and the row keep dates to the second, so a place saved during the session would otherwise never equal its stored copy and silently not move) and guards each place on its own, except the skip lists, which move as one because the relocator merges skips; that save deletes the copy. Library rows take them through ContentAnalysisSummary, and the reader in showLoadedContent by takeMovedPlaces(_:). The launch's recovery, finishLeftoverPlaceMoves, runs first in updateContentCachesAndStatistics and commits through saveMovedPlaces; PlaceSnapshot lists the copies this run is still using, and the recovery skips them, so it never sweeps or takes one a reader's rebuild is still using (the test seam forgetCopiesInUse() empties the list, as a quit does). A skipped heading the new text no longer has waits: Book.waitingSkippedHeadings and BookState.waitingSkippedHeadings hold it, a list of its own that no reader of skippedHeadingIDs sees, so it skips nothing (older builds drop the list, so for them the section plays), and it names a heading again only where that heading stands exactly as it was skipped, with saved words. Waiting skips are not a book's own settings (Book.hasOwnSettings). The debug log's lines are PLACES_NOT_KEPT, PLACES_NOT_MOVED, PLACES_RECOVERED, and PLACES_RELOCATED.
  - Files: Models/TextAnchor.swift, Models/TextAnchorCapture.swift, Models/FineWords.swift, Models/TextAnchorFinder.swift, Models/PatienceDiff.swift, Models/PlaceRelocator.swift, Models/Book.swift, Models/BookInfo.swift, Services/PlaceSnapshots.swift, Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, ViewModels/ContentAnalysisSummaryBuffer.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+SavedPlaces.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+TextAnchors.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+PlaceSnapshots.swift.
- Transcripts (FR05): a transcribed audiobook's transcript.json, and the text written from it.
  - Owner: the package. transcript.json (Transcript, in layout Transcript.currentVersion, 1) is a record, not a cache: only transcribing the book again replaces it, only Edit Text and a cleanup or its undo change it, and only Delete Transcript removes it. It is compact JSON with sorted keys and ISO 8601 dates, written atomically (TranscriptStore): its layout version; the locale; the recognizer and its model (model: the engine, SpeechTranscriber, the asset status the system reported, and the macOS version); the build of TextMouth (app: its version, build number, and commit); when the transcription finished (createdAt, in whole seconds); when the user last edited it (editedAt, in whole seconds, left out until then, so an earlier TextMouth reads an edited transcript as any other); its last cleanup by rule (cleanedUp, a TranscriptCleanupNote: when, in whole seconds, the rules' version and date, whose rules they were, "built-in" or "user", kept as words so that a source a later TextMouth adds never keeps this one from reading the transcript, and how many corrections it made; left out until a cleanup); and files, one for each audio file in the order the book plays them, each with its path as the book's audio index names it, its fingerprint (FileFingerprint: size, modification time in nanoseconds, and MD5 digest) taken just before it was transcribed, its duration in milliseconds, and its sentences. A sentence holds its text, its start and end, and its words, each an array of [UTF-16 offset in the sentence, UTF-16 length, start, end, confidence], the confidence to three decimals and left out when the recognizer gives none. Times are whole milliseconds from the start of their own file, so the recognizer's 60-millisecond steps stay exact. The text derived from it is a cache: content.md, which TranscriptTextBuilder writes (each chapter's marker and heading, then paragraphs of at most four sentences or about 600 UTF-16 units, a pause of two seconds or more starting a new one), and the transcript layer of the book's content index (BookContentIndex.transcript, a BookContentTranscriptIndex, version BookContentTranscriptIndex.currentVersion, "1.0.0"), which records the text's own index (as a text book's: chapters, headings, and counts), each chapter's sentences as [UTF-16 offset, length, start, end], in milliseconds of chapter time, the audio files' fingerprints, and a TextCacheFingerprint with transcript.json as the source and content.md as the cache. The audio index itself is unchanged, so BookContentIndex.currentVersion, and every position and bookmark, which count time, stay as they were. bookInfo.json notes the transcript (BookState.transcript, a TranscriptNote: the layout version, the locale, and the date); library.json does not. Beside it, transcript.json.before-cleanup (TextMouthPaths.transcriptBeforeCleanupPath(in:)) holds the bytes of the transcript as it was before its last cleanup, for Undo Cleanup.
  - Who may write it: TranscriptStore.install, which TranscriptionJobCenter runs off the main thread once the audio is transcribed (TranscriptionService.transcribeAndSave does both for the fixture tests), as storage work (StorageBoundary.perform(.textExtraction)): it checks that the book's audio is still the audio transcribed, writes transcript.json, writes content.md from it (TranscriptTextLoader.writeText), saves the index through LibraryPersistenceService.saveContentAnalysis, and drops the book's kept reading text. TranscriptTextLoader writes content.md and the layer again from transcript.json, as storage work, when either is missing, was changed, or was made by another version (TRANSCRIPT_TEXT_REBUILT). Before it replaces content.md, writeText(replacing:) keeps a content.md holding text TextMouth didn't write or take beside itself (BookTextEditSessions.keepCopy(of:bytes:), TEXT_EDIT_KEPT). TranscriptStore.takeEdit writes an edit of the text into transcript.json (edited text, below). TranscriptStore.cleanUp(_:expecting:with:from:now:hasher:) and undoCleanup(of:hasher:) (Services/TranscriptCleanupStore.swift) clean a transcript up by rule, and undo that, as storage work, for the Clean Up sheet (a transcript's cleanup, below). A cleanup reads transcript.json with dataless files left in the cloud, and goes on only while it still holds the transcript the sheet previewed (TranscriptCleanupError.transcriptChanged) and the rules change something; it writes the bytes it read to transcript.json.before-cleanup, replacing an earlier copy, rewrites each sentence a rule changed (TranscriptCleanup, through TranscriptSentence.rewrite(as:), which TranscriptEditing.apply shares), records cleanedUp, and installs the result as a transcription installs one (install). A cleanup that fails while transcript.json still holds what it read puts the earlier copy back, or removes the new one. undoCleanup installs the copy again, when the transcript records a cleanup and the copy can be used, then removes it. TranscriptStore.delete(for:), as storage work, removes transcript.json first, so a deletion cut short leaves no transcript to show, then the copy kept before a cleanup, content.md, and the kept reading text, and saves the audio index without its layer, which drops the record's note; the copies kept beside the files stay. The record's note follows the index each write carries, an import's included (Book.bookInfo(replacing:contentIndex:revisionFloor:), FileImportService.writeVerifiedRecord), and every other save keeps it.
  - How others observe it: ReaderViewModel.readContent loads the text (TranscriptTextLoader.load) into DocumentContent.transcript (TranscriptText), trusting it only when the layer validates against the audio index, transcript.json and content.md match their fingerprints, and the parsed content.md lines up with the layer's index; the text area shows the chapter's transcript around the sentence heard at the cursor's time (ReaderViewModel.shownText), and Where Am I? quotes that sentence, at most its first 40 words (ReaderViewModel.heardTranscriptSentence, whereAmIQuoteWordLimit), found through the same conversion of the audio cursor (TranscriptText.sentence(atAudioOffset:inChapter:), which textOffset(atAudioOffset:inChapter:) goes through too). An audio file whose size or contents changed since transcription makes the transcript stale (TRANSCRIPT_STALE), and it shows no text; one only touched keeps it, its new stamp recorded. Before it calls a transcript stale, the load reads transcript.json again, in case another Mac has transcribed the changed audio meanwhile. transcript.json and content.md are never read while either is only in the cloud, and an audio file only in the cloud is taken on trust. A transcript.json that can't be used is preserved beside itself (SavedFile.preserveUnusable, TRANSCRIPT_FILE), and one of a later layout is left as it is, unread (TranscriptStore.Lookup.unsupportedVersion). TextMouthPaths.isTranscriptFile keeps transcript.json, the copy kept before a cleanup, and the preserved copies out of the candidates for the book's own file (FileImportService.isPackageContentName). A transcript saved, edited, cleaned up, or deleted, or a cleanup undone, reaches the book's readers through LibraryBookUpdates.transcriptReplaced, which ReaderViewModel.showTranscript(_:) shows as the book plays on, or once it has loaded, and the library rows through TranscribedBooks (transcription, below).
  - Files: Models/Transcript.swift, Models/TranscriptTextIndex.swift, Services/TranscriptStore.swift, Services/TranscriptInstallation.swift, Services/TranscriptDeletion.swift, Services/TranscriptEdit.swift, Services/TranscriptCleanupStore.swift, Services/TranscriptCleanup.swift, Models/TranscriptCleanupRules.swift, Services/TranscriptTextBuilder.swift, Services/TranscriptTextLoader.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, Services/FileImportService+Packages.swift, Models/BookInfo.swift, Models/Book.swift, Models/BookContentIndex.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Transcript.swift.
- A recording's origin record (FR04): origin.json, only in the package of a book recorded in TextMouth, and the original take it may name, original-take.caf.
  - Owner: the package, apart from its record. origin.json (BookOrigin) holds the kind (recording), when recording started, the take's duration, the microphone's name, and the original take kept, if any, until the book's audio is first edited, when edits.json records it instead (audio edits, below): its file name, when the book's audio was last made from it (lastEditedAt), and a policy of the book's own (policyOverride). It is pretty-printed with sorted keys and ISO 8601 dates. Earlier versions of TextMouth rewrite bookInfo.json and library.json without the fields they don't know, but leave a file they don't know alone, so the record survives them; no save of a book's state touches it.
  - Who may write it: FileImportService.importRecording writes both files into the staged package once, as a recording is saved; afterwards only AudioEditHistoryStore, under its lock: it records a policy of the book's own beside the take, strikes the take once edits.json records it, and clears originalTake once AudioOriginalSweep has deleted the take (audio edits, below).
  - How others observe it: BookOrigin.load(fromPackage:) never downloads, throwing only the refusal for a record only in the cloud, and a record that can't be read counts as none. FileImportService.isPackageContentName keeps both files out of the candidates for the book's own file.
  - Files: Models/BookOrigin.swift, Services/FileImportService+Recording.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditHistoryStore.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioOriginalSweep.swift, Services/FileImportService+Packages.swift.

### Audio edits

- The edit history (FR04): an edited audiobook's audioEdits/edits.json, and a recording's original take until its first edit.
  - Owner: the package. edits.json (AudioEditHistory, in layout AudioEditHistory.currentVersion, 1) is a record, pretty-printed with sorted keys and ISO 8601 dates: the book's own original policy (policyOverride; none follows the global setting), and one AudioEditList for each audio file edited, named by its path inside the package. A list is the file's version control: its edits in order (AudioEditEntry: an identity, when it was made, and an AudioEdit, whose times are seconds on the audio as the edits before it left it), a cursor (how many are applied; the rest were undone and can be redone until a new edit discards them), renderedCursor (how many the book's file holds as last rendered), when it was last rendered, its original (AudioOriginal: its file, and when the audio was last edited from it), or when the policy deleted it, and the editor's markers (AudioEditMarker: an identity, a time on the audio as the applied edits leave it, and a name), written only when there are any, so a list without them is written as before; an earlier TextMouth reads such a list and drops the markers when it next writes it. While an original is kept the edits apply to it, so every edit can be undone; without one they apply to the file as last rendered, and undo stops there (undoLimit). The folder also holds originals, the originals of imported books' files, each at its file's own path, and takes, the takes of punch-ins and of audio inserted (AudioEdit.insert(at:with:), stored as "insert": a paste, or a recording made with nothing selected), as Apple Lossless when that loses nothing (AudioEditPackage).
  - Who may write it: only AudioEditHistoryStore, whose update reads, changes, and writes a history under one lock for the whole process, so an editing session, a render, and the original policy's sweep never write over one another, and then deletes the takes no edit can use any more. A recording's take stays recorded in its origin record, origin.json, as the Record window saved it, until the history holds more than it: the write that adds an edit, a marker, or a policy of the book's own, writes edits.json and strikes the take from origin.json, so exactly one record describes it. A history that can't be used is preserved beside itself (SavedFile.preserveUnusable, AUDIO_EDITS_FILE), and one of a later layout is left alone; neither is written over, and an editing session starts over only when asked to (startingOver).
  - How others observe it: AudioEditHistoryStore.read never downloads: a history, or a recording's origin record, only in the cloud is reported as such (Lookup.inCloud, AudioEditError.historyInCloud): an edit or a save is refused until it is on this Mac, and a sweep leaves the book for later. A path a history records is used only while it stays inside the package (AudioEditPackage.url(of:in:)), however edits.json was edited. FileImportService.isPackageContentName passes over the folder by its one name.
  - Files: Models/AudioEdit.swift, Models/AudioEditList.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditHistoryStore.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditPackage.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditError.swift, Models/BookOrigin.swift, Services/FileImportService+Packages.swift.
- From the list to sound: previewing, saving, and exporting edited audio.
  - Owner: values made from the list, off the main thread. AudioEditTimeline is the edited audio laid out in whole sample frames at the rate of the audio the edits start from, so every edit lands on the same sample however often it is applied; it applies the edits that only rearrange sound itself, and its chapter markers follow the sound. A filter's or a gain change's stretch goes through an AudioEditProcessing: AudioFilterProcessor renders the stretch to float PCM, runs its chain on the engine chosen when the session or the save began (AudioFilterEngine: TextMouth's own filters through AudioFilterRunner, or the installed ffmpeg as a child process), and keeps the result in this Mac's Audio Edits folder (AudioEditScratch) under a digest of everything that decides it, so undo, redo, and a later render read it back; whatever writes there makes the book's folder again first (AudioEditScratch.make), since a save removes it while an editor edits on, and macOS may empty the Caches folder. A stretch of the edited audio whose file macOS removed is made again when next needed (AudioEditSession.restorePurgedStretches, before an edit, a preview, a stretch copied or cut, and an export): callers at once share one rebuild, one made for a list that changed meanwhile is not taken, and of two renders of one stretch at once, the result the first moved into place stands (AudioFilterProcessor). AudioEditComposition makes a timeline an AVMutableComposition, the preview, sample-exact; AudioEditRenderer writes it with AVAssetReader and AVAssetWriter in one pass, writing decoded audio no further than the edits' length, so a compressed take that ends them adds none of its last packet's padding, and MP3 with the LAME built in: the composition is decoded to floats and handed to MP3Encoder buffer by buffer (AudioEditRenderer.renderMP3, along an MP3Route), or, should LAME fail to start, rendered to float PCM and encoded by an installed ffmpeg (FFmpeg.encodeMP3). The AVAssetWriter path and LAME's share the reader and the loop that cuts decoded audio at the edits' end (makeReader, drain).
  - Who may write it: AudioEditSession, on the main actor, the edit session the editor window's model works through (the editor window, below), which changes the list only through the history store, off the main thread, checking each edit against the audio first (AudioEditError.invalidEdit, a sentence saying why), and holds a use of its book while it is open; and LibraryViewModel.saveAudioEdits, which refuses a book open in a reader and runs AudioEditBookRender as storage work (StorageWork.audioEditing) under a use of the book. Under the history's lock, an imported file never rendered into has its original kept first, a clone, unless the policy keeps none; the edits are rendered into a hidden file beside the book's file, in its own format where TextMouth can write it (AudioRenderFormat.inPlace), read back and checked for length, and put in the file's place in one step, or under a new name, the old file then deleted only once the book's record names the new one (LibraryPersistenceService.writePendingBookInfoNow); under the lock again, the list notes what the file holds. The book's audio is then analyzed afresh (BookContentAnalysisService.analyzeAudio), and its row, record, and index saved. Its bookmarks and saved reading position follow the sound (AudioEditPlaces.follow): the session keeps the route from the file as last saved to the audio edited now (AudioEditSession.savedFileRoute, an AudioEditorTimeMap.Route: each edit, undo, and redo a crossing with the audio's lengths around it, an undo of the newest crossing taking it back), works it out from the list as it opens, and knows none when the file holds edits the list no longer has; saveAudioEdits reads the chapters as the reader lays them over the file before the render (BookContentAnalysisService.load), and moves each place through that layout, along the route, and through the new one, as a time on the whole file in a book of one file, or on its track in a book of several, where only the saved track's places move. A moved position is dated at the save, so it wins over older copies of itself; without a route, nothing moves, and an AUDIO_EDITS_SAVED line says why. Everything here works without a window.
  - How others observe it: the book's row and record take its new file, fingerprint, length, chapters, and index, with an AUDIO_EDITS_SAVED line; the reading text kept for it is dropped, and a transcript made of the old audio no longer matches its fingerprints, so it is stale. AudioEditSession.export writes the edits as they stand, saved or not, to a new file in an AudioExportFormat, MP3 among them without ffmpeg, leaving the book's file alone.
  - Files: Models/AudioEditTimeline.swift, Models/AudioFilterChain.swift, Models/AudioBuiltInFilter.swift, Models/AudioEditWording.swift, Models/AudioEditPlaces.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditComposition.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditRenderer.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditRenderer+MP3.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditBookRender.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioFilterProcessor.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioFilterRunner.swift, Services/AudioEditing/FFmpeg.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioExportFormat.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditSession.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorTimeMap.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+AudioEditing.swift.
- An MP3 file TextMouth writes (FR04): an MP3 book's save, or an export.
  - Owner: MP3Encoder, one for each file: LAME 3.100's encoder at a constant bit rate (MP3EncoderSettings: an MP3 book's own, or 64 kbit/s a channel for speech, LAME taking the nearest the sample rate allows), quality 2, mono or joint stereo. The file starts with MP3Tag's ID3v2.3 tag: the title (TIT2) and author (TPE1) in UTF-16; one top-level table of contents (CTOC) listing a chapter frame (CHAP) for each chapter marker, at most 255 (maximumChapters), since AVFoundation reads only a top-level table and a table counts its entries in one byte, the 255th then running to the end; and the iTunes gapless comment, iTunSMPB (MP3GaplessInfo), which AVFoundation honours, written at a fixed width first and again in place once finish() knows the numbers. LAME's own Info tag goes in the frame LAME leaves for it, when that frame has room. An encoder that doesn't finish removes its file.
  - Who may write it: AudioEditRenderer.renderMP3, for an MP3 book's save and for an export.
  - How others observe it: as any MP3, through AudioEditBase, AudioBookParser, and AudioMediaProbe.
  - The library: LAME 3.100 in Vendor/lame, built by project.yml as the static library target LAME (libmp3lame.a), optimized for size, its warnings hidden, DEBUG undefined, and linked without -ObjC (requiresObjCLinking: false). Swift imports it as the LAME module (Vendor/lame/include/module.modulemap), in MP3Encoder alone. README.md's Third-party code is its notice.
  - Files: Services/MP3/MP3Encoder.swift, Services/MP3/MP3Tag.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditRenderer+MP3.swift, Vendor/lame, project.yml.
- The originals, and the original policy.
  - Owner: AudioOriginalPolicy: keep, delete (edits then change the book's audio in place), or delete a number of days after the last edit, held globally in the preferences store under the names it had when only recordings kept an original, recordingOriginalPolicy and recordingOriginalDays (SettingsPolicy's rules: delete after 1 day until changed, from 1 to 365 days), and mirrored in config.json. A book's own policy, in its edit history or, for a recording never edited, beside its take in origin.json, wins. An original is a recording's take, original-take.caf, which a recording saved under delete never keeps, or the copy of an imported file kept as its edits are first rendered.
  - Who may write it: Storage settings' Audio Editing group (AudioEditingSettingsSection, whose AudioEditingSettingsModel follows the store through PreferenceObserver) and config.json, for the global policy; LibraryViewModel.setAudioOriginalPolicy(_:forBook:), for a book's own, which the editor's Audio Info calls (AudioEditSession.setPolicyOverride(_:)). Only AudioOriginalSweep deletes an original: at launch (startLibrary), after each recording or edit is saved, and after a book's own policy changes (sweepAudioOriginalsInBackground), off the main thread as storage work, and only when the policy says its time is up, the book's file holds exactly the applied edits (AudioEditList.canForgetOriginal), and AudioEditActivity says the book is neither open in a reader (mirrored from LibraryViewModel.openBookIDs) nor being edited or rendered. It asks under the history's lock, which a render takes only once its use has begun, so a render never loses the original it reads.
  - How others observe it: the list notes when its original was deleted, and undo stops at the audio as last rendered. A sweep never downloads: a package whose history is only in the cloud waits for a later sweep, on this Mac or another, deleting an original reads none of it, and a history it can't read is left alone. It writes AUDIO_ORIGINALS_SWEPT, and AUDIO_ORIGINAL_REMOVAL_FAILED for an original the next sweep tries again.
  - Files: Models/AudioOriginalPolicy.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioOriginalSweep.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditActivity.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditingPreferences.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+AudioEditing.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Recording.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, Views/Settings/AudioEditingSettingsSection.swift, Views/Settings/StorageSettingsView.swift.
- The filter presets.
  - Owner: AudioFilterPresetLibrary, on the main actor: the presets the editor offers (AudioFilterPreset: an identity, a name, and a chain in ffmpeg's syntax), kept in audio-filters.json in the data directory (TextMouthPaths.audioFiltersFile), beside substitutions.json. The twelve premade presets (AudioFilterPresetLibrary.standard), each with an identity made from its name, stand in, unsaved, while there is no file.
  - Who may write it: add, update, remove, move, and resetToDefaults, each saving the file whole. It is read and written as substitutions.json is: never read or written over while only in the cloud, the premade presets standing in and a save waiting; a file that can't be used is preserved beside itself; and one that arrives from another Mac while the premade presets stand in takes their place, the change made meanwhile refused (AudioFilterPresetError.fileArrived). The editor's Apply Filter (AudioEditorFilterSheet) and Storage settings' Filter Presets… (AudioFilterPresetsView) each make one, reading the file as they open.
  - How others observe it: the editor's filter chains; AUDIO_FILTERS_FILE lines tell of each wait and preserved copy.
  - Files: Services/AudioEditing/AudioFilterPresets.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorFilterSheet.swift, Views/Settings/AudioFilterPresetsView.swift.
- The editor window (FR04): one audio file of a book, open for editing.
  - Owner: AudioEditorModel, on the main actor, one for each window, over one AudioEditSession, which it changes only through the session. It holds the cursor and the selection; the markers (AudioEditMarker), which follow the sound through edits and undo (AudioEditorTimeMap), come back from the file's edit list as the window opens, and are kept there after every change (AudioEditSession.keepMarkers: written off the main thread one at a time, a newer request taking the place of one waiting, and a save waiting first for those asked before it), Revert to Original deleting them; the audio a Cut or Copy keeps for Paste (AudioEditorClip: up to ten minutes, rendered losslessly by AudioEditSession.renderStretch(_:to:) into the window's own folder in this Mac's Audio Editor Clips, which AudioEditCaches.newClipFolder() names, and kept in the package as a take once pasted); the punch-in under way (AudioEditorPunchIn); and the levels and pauses (AudioEditorLevels: a level for every hundredth of a second, and a pause wherever they stay at least 0.4 seconds below 15 dB above the quietest tenth, kept between -60 and -30 dBFS), measured from the preview after each edit on a thread of its own (AudioEditorLevels.Reading, bridged to its task with a cancellation handler that only raises a flag, since a reader may not be cancelled while it is read), one measurement at a time, a newer one waiting for the older, cancelled one to stop. It plays through AudioEditorPlayback (AVAudioEditorPlayer, an AVPlayer over the session's composition, every seek exact, touching neither Now Playing nor the media keys), and records punch-ins through RecordingService with the Record window's capture backend and microphone choice (AudioEditorRecorder), after a count-in. Its sentences are AudioEditorWording's. Its views are AudioEditorView; its sheets, one at a time (AudioEditorSheet: a question with one field, the markers and pauses, Apply Filter, the progress of a save or an export, Export, and Audio Info); and AudioEditorWaveform, hidden from accessibility.
  - Who may write it: the window's commands, pressed as a button, a shortcut, or a VoiceOver action, which all reach AudioEditorModel.perform(_:); while a punch-in records, only Stop, Play / Pause, Punch In, Clear Selection, and Where Am I? act. AudioEditorLauncher opens the window, from the Book menu (editBookAudio) or a row's LibraryAudioCommand.editAudio(_:): it refuses a book that isn't an audiobook in a package of its own, or whose manifest plays sections (AudioEditorTracks.sections); asks which track of a book of several; closes the book's reader (closeReaderForAudioEditing), waiting up to three seconds for it; and opens the session, asking first when a history that can't be read would be started over. AudioEditorWindowController.shared keeps one window for each book, brings an open one forward, asks Save, Discard, or Cancel before a window closes over edits not saved, keeps it open while work is under way or a punch-in records, and opens the book's reader again once the window closes, when the launcher closed it. As TextMouth quits, the app delegate asks it after a library move (holdQuit): an editor still working or recording holds the quit, its window brought forward to say so; otherwise each editor with edits not saved is asked about in turn, by its book's title, in an alert over every window, each answer carried out as closing carries it out (carryOut), the markers still waiting are written, and one request to quit again goes through. Audio Info gives the book an original policy of its own (AudioEditSession.setPolicyOverride(_:), through LibraryViewModel.setAudioOriginalPolicy(_:forBook:)).
  - Keys: the Audio Editor group of ShortcutAction works in a scope of its own (ShortcutScope.audioEditor, its defaultScope), so its keys may be the reader's. KeyboardShortcutManager matches a press only against the actions whose scopes reach the key window's kind (windowKind(ofKeyWindow:)): the editor's alone in an editor window, none in a sheet over one, and elsewhere those of every scope that reaches that kind of window; a key moved in Settings or config.json leaves only the actions whose scopes overlap its own (KeyClaims). In an editor window a press with Shift matches only a binding with Shift, since none of the editor's actions takes Shift along (ShortcutAction.actionsTakingShiftAlong), so a Shift-R the user gave Release Media Controls never reaches the editor's R, Punch In. An editor key posts audioEditorShortcut with the action and the window it was pressed in, and only that window's model acts (WindowCommandRouting).
  - The Edit menu: AppKit's own, so a text field anywhere keeps its Undo Typing, its Cut, and the rest. An editor window's delegate, the editors' controller, gives the window its editor's stand-in undo manager (windowWillReturnUndoManager, AudioEditorUndoManager), whose titles and availability are the editor's Undo and Redo buttons' and which NSWindow's own undo: and redo: validate and call, and answers cut:, copy:, paste:, and selectAll: (NSMenuItemValidation) only while the editor's window is itself the key window, never in a sheet over it. Each sends the editor's command as its shortcut does (AudioEditorEditMenu.send, posting audioEditorShortcut), taken through WindowCommandRouting.
  - What it added to the session: AudioEditSession.insert(takeAt:at:using:), for a paste and for a recording made with nothing selected; makePreview(applying:), which Apply Filter's Preview plays; renderStretch(_:to:), for a clip; and saveFormat(), the format a save writes, for Audio Info and a save's first sentence.
  - The sheets: AudioEditorModel+Filters.swift's applyWouldPreview(_:on:) says whether Apply previews first (the setting on, and the preset not yet previewed on that target since Apply Filter opened, per previewedFilter, which openFilters clears); chooseApply reads it, and applyHint(for:on:) turns it into Apply's hint (AudioEditorWording.applyPreviewsFirstHint or applyHint), which AudioEditorFilterSheet gives the button. AudioEditorFilterSheet takes its AudioFilterPresetLibrary (the saved presets, unless a test gives others) and asks VoiceOver's start for Cancel when it lists no preset. In AudioEditorMarkersSheet.swift the marker, chapter marker, and pause rows share row(_:subject:commands:) over RowCommand (a title, whether it is destructive, and an action): one container labelled with the row's words, the drawn text hidden from VoiceOver, a button per command labelled rowButton(title, subject), the subjects being markerSubject, chapterSubject, and pauseSubject, and the commands as the row's actions by title; AudioEditorRenameRow is a row's rename state (the field, where the keyboard goes as the row opens, Save Name as the default button, and Cancel as the cancel button), and closeShortcut(whileRenaming:) gives Close the cancel key only while no name is typed. Tests: AudioEditorWindowTests hosts the prompt sheets and the rename row, puts the keyboard in the field (windowWithKeyboardInField), and sends keys through window.sendEvent (press), which includes AppKit's key-equivalent pass, proving that one Return runs a command once; testARowsWordsAreReadOnce skips without an assistive app.
  - How others observe it: the window shows and speaks its state: Position, Status, and Last message, which repeats the last announcement made through VoiceOverAnnouncer for a braille display. AUDIO_EDITOR lines tell of a book the editor couldn't open, of a save or an export it couldn't finish, and of markers it couldn't keep, naming an error by its kind, never by a path. The editor's two settings are AudioEditorPreferences (preferences, below). Its tests play through FakeAudioEditorPlayer, which only notes what it was asked, and record through FakeCaptureBackend, so they make no sound and need no microphone.
  - Files: ViewModels/AudioEditorModel.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+Commands.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+Editing.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+Filters.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+Listening.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+PunchIn.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+Saving.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel+Selecting.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorPrompt.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorTimeMap.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditSession.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditorPlayer.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditorLevels.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditorPreferences.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditCaches.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorView.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorCommandViews.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorCommands.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorEditMenu.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorFileSheets.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorFilterSheet.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorLauncher.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorMarkersSheet.swift, Models/TimeEntry.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorWaveform.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorWindowController.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorWording.swift, Views/Settings/AudioFilterPresetsView.swift, Views/Settings/AudioEditingSettingsSection.swift, Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift, Services/KeyboardShortcutManager.swift, App/AppDelegate.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/AudioEditorWindowTests.swift.

### Text caches and content indexes

- content.md: each text book's text cache, its text as Markdown. A transcribed audiobook's content.md is its transcript's text instead (transcripts, above).
  - Owner: MarkdownCacheService. The format's version is MarkdownCacheService.currentCacheVersion ("3.0.7"), and a book whose recorded cacheFormatVersion is older (SemanticVersion.isOutdated, which judges the metadata reader's version, above, the same way) is extracted again.
  - Who may write it: MarkdownCacheService, which writes a new cache only after extraction succeeds and replaces the old one atomically (writeCache), and, through Edit Text, the user's own editor (edited text, below). Before it replaces the content.md of a book being edited, writeCache asks BookTextEditSessions (permitTextRebuild(of:writing:)), and afterwards tells it what it wrote (textRebuilt(_:writing:fingerprint:)). generateCacheContent runs inside BookContentAnalysisService.load: when an import reads its new book, when a reader opens a book whose cache is missing or outdated, and when a stale cache is rebuilt. generateCacheAnalyzeAndStamp runs for startup maintenance (extractMissingContentCaches, through TextCacheUpdateQueue, which runs ordinary EPUBs on two utility workers and everything else one at a time), for Redo Text Extraction in Storage settings, and for the legacy migration. Maintenance leaves a book whose files are in the cloud as it is (maintenance in a cloud library, below). The extraction a cache is written from parses each EPUB document once: EPUBParser converts a document to Markdown from the DOM its contents scan parsed, and again only when a contents page later in the book names a new destination in it, and asks whether an element is hidden only in markup that holds the word (PublicationSemantics.mayCarryHiddenAttribute(_:)). HTMLToMarkdownConverter wraps emphasis and links in time that grows with what they wrap, not with what was converted before them.
  - How others observe it: BookContentAnalysisService.load reads it and parses it into DocumentContent, splitting it into chapters over Substrings of the cache, one copy per chapter (MarkdownCacheService.splitChapters(_:)).
  - Files: Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift, Services/TextCacheUpdateQueue.swift, Services/BookContentAnalysisService.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift, Parsers/EPUBParser.swift, Parsers/EPUBParser+Navigation.swift, Parsers/PublicationSemantics.swift, Parsers/HTMLToMarkdownConverter.swift.
- The content index (BookContentIndex): chapter starts and lengths, word counts, headings with their offsets, totals, and, for audiobooks, the media layout and timing, with every clip of a chapter that plays several, and, for a transcribed audiobook, its transcript text's layer (BookContentIndex.transcript: transcripts, above).
  - Owner: the package record, as its contentIndex.json (BookInfo.contentIndex), beside bookInfo.json. Its version is BookContentIndex.currentVersion ("1.0.0"). library.json keeps only the totals and contentIndexVersion, so launch never decodes every heading in the library.
  - Who may write it: BookContentAnalysisService builds it (BookContentIndex.build) from parsed content, its word counts counted as the runs of Characters that are not whitespace (Chapter.wordCount(of:), which reads a Character whole only around non-ASCII bytes and makes no substring per word), and it is saved only through LibraryPersistenceService (saveContentAnalysis or saveBookInfo(for:contentIndex:)).
  - How others observe it: BookContentAnalysisService.load uses a saved index only when it passes validationFailure(for:audioBaseURL:) and, for a text book, alignmentFailure(with:countingWords:) against the parsed chapters. One that fails is logged as CONTENT_INDEX_REJECTED and replaced by a fresh index. The reader takes headings, chapter offsets, and totals from the index inside its DocumentContent.
  - Which version to change: a change to source extraction changes MarkdownCacheService.currentCacheVersion. A change only to how the reading text or the index is derived changes BookContentIndex.currentVersion instead, so existing Markdown is indexed again without converting every source. Reading Markdown tables a row at a time (each row a paragraph, its cells one space apart, the table set apart from the text around it, as HTMLReadingText reads an HTML table) changed neither version: content.md is unchanged, and the change only adds breaks and spaces, so the saved index of every book it affects, a DOCX or Markdown book holding a Markdown table, no longer lines up with its reading text, fails the alignment check once (CONTENT_INDEX_REJECTED), and is rebuilt from the cache.
  - What guards the output: TextPipelineGoldenTests hold the digests of what the text pipeline produced on main before the performance work of October 3, for TestData's books (its two untracked DAISY audio ZIPs only where a checkout has them), synthetic books, and generated EPUBs: every content.md an import writes, the reading each book opens to (titles, text, headings, and word counts), and the converters' own output. A change meant to keep the output must leave them passing. A deliberate change of output, which changes one of the versions above, records the new digests, which each failure names, once the new output has been checked. TextPipelineBefore (TextMouthProductionTests/TextPipelineFixtures.swift) keeps the code each change replaced, for these tests and the benchmarks to compare with.
  - Files: Models/BookContentIndex.swift, Models/DocumentContent.swift, Services/BookContentAnalysisService.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/TextPipelineGoldenTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/TextPipelineFixtures.swift.
- Text cache fingerprints (TextCacheFingerprint): for the source and for content.md, the size, the modification time, and the MD5 digest, as they were when the cache was written. Times are kept in nanoseconds but compared to the microsecond (FileStamp.hasSameModificationTime(as:)), since a sync service can give a file back with its time moved by a fraction of a microsecond.
  - Owner: the package record (state.textCacheFingerprint, also copied into the index), mirrored on the library row.
  - Who may write it: MarkdownCacheService records them when it writes a cache, and BookTextEditSessions when it takes an edit. BookContentAnalysisService fingerprints a current cache written before fingerprints existed, once, the first time the cache passes every check; that result is saved by the usual save.
  - How others observe it: BookContentAnalysisService.loadExistingCache compares the files' sizes and dates with the record before it trusts the cache or its index, and reads a file for its digest only when the size is unchanged but the date is not. A changed file is logged as CACHE_STALE and the cache is rebuilt from the source. Startup maintenance compares the sizes the library row recorded with the files, two stat calls a book, to find stale caches without reading anything, and reads for its digest only a content.md of the recorded size with another date (hasRedatedTextCache), which may hold an edit saved while TextMouth was closed (edited text, below): one only touched keeps its digest, and the row records its new stamps, so the next launch reads nothing. completedTextCacheIndex checks a package's record when recovering an interrupted batch. A dataless file, whose content is only in the cloud, is never judged by the size and date it reports, since an evicted iCloud Drive file can report 0 bytes, and never read for its digest (FileStampComparison.dataless).
  - Files: Models/TextCacheFingerprint.swift, Services/BookContentAnalysisService.swift, Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift.
- Kept reading text (ReadingTextCache, PERF22): this Mac's copy of a large text book's reading text (each chapter's title, text, and headings as parsed), at MachineLocalPaths.readingTextCache/<book ID>.readingtext, for a content.md of at least 1 MiB. The file is a prefix ("TMRT", the layout version, the header length), a JSON header (the producer, the ReadingTextKey, the chapter entries), then the UTF-8 chapter texts.
  - Who may write it: BookContentAnalysisService.loadExistingCache, only when given the cache (only ReaderViewModel.readContent passes it), after parsing a content.md its record vouches for, in the background (storeInBackground: a partial file renamed into place). MarkdownCacheService.writeCache, LibraryViewModel.removeDeletedBook, BookTextEditSessions, when it takes an edit, and LibraryViewModel.settleFeedFileRemovals(_:failures:), with which every removal of a feed item's file ends (FR11's branches A2 and B), remove a book's file; removeExpired removes files unused for a day (by modification time, which each use refreshes), at startLibrary and after each store.
  - How others observe it: used instead of parsing when the fingerprints verify, the saved index validates and was measured from the same content.md, and the key matches (the record's content.md digest, its ContentFileIdentity of size, modification time, inode, and status-change time, and the book title), together with the producer (BookContentIndex.currentVersion and the build identity) and the layout. The chapters must pass alignmentFailure(with:countingWords: false). Otherwise the book is parsed; a damaged or mismatched file is logged as READING_TEXT_CACHE_REJECTED and removed. content.md is read only for a digest or a parse.
  - Files: Services/ReadingTextCache.swift, Services/BookContentAnalysisService.swift, Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+BookOperations.swift.
- Edited text (FR05): a text book's content.md, or a transcribed audiobook's, never a feed's episode's or article's (BookTextEditor.canEditText(of:isTranscribed:), FR11), as the user edits it in an editor of their own (Edit Text in Default Editor and Edit Text With…).
  - Owner: BookTextEditSessions.shared, one session for each book being edited: its baseline (BookTextBaseline: the fingerprints of the source, a text book's own file or a transcribed audiobook's transcript.json, as BookTextEditSessions.sourceURL(of:) names it, and of content.md, taken when the edit began, or when TextMouth last took or rebuilt the text), the edit the last check refused, and a copy a rebuild under way kept. BookTextEditor.shared, on the main actor, starts sessions, for a text book or an audiobook whose package holds a transcript (looked for off the main thread), watches each content.md (ContentFileWatcher, which checks 0.75 seconds after the last change and follows a file an editor's save replaces), and says what happened. A session ends when its book leaves the library, when its transcript is replaced or deleted (BookTextEditor.end(_:)), or when TextMouth quits.
  - Who may write it: the user's editor. Checks run on the sessions' serial queue, as storage work (StorageBoundary.perform(.contentAnalysis)): when content.md settles, when TextMouth becomes active, and before a reader reads the book. A check takes an edit when content.md changed and the source did not: it parses it, saves the new index with new fingerprints at the current cache format (LibraryPersistenceService.saveContentAnalysis), and drops the book's kept reading text. A transcribed audiobook's edit is taken into its transcript instead (TranscriptStore.takeEdit): TranscriptEditing.apply lays the parsed chapters over the transcript's sentences (TranscriptEditAlignment), each sentence taking the words that stand for it and keeping its time, a changed one dropping its words' timings (TranscriptSentence.rewrite(as:)), and one whose every word went being removed; transcript.json is written with editedAt, and the audio index's layer is saved with that transcript.json as its source and the user's content.md, unchanged, as its cache, so no load writes the text again over the edit. A layer the index or the record refuses puts transcript.json back as it was. An edit it can't take (BookTextEditRefusal: notText, noChapters, sourceChanged, transcriptChanged, chaptersChanged(found:expected:), or failed, which covers an audiobook whose audio changed since it was transcribed) is first kept beside content.md as content.md.edited- and the time (keepCopy(of:bytes:), through SavedFile.preserveUnusable and LibraryIndexQuarantine under TextMouthPaths.keptTextCopyMarker; an earlier copy of the same bytes is used again).
    - While an edit not taken stands beside an unchanged source, nothing replaces content.md. ReaderViewModel.readContent asks settlePendingEdit(of:), which answers readTaken, readAsUsual, or hold; hold throws BookTextEditPending for a text book, which the reader shows in place of the book (loadHeldByTextEdit) until an edit is taken, and leaves out only an audiobook's transcript text, its audio loading and playing as ever.
    - MarkdownCacheService.writeCache, the one path every rebuild of a text book's content.md takes, calls permitTextRebuild(of:writing:) before it replaces the file. That keeps any other text it would replace (TEXT_EDIT_KEPT) and refuses over an edit not taken (TEXT_EDIT_HELD), so the cache batch and Redo Text Extraction skip that book; Redo counts the books it left so (HeldTextEditTally), and Storage settings say how many (textReextractionOutcome(skippedInCloud:heldByEdits:)). textRebuilt(_:writing:fingerprint:) then makes the written text the session's baseline. For a book no session watches, it keeps the text content.md holds, when that isn't what the book's record says TextMouth last wrote or took there, beside it before the rebuild (keepTextSavedWithoutSession(of:replacedBy:), TEXT_EDIT_KEPT), and refuses the rebuild when no copy can be made.
    - Launch's cache batch takes an edit saved while no session watched, as while TextMouth was closed: for a book whose content.md no longer matches its row's fingerprints, and which no earlier run committed, it checks content.md as a session's save is checked, against the fingerprints the book's record keeps (BookTextEditSessions.settleEditSavedWhileClosed, recordedTextCacheFingerprint(of:access:)). An edit beside an unchanged source is taken when it parses, logged as TEXT_EDIT_ADOPTED and announced to no one; any other is kept beside content.md, and the cache is rebuilt from the source.
    - The statistics pass skips books being edited (commitStatistics).
    - FileImportService.isPackageContentName (TextMouthPaths.isKeptTextCopy) keeps the copies out of the candidates for the book's own file.
  - How others observe it: LibraryViewModel.commitTextEdit applies the analysis to the row and sends what the edit holds (BookTextEdit.text): LibraryBookUpdates.textReplaced for a text book's DocumentContent, and transcriptReplaced for an audiobook's TranscriptText, which ReaderViewModel.showTranscript(_:) shows as the book plays on. Readers show a text book's new text (ReaderViewModel+EditedText), the cursor at the same words (EditedTextCursor), playing on if they were; a reader still loading shows it once loaded, and a reader held over an edit opens the book with it. VoiceOver hears "Text updated"; why an edit wasn't taken, with the copy's name, once for each saved version; and where a rebuild kept replaced text, once for each copy; each only while TextMouth is active, or as soon as it becomes so. An edit saved while TextMouth isn't running has no session: a text book's is taken by the next launch's batch, or kept beside content.md by whatever rebuilds the text first, as a reader that opens the book before the batch reaches it does, and a transcribed audiobook's is rebuilt over once TranscriptTextLoader has kept it beside content.md (transcripts, above).
  - Files: Services/BookTextEditing.swift, Services/BookTextEditor.swift, Models/BookTextEditWording.swift, Services/TranscriptEdit.swift, Services/TranscriptEditAlignment.swift, Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift, Services/LibraryIndexQuarantine.swift, Services/SavedFile.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, Services/FileImportService+Packages.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+TextEdits.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift, ViewModels/LibraryBookUpdates.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+EditedText.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates.swift, Views/Reader/ReaderBookCommands.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift.

### The data directory

- Its location, and moving it.
  - Owner: StorageBoundary.shared, which holds the current StorageGeneration, the data directory's root numbered by the moves this process has published, and the earlier ones. TextMouthPaths.root is the current generation's root: at launch the folder TextMouthPaths.root(for:) names (~/.TextMouth, or the folder the textMouthDataDirectory preference names, which a test host ignores), and after a move the new location. It changes only when a move publishes its copy, never because the preference changed.
  - Who may write it: a published move, and a check whose bookmark finds the library under another path (its availability, below). TextMouthPaths.moveDataDirectory(to:library:) changes the preference, the current generation, and the library only once the copy is published, and publishes the generation between the persistence queues' writes (LibraryPersistenceService.betweenWrites), so no write resolves its file under the old root and writes it afterwards. Change Location's Move Library Here and Reset to Default in Storage settings, and a changed dataDirectoryPath in config.json, reach the move in LibraryViewModel+Migration, which refuses while the index cannot be read or the data directory is unavailable, while any book is open (openBooksRefusal, naming the books), and while any book inside the data directory has files only in the cloud, which copying would download (countBooksOnlyInCloud, through MaintenanceFileAccess.itemInCloud).
    - Choosing: a folder chosen in Storage settings (StorageSettingsView.makeLibraryFolderPanel, a chooser that shows hidden folders and can make one) is judged off the main thread, from metadata alone, by StorageDestinationAssessment: a move for a missing or empty folder, Use This Library for one holding a library (an identity marker, or library.json beside a Library folder), the library in use, a new library in the default location while the library is unavailable, or a refusal with its reason. StorageChoice and StorageWording word the confirmation or the status line.
    - Holding: withStorageWorkHeld(moving:waiting:_:) holds new storage work back (StorageBoundary.holdWork(for:)), waits for the import, text extraction, and book analysis under way (StorageWorkHold.waitForWorkUnderWay), flushes the queued saves and logs, runs the move, and then lets the held work go on under the new generation. Work that captured paths before the publication moves them under the new root first (rebasedToCurrent and rebaseToCurrent), so nothing is written under the old one. Rebuild Directory holds storage work the same way, with no move, while it relocates and adopts packages, so an import that has published its package but not yet added its book finishes first and is never adopted as an orphan too; Storage settings show what the rebuild waits for. It reconciles the library's own books with the Library folder, a feed's items waiting aside meanwhile (Library.withFeedItemsSetAside(_:), FR11). So do the folder edits made from the library's sheets (performLibraryTransition: moving books to a folder, and moving, renaming, or deleting a folder while keeping its contents), which then move their packages and rewrite their records off the main actor (OffMainWork.runThrowing), VoiceOver hearing what they wait for. Each hold says what it is for (StorageHoldReason: moving the data directory, rebuilding it, or moving books between folders), and only one is in place at a time: while one is, holdWork(for:) refuses another with a StorageBusyError naming the operation under way, so a refused move, rebuild, or folder edit says what is actually running, as in "TextMouth is moving books between folders. Try again when that finishes."
    - Copying: copyAndPublish runs TextMouthPaths.moveDataDirectory on a background queue (OffMainWork) with the task-local DataDirectoryMigrator.copyObserver bound, which hears each DataDirectoryCopyProgress and answers Cancel. DataDirectoryMigrator takes each location as the real folder its links lead to (DataDirectoryLocation): a data directory that is a link is copied from its folder, and a new location that is a link to an empty folder receives the data in that folder, the link staying as it is. Before copying, it refuses overlapping real folders, naming both, a link that leads nowhere, a file at the new location, and a folder that is not empty. It copies into a hidden staging folder (.TextMouth-migration- followed by a UUID) beside the new location's real folder, one item at a time in the order DataDirectoryCopyPlan gives: each package whole, which clones it on one APFS volume, the books first and the small files at the top last, and each folder it makes given its original's metadata with copyfile. Cancel stops the copy between items, or after the last, with DataDirectoryMigrationError.cancelled, and the staged copy is deleted. The migrator points the copy's package records, each feed's index (stageFeedIndexes, a feed item's package staged in its feed's folder of the copy, FR11), and library.json at the new location and checks them, then looks again: the name must still lead to the same folder, and that folder must still be empty or missing. It removes nothing but an empty folder, with rmdir, which the kernel refuses once anything is inside, and publishes the copy with one rename (moveItem, which never replaces an item). Anything that arrives meanwhile stops the move: the staged copy is deleted, whatever arrived stays, and the data directory in use and its setting stay as they were. The library is taken once the wait is over, and nothing should change it during the copy; a change made anyway is kept, its paths rebased.
    - Use This Library (useLibraryReturningOutcome(at:relaunching:)) moves nothing but holds the storage work itself (StorageHoldReason.switchingLibraries, "TextMouth is switching to another library and opening again on it."), so a move, a rebuild, or a folder edit under way refuses it with the StorageBusyError naming that operation, and nothing starts one while the switch runs. It writes pending saves, refuses while changes can't be saved unless the library is unavailable, records the folder for the next launch with TextMouthPaths.setCustomRoot (nil for the default location), and has TextMouthRelauncher open a new instance of TextMouth and quit this one. A process hosting tests never opens again.
  - How others observe it: LibraryViewModel.dataDirectoryMove, a DataDirectoryMoveProgress, is set while a move runs: every Library window shows DataDirectoryMoveSheet and refuses to open or add books, Storage settings show DataDirectoryMoveProgressView, VoiceOver hears what the move waits for, its start, each quarter, and Cancel, and AppDelegate calls DataDirectoryMoveProgress.stopForQuit before quitting. config.json's dataDirectoryPath names the folder in use after every attempt. The Storage settings status line, which VoiceOver announces once, or else the library's alert, says "Done. Data is now at…", naming the folder a link leads to, "Move cancelled. The library is still at…", or why nothing moved, naming what arrived and where. The old folder is left where it was.
  - Files: Services/StorageGeneration.swift, Services/StorageDestinationAssessment.swift, Services/DataDirectoryMigrator.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift, ViewModels/DataDirectoryMoveProgress.swift, Views/Library/DataDirectoryMoveSheet.swift, Views/Settings/StorageSettingsView.swift.
- Its availability: whether the data directory can be used.
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel.dataDirectoryUnavailability, the reason it can't (DataDirectoryUnavailability), nil while it can, and DataDirectoryGate, the data directories found unavailable, kept by path, which every writer of the data directory consults.
  - Who may write it: only the library model's data-directory functions, in LibraryViewModel.swift. checkDataDirectory runs DataDirectoryCheck, which reads metadata only, inside DatalessFiles.withoutDownloading: at launch, before anything is created or loaded; on Try Again (retryDataDirectory); when the volume holding the data directory is unmounted (volumeDidUnmount, from the workspace's notifications); and when a write found a custom data directory's folder missing (dataDirectoryWriteFoundFolderMissing, which LibraryPersistenceService posts through DataDirectoryEvents). The default location is always available, and created when missing. A custom one must be a readable folder holding this library: an identity marker naming the identity this Mac expects or, for a library given none yet, a library index. When its path is missing, its bookmark may find the library under another path, never in a Trash, which then becomes the data directory. The library index, the identity marker, the Feeds folder, and each feed's folder and index (DataDirectoryCheck.assessLibrary(in:itemStatus:), through feedIndexFiles(in:), FR11), when only in the cloud, are asked of the sync service, and the library stays unavailable (notDownloaded, whose explanation names the library index whichever it was) until a later check finds them on this Mac.
  - How others observe it: every Library window shows DataDirectoryUnavailableView in place of the library, with the reason and, read right after it, where to choose another folder (DataDirectoryUnavailableText.anotherFolderPointer, which names Storage settings' two buttons by their titles in StorageLocationWording, so it follows any rename), says so in its title, and refuses the app-wide actions that add or open books (DataDirectoryUnavailableText.refusal(of:)); Storage settings show the reason and offer Choose Another Library and Use Default Location; their status line asks whether the data directory exists off the main thread, as the pane appears, after each data-directory operation, and when its path or availability changes (StorageSettingsView.directoryCheck), never while drawing, since a network or unplugged volume can block there. Meanwhile TextMouthPaths.ensureDirectoryStructure throws DataDirectoryUnavailableError, and it never creates a custom data directory or any folder above it; library index writes are skipped, not counted as failures; record writes that fail stay listed for a retry; ConfigService neither reads, writes, nor watches config.json; the log writer writes to ~/Library/Logs/TextMouth instead (the diagnostic logs, below); and ReadingStatsService and TextSubstitutionService use stand-ins that they never save over their files, as they do while a file is only in the cloud (files several Macs write, below). A check that finds the library again posts dataDirectoryDidBecomeAvailable, and those services read their files. After a loss while TextMouth ran, reloadLibraryAfterDataDirectoryReturned reads library.json afresh, carrying the session's newer reading positions and moved packages into it.
  - Files: Services/DataDirectoryAvailability.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+IndexRecovery.swift, Services/LibraryPersistenceService.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, Views/Library/DataDirectoryUnavailableView.swift, Views/ContentView.swift.
- Its identity: which library a data directory holds.
  - Owner: the marker library-identity.json at the data directory's root (LibraryIdentity: a UUID and the date it was given), and DataDirectoryPreferences, this Mac's record of the custom location's path, a bookmark of its folder, and the identity expected there, all machine-local preferences.
  - Who may write it: LibraryIdentity.adopt writes a marker only where there is none, creating the file exclusively, so one another Mac wrote a moment earlier stands: when the default location is created, and when a library without one starts (recordLibraryIdentity). TextMouthPaths.setCustomRoot records the path, the bookmark, and the identity together; recordLibraryIdentity records the identity again whenever a library starts, and a bookmark for a location chosen before bookmarks; TextMouthPaths.recordRelocation records a folder the bookmark found.
  - How others observe it: DataDirectoryCheck compares the marker with the identity expected, so a folder that only has the right name is never taken for the library, and StorageDestinationAssessment recognizes a folder holding another copy of the library in use.
  - Files: Services/LibraryIdentity.swift, Services/DataDirectoryAvailability.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift.
- The session lease: this Mac's note, in a library another Mac can open, that it has the library open.
  - Owner: LibrarySessionLease.shared, which keeps this Mac's file, Sessions/<Mac ID>.json (the computer's name, when TextMouth was last active, and the app's version), in a data directory in a cloud location or on another volume, asking the cloud services' provider where the directory is. One on the startup disk gets none. Its disk work, writing and refreshing the file, reading the other Macs' files, and removing stale ones, runs on its own serial queue (diskQueue) with dataless files left in the cloud, so a Sessions folder that hasn't synced never stalls the main thread; refresh() is awaited, and ending the lease at quit waits at most two seconds (removalWait) for the file's removal.
  - Who may write it: the library model begins the lease when the library starts, and again after Try Again, writing the file and refreshing it every minute, and pauses it when the library is lost; AppDelegate ends it at quit, removing the file. A refresh removes leases untouched for 30 days, and the file a move left in the old data directory.
  - How others observe it: each Mac's refresh reads the other files with dataless files left in the cloud, judging one only in the cloud by its modification date. Another Mac's lease refreshed in the last three minutes brings one warning a session (TextMouth Is Open on Another Mac, with Continue and Quit TextMouth); nothing is locked. A file whose name begins with this Mac's ID, as a sync service's conflict copy of this Mac's own lease does ("<Mac ID> 2.json"), never counts as another Mac, and ages out as any lease does.
  - Files: Services/LibrarySessionLease.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, App/AppDelegate.swift.
- Files several Macs write: reading_stats.json and substitutions.json, which TextMouth reads whole and replaces whole, and which another Mac sharing a library in the cloud writes too.
  - Owner: ReadingStatsService and TextSubstitutionService, each for its own file, which each reads through SavedFile with dataless files left in the cloud, so a read on the main thread never waits on the network. SavedFile.read(_:using:) tells a missing file, one only in the cloud, one that can't be read, and its bytes apart.
  - Who may write it: each service, never over a file only in the cloud, and over one it can't read or decode only after keeping a copy of it, or logging that none could be made. A file found only in the cloud is asked of the sync service (SavedFile.requestDownload(of:)), with one line in the log, and stand-ins are used meanwhile, never saved over it. Fresh statistics stand in (statsAwaitFileInCloud), counting this session's listening; each save first reads the file again, and the first that finds it on this Mac adds the stand-ins to what it holds (ReadingStats.adding(_:)) and saves the sum. ReadingStatsService.statsReadFromFile says whether the statistics hold what the file held: true once it was read and decoded (at launch, when the library is available again, or once a file only in the cloud was added to the stand-ins), never for stand-ins. The standard rules stand in for the substitutions (rulesAwaitFileInCloud); the file is read again when the app becomes active and before each save, a save meanwhile is reported to PersistenceFailureCenter as unsaved, with a retry (SavedFile.InCloudError), and once the file is on this Mac its rules replace the stand-ins and the notice is withdrawn, dropping the change made to them. With no substitutions.json at all, the standard rules stand in without being written, and the file is written only when a rule changes, so one the sync service brings down later is adopted, on activation or before the next save, without a conflict copy. A file that can't be read or decoded, as a write cut short on another Mac leaves it, is preserved beside itself before anything replaces it (SavedFile.preserveUnusable, through LibraryIndexQuarantine), and fresh statistics or the standard rules stand in until the next save replaces it.
  - How others observe it: the Stats tab, the Text tab, and the reader's substitution pass use the stand-ins until the file is read, and READING_STATS_FILE and SUBSTITUTIONS_FILE lines record each wait, arrival, and preserved copy. A sync service's conflict copy of library.json, a package record, the identity marker, config.json, or one of these two files is never read in place of the file it copies, nor removed; a package copied under another name is never adopted as a second book; and nothing merges what two Macs wrote. MultiMacCrashSafetyTests feeds library.json, the packages and their records, the identity marker, the leases, config.json, these two files, and the checkpoint journal what another Mac can leave (conflict copies, files cut short, files only in the cloud, packages that disappeared, another Mac's lease, an index listing packages not here yet), and checks that the code reading each may log a line, warn of another Mac, show the recovery sheet or the Library Not Available screen, or leave a row as it is, but never crashes, hangs the main thread, or deletes anything.
  - Files: Services/SavedFile.swift, Services/ReadingStatsService.swift, Services/TextSubstitutionService.swift, Services/LibraryIndexQuarantine.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/MultiMacCrashSafetyTests.swift.
- This Mac's own folders.
  - Owner: MachineLocalPaths: ~/Library/Logs/TextMouth, where the logs go when Storage settings choose this Mac and while the data directory can't take them (the diagnostic logs, below), and, in ~/Library/Application Support/com.textmouth.TextMouth, Working Copies, Import Staging, Archive Extraction, Recordings (MachineLocalPaths.recordings), and Feed Downloads (MachineLocalPaths.feedDownloads), beside one file, Kept Books.json (MachineLocalPaths.keptBooks), and, in ~/Library/Caches/com.textmouth.TextMouth, Reading Text (MachineLocalPaths.readingTextCache), Feeds (MachineLocalPaths.feedCaches), Audio Edits (AudioEditScratch.root), and Audio Editor Clips (AudioEditCaches.clipsRoot), which macOS may empty. They never sync, and stay usable while the data directory is unavailable; under hosted tests they are in the sandbox.
  - Who may write it: the log writer (the diagnostic logs, below); ReaderWorkingCopies (the working copy, below); KeptBooks, Kept Books.json (books kept on this Mac, below); ReadingTextCache (kept reading text, above); RecordingService, whose takes stay in Recordings until they are saved as books, or until a punch-in's edit holds them (a take being recorded, below), and the Record window, which saves or discards a take left there (takes left behind, below); the feed engine, whose finished downloads wait in Feed Downloads and whose fetch details, resume data, and list of the downloads TextMouth quit during are in its Caches folder (feeds, below); audio editing, whose filtered stretches and silence wait in Audio Edits, a folder for each book, until the book's edits are saved or its editing session ends (AudioEditScratch, audio edits, above); the audio editor window, whose audio kept for Paste waits in Audio Editor Clips, a folder for each window, removed as the window closes (the editor window, above), and what a crash, or a quit with editors open, left in either folder is removed at the next launch, off the main thread, never a folder in use (AudioEditCaches.removeStale, from startLibrary, logged as AUDIO_EDIT_CACHES_SWEPT: a book's while it is being edited or saved, looked at and set aside under the lock its folder is made under, and a window's while the window is open); and imports, through ImportScratchSpace, which stages packages and extracts archives here while the data directory is on this folder's volume (the startup disk, where iCloud Drive's and File Provider's folders are too), and otherwise beside each package's destination and in the data directory's Archive Extraction folder, so publishing stays one rename on one volume. ImportScratchSpace never creates a folder inside a data directory that vanished.
  - How others observe it: at launch, ArchiveExtractionDirectory.removeLeftovers sweeps both Archive Extraction folders, listing the data directory's with dataless files left in the cloud, and removes the data directory's once it is empty while this Mac's is in use. An extraction folder is named for the process that made it (extract- followed by its process number and a UUID); the sweep removes one of this process's own that no import of it uses, and one whose maker has ended, by the rule the test sandbox sweep follows (TextMouthRuntime.isFolderMakerRunning, below), so a later process that took the number keeps no leftover. Only plain folders go. Each import sweeps the folder it stages in, and, when that is this Mac's, the destination folder that earlier versions staged in. While an import stages a book, the book's tmbDirectory is the staged package, wherever it is (ImportStaging.directory(ofStagedPackage:)).
  - Files: Models/CloudStorageContract.swift, Services/ImportScratchSpace.swift, Services/ImportStaging.swift, Services/ArchiveExtraction.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditComposition.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditCaches.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, App/TextMouthRuntime.swift.

### Cloud state

CLOUD-STORAGE-RESEARCH-2026-10-03.md and CLOUD-STORAGE-PLAN-2026-10-03.md, at the repository's root, explain the design, and CLOUD-STORAGE-MANUAL-TESTS-2026-10-03.md says what real drives have verified and what remains to check by hand.

- Where the library lives, and each book's cloud state.
  - Owner: nothing stores either. Both are read fresh from file-system metadata, with downloading switched off, by the two services CloudServices holds. The contract (Models/CloudStorageContract.swift) defines what they answer with (StorageLocation, CloudItemStatus, BookPackage, BookDownloadProgress, BookCloudState, and BookCloudError) and the words each state and failure is shown and spoken in. Until TextMouthApp installs the services, LocalItemStatusProvider and LocalBookStorage treat every library as local; tests install fakes (TextMouthProductionTests/CloudTestDoubles.swift) and put the previous services back.
    - UbiquitousItemStatusProvider reads single items, files and folders: Foundation's ubiquitous-item keys and the file system's dataless flag, which overrules a key that disagrees, and, from the same read, whether the item carries sync flags of its own (SyncFlagReporting.flaggedStatus(of:), a FlaggedItemStatus). It tells where a folder lives from its path (iCloud Drive's Mobile Documents, or a File Provider drive in CloudStorage, named for its service), from iCloud Drive managing it elsewhere, as it does Desktop and Documents, or from its volume, and it asks the sync service to download a file or remove its local copy (FileManager's startDownloadingUbiquitousItem and evictUbiquitousItem).
    - BookCloudStorageService reads whole books: each look at a package lists it and reads the state of each file and of the package folder itself (a Survey), and decides once which items the sync service syncs (Survey.packageItem). The package is the one item when it carries sync flags and none of its listed files does, as on iCloud Drive, whose files inside a package carry none and are refused, as items that don't exist, when a download or a removal is asked for one of them; otherwise each file is, as on Google Drive and Dropbox, whose files carry flags of their own (as their package folders do too). For a package synced as one item, the book's state comes from the package's flags, Downloading while the sync service fetches it whole; the refusal of a book with changes not uploaded reads the package's upload flag; a download asks for the package and ends on its download error; and a removal evicts it. Nothing inside such a package can be looked up while it is evicted without downloading the whole package, so every check of a file inside a package runs with downloading off, as the reader's and Book Info's do (LocalBookFiles.availability(of:isDataless:)); Book Info looks at the package first and then the file (LibraryBookInfoReader).
    - It downloads by asking the sync service for every missing item and checking every half second until all are here, failing at once while this Mac is offline (NetworkReachability), when an item reports a download error, or when the package disappears, and after a minute in which nothing arrives or downloads. Meanwhile it listens, through PublishedFileProgress (Progress.addSubscriber), for the progress the sync service publishes for the package as a whole, as iCloud Drive publishes it for a package it syncs whole, and for each file still to come, as Google Drive and Dropbox publish it. The listener hands on only a download of the item it listens to (isDownload(_:of:)): an upload, or the progress the system also hands a folder's listener for each item inside the folder, is not, which it tells from the operation and the URL the published progress carries in its user info, since the proxy's own properties can still read nil then.
