The parts it hears fold into BookDownloadProgress (completedBytes by size, partialFiles by files), in whole percent per file, never going back, forgotten once the file is here, and a growing part keeps a stall away.
    - It removes downloads, refusing while changes wait to upload, and reporting a removal the sync service refuses as BookCloudError.removalFailed.
  - Who may write it: BookCloudStates.shared, on the main actor, records what the library list and the readers learn: where the library lives, and each book's latest state, a download's progress included. LibraryCloudRefresher.shared refreshes it while a Library window is visible: when one appears, when the app becomes active, when a book is added, moved, or removed, and on the cadence LibraryCloudRefreshCadence gives, every minute, or every 2 seconds while any book downloads. Those 2-second refreshes read only the books recorded as downloading, and every thirtieth reads every book, so the rest of the list is still read once a minute (LibraryCloudRefreshScope); every refresh an event asks for reads every book. Refreshes never overlap, and each reads off the main thread: where the library lives, once (libraryLocation(), then state(of:in:) for each package), stopping there for a library outside a cloud location. BookCloudStates.refresh records the results in one assignment, publishing nothing when nothing changed. A download TextMouth runs is bracketed by beginDownload and endDownload, and refreshes leave that book's state to it meanwhile. In a process hosting tests the shared refresher never refreshes.
  - How others observe it: every Library window observes BookCloudStates.shared. Only in a cloud location do its rows show and speak each book's note (BookCloudNote) and does TimeRemainingBar count the books in the cloud; elsewhere the list is as it was.
  - Files: Models/CloudStorageContract.swift, Services/UbiquitousItemStatusProvider.swift, Services/BookCloudStorageService.swift, Services/PublishedFileProgress.swift, Services/NetworkReachability.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Cloud.swift, Views/Library/BookCloudNote.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift.
- The list's cloud commands.
  - Owner: LibraryCloudCommands.shared, which runs Download Now, Remove Download, Keep on This Mac, Stop Keeping on This Mac, and Show in Finder. LibraryCloudCommand.commands(for:isSelection:states:kept:) decides which a book or a selection offers, from the recorded states and the books kept on this Mac.
  - Who may write it: a book row's context menu, and its VoiceOver actions, which offer the same commands under the same names, and the Library menu, for the two keep commands (books kept on this Mac, below). Downloads start in three ways, each downloading its books one at a time and recording each one's progress and how it ended: Download Now; Keep on This Mac, for the books it keeps that are in the cloud; and the refresh that finds kept books only in the cloud. Each says and logs what its DownloadOrigin calls for. Remove Download refuses a book open in a reader or kept on this Mac without asking the storage (BookCloudError.bookIsOpen and .keptOnThisMac), the storage refuses one with changes not uploaded, and a second request for a book under way does nothing.
  - How others observe it: BookCloudStates.shared shows each download in every window. VoiceOverAnnouncer announces each command's start and, when every download was removed, one summary from the window that asked; a removal that kept any download is shown in an alert alone (LibraryCloudRefusal), whose message is the whole summary of what was removed, what stayed, and why, with nothing announced besides (REV14's alert-only rule, #2094); Keep on This Mac's one announcement covers the start of the downloads it begins, and a refresh's downloads of kept books announce nothing. LibraryCloudWording words every title and announcement. Book Info reads a book's metadata off the main thread through LibraryBookInfoReader and never downloads: a book only in the cloud shows the metadata the library keeps, and writing such an EPUB's file is refused.
  - Files: ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Cloud.swift, Models/LibraryCloudWording.swift, Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift, Views/Library/LibraryBookInfoLoader.swift.
- Books kept on this Mac (Keep on This Mac).
  - Owner: KeptBooks.shared, on the main actor: the IDs of the books this Mac keeps downloaded, a choice of this Mac's alone, as Finder's Keep Downloaded is. It is saved atomically in this Mac's own folder (MachineLocalPaths.keptBooks, Kept Books.json), never in library.json or a package record, which every Mac reads. A file that can't be read or decoded is preserved beside itself (SavedFile) and keeps no book. A book that leaves the library leaves its ID behind, unread, so a package taken back into the library keeps the choice made for it.
  - Who may write it: LibraryCloudCommands.keep(_:in:from:) and stopKeeping(_:from:), from a row's context menu and its VoiceOver actions (LibraryCloudCommand.Kind.keepOnThisMac and .stopKeepingOnThisMac), and from the Library menu's Keep on This Mac and Stop Keeping on This Mac, which post keepOnThisMac and stopKeepingOnThisMac; the Library window that takes the command (LibraryCloudWindowSupport, through LibraryView's takesCommand(_:)) runs it on its selection with setKeptFromMenu(_:for:in:from:), and when no row of the selection would offer it, VoiceOver hears why nothing changed (LibraryCloudWording.keepMenuRefusal). Keeping a book that is in the cloud, or whose download failed, downloads it at once, as Download Now does, under one announcement for both (keepStarted); stopping downloads and removes nothing.
  - How others observe it: Library windows observe it. LibraryCloudCommand.commands(for:isSelection:states:kept:) offers Keep on This Mac for the books not kept and Stop Keeping on This Mac for the kept ones, only in a cloud location and whatever each book's state, so a book waiting for sync can be kept before it arrives. Rows show and speak "Kept on this Mac" after the cloud note (BookKeptNote, BookCloudStates.listsAsKept), and TimeRemainingBar counts the kept books (countKept). After each refresh, LibraryCloudRefresher hands the library to LibraryCloudCommands.downloadEvictedKeptBooks(in:), which downloads every kept book the refresh recorded as only in the cloud, announcing nothing; one whose download failed waits keptBookRetryDelay, five minutes, before a refresh tries it again. Remove Download refuses a kept book (BookCloudError.keptOnThisMac). The log has KEEP_ON_MAC lines for keeping and stopping, and KEPT_BOOK_DOWNLOAD lines for the downloads.
  - Files: Services/KeptBooks.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Cloud.swift, Models/LibraryCloudWording.swift, Models/CloudStorageContract.swift, Views/Library/BookCloudNote.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift, Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift.
- Maintenance in a cloud library.
  - Owner: MaintenanceFileAccess, through which the startup repair, the metadata refresh, the text cache batch, Redo Text Extraction, and the statistics pass ask whether a book's files are in the cloud, and read them: live in the app, and a fake in hosted tests, which cannot make real dataless files.
  - Who may write it: each pass checks, before it reads a book, that none of the files it will read is in the cloud (fileInCloud(of:reading:), with the MaintainedFiles it reads), reads with dataless files left in the cloud, and checks again before it saves, so nothing read from a file that went to the cloud meanwhile is committed. presence(of:) answers present, absent, or in the cloud, and only an absent file counts as missing. Each pass counts the books it left in its CloudSkips.
  - How others observe it: a pass that skipped any book writes one MAINTENANCE_SKIPPED_IN_CLOUD line, and Redo Text Extraction also says how many in Storage settings' status line (textReextractionOutcome(skippedInCloud:)). A skipped book is caught up the next time its files are on this Mac, and a reader that downloads a book rebuilds its outdated cache as it loads.
  - Files: Services/MaintenanceFileAccess.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Migration.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+MetadataRefresh.swift, Services/FileImportService.swift, Services/TextCacheUpdateQueue.swift, Services/BookContentAnalysisService.swift, Models/TextCacheFingerprint.swift.

### Preferences

- The preferences store.
  - Owner: TextMouthRuntime.current.preferences, which is UserDefaults.standard in the app and a sandboxed store under hosted tests. Each preference has one owner that reads and writes it:
    - SettingsViewModel: the Voice, Playback, and Miscellaneous settings, through properties backed by @AppStorage.
    - SoundEffectService: whether sounds play, their volume, and each event's sound.
    - TextSubstitutionService: whether the substitution rules apply, and the repeated-symbol style, threshold, and symbol set (repeatedSymbolStyle, repeatedSymbolThreshold, and repeatedSymbolSet), which that switch does not affect. It also owns the rules, kept in substitutions.json.
    - KeyboardShortcutManager: the record of the shortcuts the user edited, under keyboardShortcutEdits, from which every action's keys in force follow, no key in force for two actions in one kind of window (ShortcutScope: every window but an audio editor's, Library windows, reader windows, the audio editor's windows, or the Record window; keyboard shortcuts, below), and the double-press time, under shortcutDoublePressSeconds. The whole map of every action's keys that earlier releases saved, under customKeyboardBindings, is read once, at the first launch without a record, and never written again.
    - SleepTimerService: the last timer started, under sleepTimer.lastUsedDuration.
    - TextMouthPaths and the library model, through DataDirectoryPreferences: the data directory's location, a bookmark of its folder, and the identity of the library expected there, under textMouthDataDirectory, textMouthDataDirectoryBookmark, and textMouthLibraryIdentity.
    - LibrarySessionLease: this Mac's ID, which names its file in a library's Sessions folder and, in a library in the cloud, the notes a launch leaves in a feed's folder when it finds the feed's record missing (FeedRecordAbsence), under textMouthSessionMacID.
    - RecordingInputChoice: the microphone the Record window and the audio editor's punch-ins record from, under recordingInputDeviceID, empty until one is chosen.
    - RecordingLevelAnnouncements (Services/RecordingPreferences.swift): whether the Record window announces the level while recording, under recordingAnnouncesLevel, off until turned on, and how many seconds apart, under recordingLevelAnnouncementSeconds (SettingsPolicy.recordingLevelAnnouncementSeconds: 5 to 60, 10 until changed), read as the Record window opens and written as they change.
    - AudioOriginalPolicy: what happens to the original audio of recordings and edited audiobooks, under recordingOriginalPolicy and recordingOriginalDays, the names it had when only recordings kept an original, which Storage settings' Audio Editing group changes.
    - AudioFilterEngine.usePreference: whether an installed ffmpeg, when TextMouth finds one, runs the audio editor's filters, and writes MP3 should the LAME built in fail to start, under useInstalledFFmpeg, on until turned off, which the same group changes. SettingsViewModel mirrors the Record window's keys (RecordingPreferences.keys) and audio editing's (AudioEditingPreferences.keys) in config.json.
    - AudioEditorPreferences: whether the editor's Apply Filter previews a filter before applying it, and whether the editor plays half a second at the cursor after each move, under audioEditorPreviewsFilters and audioEditorPlaysAfterMoves, both on until turned off, which the same group changes; they are among AudioEditingPreferences.keys, so config.json mirrors them.
    - DiagnosticLog.TraceSwitch: the hidden trace-logging switch, diagnosticTraceLogging.
    - The log writer, through LogLocation.preference: where the logs are written, logLocation ("dataDirectory" or "machine"), which it reads for every line. Storage settings change it through SettingsViewModel.logLocation, and config.json mirrors it.
    - Export, through BookExportPreferences.showInReaderToolbar: whether reader toolbars show an Export button, under showExportInReaderToolbar. Playback settings change it through SettingsViewModel.showExportInReaderToolbar, as Storage settings change logLocation, and config.json mirrors it.
    - BookTextEditor: whether Edit Text has explained itself on this Mac, under editTextExplained, which stays out of config.json.
    - The library model: whether this Mac has made the one-time move of finished books to their ends, under library.finishedBooksAtEndVersion (LibraryViewModel.finishedBooksAtEndKey, P07), which stays out of config.json too (ConfigService.AppConfig.Portability.localStoreKeys, since P08).
    - FeedSettings: the seven feed settings (feedRefreshAtLaunch, feedRefreshIntervalHours, the List and download choice under feedItems and feedNewestCount, read together as itemPolicy, feedDeleteDownloadsDaysAfterPlayed, feedRefreshAlert, and feedNotifications), which the feed engine reads, the Feeds tab of Settings (FeedSettingsView) changes through SettingsViewModel.feedSettings, and config.json mirrors. The two settings the choice retired, feedKeepEntries and feedAutoDownload, stay in the store as they were, read only by the move to the choice (FeedSettings.moveToItemPolicyIfNeeded(in:); the List and download choice, under Feeds, below), and listed as retired (FeedSettings.retiredPreferenceKeys, in the config.json mirror's retiredStoreKeys). FeedItemPolicyControls (Views/Feeds/FeedItemPolicyControls.swift) draws the List and download choice here and in Feed Info: a menu-style Picker labelled "List and download", whose titles and hints come from FeedWording.itemPolicyTitle and itemPolicyHint, with the feed's ItemNoun in Feed Info, and its number through FeedNumberField, the field and stepper every feed number shares, disabled while the choice downloads by hand. FeedThresholdControl draws the two that can be off, refreshing only at launch and when asked and never deleting a played download, the second in Feed Info too, each a FeedNumberField with a checkbox for its off value.
    - TranscriptionLanguage: the language transcription uses, under transcriptionLocale (TranscriptionLanguage.preference), which reads a value that can't be a language identifier, or none, as this Mac's English. Playback settings' Transcription group (TranscriptionSettingsSection) changes it through SettingsViewModel.transcriptionLanguage, and config.json mirrors it.
    - TranscriptionPreferences.offerAfterImport: whether an import offers to transcribe each audiobook it adds, under offerTranscriptionAfterImport, off until chosen, which the same group changes through SettingsViewModel.offerTranscriptionAfterImport; config.json mirrors it.
  - Who may write it: each preference's owner, through its StoredPreference definition (the key, how the value is read, and how it is written, in one place) or its @AppStorage property. SettingsViewModel also applies config.json's values, using the services' own definitions for theirs.
  - How others observe it: SettingsViewModel publishes its own changes and increments a revision that open playback bars watch. PreferenceObserver (key-value observing) tells SettingsViewModel when a service-owned key changes, so config.json follows; a change of logLocation, of showExportInReaderToolbar, of a feed setting, or of a transcription setting is also announced, so the controls showing it, and the readers' toolbars, follow it. SpeechService reads the speed, pitch, and voice from its store through SettingsPolicy.
  - Files: Models/StoredPreference.swift, Models/LogLocation.swift, Models/TranscriptionLanguage.swift, Models/TranscriptionPreferences.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptionSettingsSection.swift, Models/AudioOriginalPolicy.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditingPreferences.swift, Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditorPreferences.swift, Services/AudioEditing/FFmpeg.swift, Models/FeedSettings.swift, Models/FeedItemPolicy.swift, Views/Feeds/FeedSettingsView.swift, Views/Feeds/FeedItemPolicyControls.swift, Views/Feeds/FeedThresholdControl.swift, Services/RecordingInputDevices.swift, Services/RecordingPreferences.swift, ViewModels/BookExportModel.swift, Services/BookTextEditor.swift, ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift, Services/SoundEffectService.swift, Services/TextSubstitutionService.swift, Services/KeyboardShortcutManager.swift, Services/SleepTimerService.swift, Services/DataDirectoryAvailability.swift, Services/LibrarySessionLease.swift, App/TextMouthRuntime.swift.
- The config.json mirror.
  - Owner: ConfigService.shared, on the main actor. Its config property holds the file's values. While TextMouth runs, the preferences store is the source of truth and config.json follows it; at launch, the file is applied first, because it can change while TextMouth is closed.
  - Who may write it: SettingsViewModel, after each settings change (syncToConfig) and when reconciling at launch, and the Storage settings for the three choices only the file holds. A change updates ConfigService.config at once, and scheduleSave() writes the file once changes pause for 250 ms (defaultSaveDelay), so a slider dragged through its steps writes it once; flushScheduledSave() writes a waiting save when TextMouth quits (UnsavedChangesTerminationGuard.flushPendingWrites()) and before the data directory moves, and in the hosted tests as each test ends (ConfigSaveFlush). ConfigService.saveToDisk, which writes at once, first merges any outside edit not yet handled, writes nothing when the file already holds the same bytes, and reports a failed save to PersistenceFailureCenter. Every read leaves dataless files in the cloud: a config.json only in the cloud is neither applied at launch nor written over, with one line in the debug log, until a save finds it on this Mac and merges it as an outside edit. While the data directory is unavailable, ConfigService neither reads, writes, nor watches the file; dataDirectoryBecameAvailable then merges it as an outside edit, keeping each setting changed meanwhile, saves it, and watches it again.
  - How others observe it: a file-system watcher notices saved edits, tells them from TextMouth's own saves by their bytes, and publishes each once through externalEdits, which SettingsViewModel.applyConfigChanges applies. A changed dataDirectoryPath posts dataDirectoryChangeRequested, and LibraryViewModel moves the data directory, unless DataDirectoryRequest finds the folder missing on this Mac or already holding a TextMouth library, as another Mac's path in a library in the cloud would: that edit is ignored with one CONFIG_FILE line.
  - Files: Services/ConfigService.swift, ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift, Models/LenientFields.swift, App/UnsavedChangesTermination.swift.
- The policy table.
  - Owner: SettingsPolicy, the single table of validated preferences. Each rule (NumericSettingRule, EnumSettingRule, KeyedSettingRule, ThresholdSettingRule, WholeNumberSettingRule, or CharacterSetSettingRule) states a key, the supported range, cases, or characters, the default, and, for speed and pitch, the value meaning "not chosen yet", or, for a threshold, the value at or below which it is off: -1 for the repeated-symbol threshold, for the feeds' days, and for their retired kept entries, which only the move to the List and download choice reads, and 0 for the feeds' refresh interval. A WholeNumberSettingRule has no off value and moves anything outside its range to the nearest bound, as the List and download choice's number does (1 to 2,000).
  - Who may write it: no one while the app runs; it is a static table whose ranges match the Settings controls.
  - How others observe it: decoding config.json (LenientFields), applying it, reading @AppStorage values, services reading the store directly, and decoding per-book speed and pitch from bookInfo.json and library.json all pass values through it. ConfigService.AppConfig.Portability lists which preferences config.json mirrors, which only it holds, which stay on this Mac, and which an earlier release wrote and none writes now (retiredStoreKeys: customKeyboardBindings, and FeedSettings.retiredPreferenceKeys, feedKeepEntries and feedAutoDownload); the tests take their expectations from those lists.
  - Files: Models/SettingsPolicy.swift, Models/LenientFields.swift, Services/ConfigService.swift.
- Keyboard shortcuts (FR12): the record of the shortcuts the user edited, every action's keys in force, and the double-press time.
  - Owner: KeyboardShortcutManager (Services/KeyboardShortcutManager.swift), the one the app makes and every window shares. Its edits, a ShortcutEdits (Models/ShortcutEdits.swift), hold each action the user edited, with its keys, and nothing else: an action is edited exactly while the record holds it, even with its default keys, and an edited action without a key holds an empty list. The preferences store keeps the record under keyboardShortcutEdits (editsPreference) as JSON keyed by the actions' names, the form the whole map had, an action working in a scope the user chose holding an object, {"keys": [...], "worksIn": "<the ShortcutScope's raw value>"}, written with sorted keys so one record is always the same bytes (storedData), and read entry by entry (ShortcutEdits.reading(_:)), so an entry naming no action this build knows, or whose keys can't be read, is left out with a STORED_VALUE_REPAIRED line while the rest load, and an entry naming a scope this build doesn't know, or one its action can't be given, keeps its keys at its default scope with such a line. Each action works in a scope (ShortcutScope: .app, every window but an audio editor's; .library; .reader; .audioEditor; .recording, the Record window), its defaultScope its group's or one of its own (ShortcutAction.defaultScopesOfTheirOwn: New from Text… and the Library menu's Import DAISY Folder… and Add Feed… in Library windows, Show Sleep Timer and the Playback menu's Copy Reading Position and Search in Book in reader windows), and the user may change it among availableScopes, which offer .app, .library, and .reader to the app's actions and the File menu's commands and fix every other's. The record keeps a chosen scope beside the action's keys only when it is not the default (scopesByEditedAction, setScope(_:for:keys:), cleared by Reset to Default). The Record window's buttons are the group ShortcutAction.recordingWindowCategory, "New Recording", the window's own title (RecordingWording.windowTitle), which begins their names, as in "New Recording: Stop", where the group was "Recording Window" before REV11. The menu commands' keys, and the Record window's buttons', are actions too (ShortcutAction.keyEquivalentActions): each is one press with Command or Control that no text field edits with (KeyBinding.canBeKeyEquivalent, TextEditingKeys), and claims its double press as well (KeyClaims.forms(of:for:)). ShortcutAction.refusal(ofKeys:holding:), the one refusal rule since REV11, refuses, among the keys a change adds to those the action holds, any other key for one of them and a key past the one its item may have, or past as many as it holds, and, for any action, a key in KeyBinding.systemCommands that is not the action's own default, with a ShortcutRefusal naming the first key at fault, and never a key the action holds already: the manager passes the action's keys in force (change(_:to:)), and config.json none (ShortcutListing.refusal(for:)), asking it only of the entries the record doesn't hold (ShortcutEdits.entriesLeftOut(of:)). Its bindings, every action's keys in force, are ShortcutEdits.effectiveBindings(defaults:) over this build's ShortcutAction.defaultBindings, a static let built once, as audioEditorDefaultBindings is: the edited actions claim their keys first, then every other action takes its defaults less any key that clashes with one already claimed in a kind of window where its keys act (KeyClaims, by ShortcutWindowKind), each group in ShortcutAction.allCases order, which since FR12's branch B is the order the Shortcuts tab lists the actions in, each group whole (#1528, pinned by ShortcutsSettingsTabTests), so no key is in force for two actions in one kind of window, and a default a later build adds or changes reaches every action the user never edited and none the user did. ShortcutScope.windowKinds(of:) gives the kinds of window where an action's keys act: its scope's, and, for a key equivalent, every kind its menu item acts in (admitsMenuCommand(in:)), an audio editor's included for a File menu command working in every window, so no editor action holds a key such a command answers there (REV11). ShortcutAction.sharesAWindow(in:with:in:) is the clash rule's overlap (ShortcutEdits.key(_:of:clashesWith:of:), and the move from the saved map), which KeyClaims, ShortcutPressDispatcher.Index, and the tests' ShortcutKeyOwners (keysOnTwoActions(in:scopes:) and actions(holding:in:sharingAWindowWith:scopes:), which took the place of KeyboardShortcutManager.action(holding:in:), used by tests alone) follow too. A KeyBinding can be a double press (isDoublePress, FR12's branch B): its stroke, the key with its modifiers, pressed twice within the double-press time. A key's single press and its double press are two keys, which two actions may hold, unless one of them is a key equivalent's, whose key claims both. KeyBinding's Codable is written by hand, so a binding saved without the flag, as every binding before it was, reads as a single press, and a single press is saved exactly as before, with no flag. The double-press time is SettingsPolicy.shortcutDoublePressSeconds (0.2 to 2 seconds, 0.5 by default), one time for every shortcut: the manager reads and writes it in tenths of a second (doublePressTimePreference, through SettingsPolicy.validatedDoublePressSeconds(_:)), correcting a stored value out of range or between tenths silently, with no log line, and publishes it as doublePressTime.
  - Who may write it: the manager, every change going through the record and saved at once. updateBindings(for:to:), addBinding(_:to:), and removeBinding(_:from:) make an action the user's through ShortcutEdits.setKeys(_:for:), which takes each of its keys from every other edited action that shares a kind of window with it (sharesAWindow(in:with:in:)), those keeping their other keys and staying edited; keys that leave an unedited action as it is change nothing, and a key the action can't take changes nothing and comes back as a ShortcutRefusal (ShortcutAction.refusal(ofKeys:holding:)). setScope(_:for:), from the tab's Works in, makes the action edited at its keys in force in the scope chosen, taking them from the actions whose scopes now overlap. resetToDefault(_:) takes one action out of the record (Reset to Default), and resetToDefaults() empties it (Reset to Defaults). The tab's Reset to Defaults asks first, then KeyboardShortcutsSettingsView.resetEveryShortcut(_:announce:) resets and announces ShortcutsSettingsWording.resetDone once the alert has closed (VoiceOverAnnouncer.announceAfterSheetCloses); the Sound Effects tab's, which asks nothing, runs SoundEffectsSettingsView.resetEverySound(_:announce:), which announces SoundEffectsSettingsWording.resetDone at once. At the first launch whose store holds no record, SettingsViewModel's applyFileAtLaunch calls moveSavedShortcutsIfNeeded(in:fileListings:) before it applies config.json. It reads, once, the whole map every earlier release saved under customKeyboardBindings, with config.json's lists in the old form, and makes the record (ShortcutEdits.migrating): an action takes the file's keys where they differ from the map's, an edit made while TextMouth was closed; an action whose keys are its default, today's or a former one (ShortcutAction.formerDefaultBindings: Move Book Up's and Move Book Down's Control-Option arrows before March 22, 2026, and Next Bookmark's Shift-Command-B before March 24), whole or less the keys other actions whose default scopes overlap its own hold, counts as unedited; and a key two edited actions then hold goes to the one the file edited, or, where the file edited both or neither, to the first in allCases order. It logs SHORTCUT_EDITS_MOVED with the actions edited, writes the record, and returns true, so applyFileAtLaunch leaves the file's shortcuts out of what that launch applies and the launch's own save writes config.json in the new form; nothing writes customKeyboardBindings again, so it stays as a copy to go back to. A config.json already in the new form, holding an entry that follows the defaults, moves nothing: an empty record is written and the file is applied over it. config.json's entries (SettingsPolicy.keyboardShortcuts, whose KeyedSettingRule reads an action's name in any letter case through ShortcutAction.named(_:), the exact spelling winning over another, and a name ending in an ellipsis read with three periods or none; a former name, as the Record window's buttons' "Recording Window: …" or the bookmark command's "Add Bookmark" (its case is still addBookmark, its name Toggle Bookmark), is read as its action's, here and in the stored record (ShortcutAction.formerNames, through named(_:) and exactlyNamed(_:), which ShortcutEdits.reading(_:) uses), and only the names in force are written) are ShortcutListing values (Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift): a list, an edited action's keys; an object (.object(keys:worksIn:)), an edited action's keys and the scope it works in, written with ShortcutScope.title and read by ShortcutScope.named(_:), its field names in any letter case (ShortcutJSONKey), and "keys" also as one shortcut written as text; or text beginning with "default", an action following the defaults, whose other words are never read, naming a Library or reader default after it ("default in reader windows: …", ShortcutScope.isSaidAsADefault); any other text is one shortcut. Each shortcut is written as KeyBinding.configText and read back by KeyBinding(configText:), which takes "twice" after a shortcut, or "double" before it, in any letter case and with or without spaces, as a double press, and a key by its name in KeyBinding.keyNames, such as pageDown or f5, or by its own character. applyConfigChanges puts them in place through KeyboardShortcutManager.adopt(_:in:) (ShortcutEdits.adopting(_:)): a list makes its action the user's in its default scope, an object in the scope it names, and "default" takes it out of the record, keys and scope; an entry ShortcutEdits.entriesLeftOut(of:) names (a scope its action can't have, or keys it refuses) is left out with a STORED_VALUE_REPAIRED line and adopt(_:in:) returns true; and a key two edited actions then hold goes to the one whose entry changed the record, or, where both or neither did, to the first in allCases order (resolvingSharedKeys(favoring:)), with a STORED_VALUE_REPAIRED line naming the key and both actions; after either, applyConfigChanges saves config.json again (syncToConfig), so the file shows the keys in force. The double-press time is set by the Shortcuts tab's stepper (setDoublePressTime(_:)) and by config.json's shortcutDoublePressSeconds, which ConfigService reads moved into range and to the nearest tenth, logging the repair, and applyConfigChanges adopts through doublePressTimePreference, a file without it leaving the time alone; a change of either in the store reaches the manager through its PreferenceObserver.
  - How others observe it: the manager publishes once for each change of the record (take(_:)), a change another writer makes in the store included, such as applying config.json (PreferenceObserver), and once for each change of the double-press time, and key presses go by both at once (key presses, under Windows, below). syncToConfig lists every action in config.json (ShortcutEdits.listings(defaults:)): an edited action as the list of its keys in force, or, when it works in a scope the user chose, an object of them and the scope's ShortcutScope.title, and every other as "default: " and its keys in force, or "default: none", with "default in Library windows: " or "default in reader windows: " where ShortcutScope.isSaidAsADefault; it writes shortcutDoublePressSeconds beside them. The Shortcuts tab (KeyboardShortcutsSettingsView and ShortcutBindingRow, in Views/Settings/KeyboardShortcutsSettingsView.swift) shows "Edited" and Reset to Default on the row of an action isEdited(_:) names, Reset to Default as a button and as an accessibility action of the row, and its words are ShortcutsSettingsWording's, pinned by ShortcutsSettingsTabTests: the row's VoiceOver label (rowLabel(for:keys:isEdited:defaultsHeldElsewhere:worksIn:), which says where the action works when that is Library or reader windows or other than its default, and, for an unedited action that goes without a default key, which action has it, ShortcutsSettingsWording.defaultsHeldElsewhere(_:), from the manager's defaultKeysHeldElsewhere(for:), as the row shows it too); its keys in words through KeyBinding.accessibleDisplayString, from the one table of named keys, KeyBinding.keyNames (KeyName: the key as a binding holds it, its words, what the screen shows, config.json's name, and the key code of a key matched by its code), which holds the nine keys matched by their codes (Space, Tab, Return, Delete, Escape, and the arrows), every AppKit function key, held as its private-use character but named in words (Forward Delete, Home, End, Page Up, Page Down, the F keys, and the rest), the keypad's Enter, and the punctuation marks, named in words and shown as themselves, any other key being its character, a letter in capitals, and a double press ending in " twice"; the announcement of a key recorded, with every action that lost a key to it (actions(thatLostKeysSince:besides:)); and that of a reset, with the keys the action has then and, when the reset changed it, where it works (resetAnnouncement(for:keys:worksIn:)). ShortcutBindingRow adds a Works in Picker on each row whose action has more than one scope available, bound to the manager's scope(of:) and setScope(_:for:) and announced by scopeChanged(_:to:takenFrom:). A key from the private-use area that no name says can no longer be recorded or read (KeyBinding.isUnnamedPrivateUseCharacter(_:)). The tab opens with DoublePressTimeStepper (setDoublePressTime(_:), its value spoken through CountWording.secondsToTheTenth(_:), as the Miscellaneous tab's gesture time window is) and Try a Double Press (tryDoublePress(report:), over a DoublePressTrial, in Services/ShortcutPressDispatcher.swift, which takes the next two presses for five seconds as no shortcuts and reports a DoublePressTrial.Outcome). A key's button records through ShortcutRecorderEventHandler, whose local key monitor hands each press to a ShortcutRecording: a single press once the double-press time passes, a second press of the same key within it as a double press, another key ending the wait with the first recorded as a single press, a held key's repeats ignored, and Escape cancelling; for a key equivalent's row it takes the first press at once (takesDoublePresses false). DoublePressTrial and ShortcutRecording measure two presses through one TwoPresses, and Try a Double Press keeps its last outcome as text under the button (trialStatus, from the manager's lastDoublePressTrialOutcome, kept until TextMouth quits). recordingStarted(by:) and recordingStopped(by:) tell the manager, which holds the recorders weakly and acts on no key while one records. Make Double Press and Make Single Press (ShortcutsSettingsWording.pressChange(for:)) are each key button's accessibility action and context menu item, but a key equivalent's, whose row holds one key. A key recorded is announced through ShortcutsSettingsWording.recorded(_:for:takenFrom:nowWaitsBefore:), which adds nowWaits(_:before:) when the manager's waitingAction(for:in:) newly names an action the key's single press waits before. An audio editor window's model reads the keys in force for its buttons' hints through bindingsPreference, a read-only StoredShortcutBindings over the record's key, says them in the same words (AudioEditorWording.shortcutSentence(_:)), and redraws as the record changes. The Record window does the same for its buttons (RecordingWindowView, through StoredKeyEquivalents): each hint ends with the button's key in force, in those words (RecordingWording.recordHint(device:key:), pauseHint(key:), resumeHint(key:), and stopHint(key:)), or with nothing while the button has no key, where it once named the default keys whatever was set. The Bookmarks menu (App/MenuCommands.swift) titles each of its items from its ShortcutAction raw value, so the menu, the Shortcuts tab, and config.json cannot name a command differently.
  - Files: Models/ShortcutEdits.swift, Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift, Services/KeyboardShortcutManager.swift, Services/ShortcutPressDispatcher.swift, Models/SettingsPolicy.swift, Services/ConfigService.swift, ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift, Views/Settings/KeyboardShortcutsSettingsView.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWindowView.swift, ViewModels/AudioEditorModel.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorWording.swift.

### The reader

- A reader's state: its content, cursor (chapter and character offset), headings, bookmarks, playback flags, and errors.
  - Owner: one ReaderViewModel per reader window, on the main actor, created by BookReaderWindowView with a copy of the library's Book.
  - Who may write it: the reader itself, in its extensions: Playback, Navigation, Bookmarks, Jump, VoiceAndSpeed, WhereAmI, LibraryUpdates, CloudDownload, EditedText, Transcript, ReadingList, and SpeechRefusal.
  - How others observe it: ReaderView and its subviews observe the reader, refreshed at most every 0.12 seconds during playback. ReaderView's body only puts its parts together, since the compiler type-checks a body as one expression: the views ReaderLocationHeader, ReaderTextArea, and ReaderLoadFailureView, the toolbar's items, and the modifiers ReaderErrorAlert, ReaderPanelSheets, and ReaderPanelCommands, which share one ReaderPanels, the panels showing; each is type-checked on its own. ReaderTextArea puts its label, ReaderTextArea.accessibilityLabel ("Book content"), on its ScrollView, never on the Text: a label on a SwiftUI Text replaces the text's accessibility value, so a playing book's text read "Book content", and a paused one's, selectable, held the book's characters under that 12-character value, of which VoiceOver read twelve (A11). The Text shows ReaderViewModel.displayVisibleTextWindow, which VisibleTextWindowPolicy.bounds(in:around:maximumLength:) (ReaderViewModel.swift) cuts, at most ReaderViewModel.maxDisplayChars (20,000 UTF-16 units), around the reading position, which it always holds: each cut edge moves inward at most a quarter of the window, to a paragraph break, else a line break, else stays where it falls between words, else to a space; text with no space there is cut between whole characters. BookSettingsSheetWording (Views/Reader/ReaderView.swift) names a book's own settings sheet (BookVoicePickerView) and the toolbar button that opens it: a text book's Voice ("Change voice for this book" to VoiceOver) and Voice & Speed; an audiobook's Audio Settings, the same on screen and to VoiceOver; and audioSpeedLabel, for the audiobook sheet's one speed control, a slider. The Headings, Voice, and Bookmarks sheets observe a ReaderSnapshot of what they show instead, which reads the reader on the main queue's turn after each change and republishes only a value that differs, and, as SheetOverOneReader views, compare equal while they show the same reader, so a playing reader's publishes redraw none of them; when playback crosses into another heading, two of the Headings sheet's rows redraw. SleepTimerView compares its reader and its auto-close setting. HeadingNavigatorView labels its list's scroll area (listLabel(headingCount:)), since a label on the whole sheet renamed its pickers, and its level filter is one segmented picker labelled Show levels (levelFilterLabel, its choices filterChoiceTitle(maximumLevel:)); each heading's row offers Go To and Skip or Include Section as VoiceOver actions and in its context menu alike. A bookmark's row shows and speaks the day it was added (Bookmark.dateText, the end of its label), and the empty Bookmarks list names the keys in force for adding one (BookmarkPanelView.emptyListHelp(keys:)). A book's places are named by times on one clock (P08): ReaderViewModel.placeClock, a PlaceClock (Models/PlaceClock.swift) built from the content index on each use, recording(of:) for an audiobook, its recording's own time whatever the speed, each chapter as long as its index records it (one file's placeholders summed, as AudioLayout.chapterStartTime plays them; a file per chapter, each to the tenth of a second; a DAISY section, its clips' measured lengths), and reading(of:readingTime:) for a text book, its words at the reader's words per minute, as its times always counted. The status row's percent and time left (BookInfoBar) open Go to Percent and Go to Time (Views/Reader/ReaderJumpPanels.swift), each a JumpFieldPanel, one plain text field whose name is shown for the eye and set as its own label and hint, with Jump and Cancel: JumpToPercentPanel's field, "Percent through the book", goes through goToPercent(typed:), and JumpToTimePanel's, "Time in the recording" or "Time at 500 words a minute" (ReaderTimeWording.timeFieldLabel(_:), from timeCount), through goToTime(typed:), which reads the time with JumpInputValidator.time(_:length:count:) and TimeEntry, refusing one past the clock's length by more than lengthTolerance, and jumps through jumpToTime(seconds:) to PlaceClock.place(forTypedTime:headings:), which takes a heading starting less than a second after the time, the earliest of several. Its field starts at goToTimeStartingText, the place's time in whole seconds. JumpFieldPanel keeps the text its field opened with and takes Jump and Return through JumpFieldOutcome.taking(_:startedAt:submit:): that text unchanged, spaces aside, is .unchanged and closes the panel through onCancel without calling submit; anything else is .jumped or .refused(String). ReaderViewModel.goToPercentStartingText sits beside goToTimeStartingText. The Headings sheet's rows (HeadingNavigatorView.headingItems) give Time In on the same clock and Duration heard at the book's speed, PlaceClock.span(from:to:) from the heading to the next heading shown, or to PlaceClock.end after the last, and Copy Reading Position adds the place's time on it (ReaderTimeWording.copiedPlace(_:of:_:)). The rows follow a speed change made while the sheet is open: HeadingNavigatorView.RowTiming, the time count, the speed an audiobook's lengths are heard at, and the speed the setting names, is in the sheet's ReaderState, and the sheet rebuilds its rows when it changes, with a listener of its own on the settings model (objectWillChange), which rebuilds only if the timing really changed. One file's chapter marks are not on the clock as marked: the content index keeps no mark's own start, so each chapter there starts at the sum of the placeholders before it, up to a tenth of a second per chapter before its mark, which the Headings sheet's times and Next Heading follow; mending it needs the parser to keep mark starts and chaptered books analyzed again. With SettingsViewModel.includeSpeedWithTimes on, namedListeningSpeed (a ReaderTimeWording.ListeningSpeed) names the speed beside the status row's time left and total, Where Am I?'s time left and total, Copy Reading Position's time left, and the Headings sheet's durations (shownSpeed(_:), spokenSpeed(_:)), an audiobook at 1x naming none. The Search in Book sheet (BookSearchView) lists at most BookSearchEngine.resultLimit, 500, matches, and keeps every result row alive in a VStack, as the headings and bookmarks panels do, so VoiceOver's cursor is not sent back up a long list; a kept row costs about half a millisecond and a tenth of a megabyte to draw. Find Duplicate Books' list (DuplicateGroupList) keeps every row of the page it shows the same way, and shows a scan's groups in pages of whole groups listing at most 40 books (DuplicateGroupPages): each book's native checkbox costs 4 to 8 ms and a quarter of a megabyte to draw, so an author scan of a 700-book library (688 books in 116 groups) took 4 to 5.4 seconds and 184 MB to show at once, and its first page 0.23 seconds and 10 MB (measured on October 10, 2026, in a Debug build, by an opt-in benchmark). The library sees the cursor through LibraryViewModel: the live position about once a second (updateLiveReadingPosition, into live reading positions), and a durable checkpoint (persistReadingPosition) every 30 seconds of active playback (PlaybackCheckpointPolicy) and whenever the reader pauses, stops, closes, finishes a chapter, or navigates.
  - Files: ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift and the ReaderViewModel+ extensions, Views/Reader/.
- A reader's copy of its book.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.book, a copy of the library's row taken when the reader opens. Its library fields (Book.LibraryFields: where the book's files are, its editable metadata with the record of hand edits, and the content analysis fields) follow the library; the rest (the position, bookmarks, voice, speed, and other per-book settings) is the reader's own, and the reader writes each change of it through to the library.
  - Who may write it: the reader, for its own fields and for the metadata its editor saves through the library. Otherwise the library fields change only through LibraryViewModel.bookUpdates (LibraryBookUpdates). LibraryViewModel offers it every change of library, and whenever a book open in a reader has library fields other than those its readers were last given, they receive its row with the book's next revision; no change to the reader's own fields is published, so those never come back to it. A package's move on disk is bracketed: commitLibraryTransition and performLibraryTransition, and a folder rename that only respells the folder, call packagesWillMove before anything moves and packagesDidMove once the library holds the new rows or the move has been put back.
  - How others observe it: ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates applies only updates newer than the last it applied, without reloading the text or interrupting speech. A new path points the audio at the files where they are now, and new metadata reaches the metadata editor's starting values and Now Playing; the window title, Where Am I?, and announcements read the copy itself. Audio that is playing when a move is announced stops and releases its file, then plays on from the same place once the move ends; paused audio loads again when it resumes: it is marked restartPlaybackBackendOnNextResume, as a change of the sound output marks a paused book (invalidatePlaybackBackendForAudioRouteChange()), and its next resume, Play's (resumeAudioPlayback(), resumeSpeechPlayback()) or a Where Am I? announcement's, plays it afresh from the cursor through restartPlaybackAtCursor(), which clears the mark: an audiobook through restartAudioAtCursor(volume:), at its intended volume, and a text book through speakCurrentChapter(), always in direct speech. Meanwhile the audio set aside (audioPausedForMove) counts as playing (isPlaybackUnderWay), so playUnlessUnderWay() leaves it to the move's end and the Now Playing session reports it playing; pause(playingPauseSound:) and stopPlaybackForSleepTimer(waitingAt:characterOffset:) let it go with the other waits to play (stopAudioWaitingToPlay(because:)), and stop() lets it go (letGoOfAudioSetAsideForMove(because:)) and counts it as active, so a pause or stop made during an off-main move, as by Play/Pause, the AirPods, a recording or another book starting, or Release Media Controls, leaves the book as the listener left it when the move ends. resumeAudioAfterPackageMove() holds the audio as isChapterHeldByWhereAmI, at the volume it was set aside at, while Where Am I? speaks. Audio playing from the book's working copy (below) reads none of the package's files, so it plays on undisturbed. A load that fails because the book moved meanwhile starts again from the new path. LibraryBookUpdates.textReplaced carries a text edit the library took, which the reader shows in place of its text (edited text, above), and transcriptReplaced an audiobook's transcript text, saved, edited, or deleted, which the reader shows in place of the text it had as the book plays on (ReaderViewModel+Transcript; transcripts, above).
  - Files: ViewModels/LibraryBookUpdates.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+BookOperations.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+FolderManagement.swift, Models/Book.swift.
- A closed reader: what a reader keeps after its window closes.
  - Owner: the reader itself. SwiftUI keeps a closed reader window, and with it the window's ReaderViewModel, for the rest of the session, as AppKit keeps every closed window that was ever key, so a closed reader must not hold what grows with its book.
  - Who may write it: close(), once. After it ends the reader's cloud work, stops playback, saves the position, and releases its sessions, it lets go of everything that grows with the book (releaseWhatGrowsWithTheBook): its subscriptions, the look-ahead, the text speech was speaking and the rendered audio of the chapter last played (SpeechService.releaseSpeech(), which makes no engine to do it), the offset maps, the text window shown, an audiobook's lists of files and clips, and the book's content with its headings; an audiobook's player and item went with stop(), which also empties the prepared speech. A background preparation that finishes afterwards keeps nothing. What is left is the reader and its services, whose size does not depend on the book.
  - How others observe it: nothing reads a closed reader; ViewsAndWindowsClosedWindowTests checks that it keeps nothing that grows with its book.
  - Files: ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, Services/SpeechService.swift, Views/Reader/BookReaderWindowView.swift.
- Getting a book from the cloud: a reader's download, in a library in a cloud location.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.cloudPhase (a ReaderCloudPhase: downloading, not downloaded after a cancel, failed, or waiting for sync), which ReaderView shows in place of the book (ReaderCloudPhaseView), and ReaderViewModel.cloudSession (a ReaderCloudSession): where the library was found, the download under way and what it is for, its announcements, and the working copy.
  - Who may write it: ReaderViewModel+CloudDownload. loadContent reads where the library lives and, in a cloud location, the book's state, off the main thread; outside one it reads the book in the same step, as it always has. In a cloud location continueOpening loads a book that is on this Mac, downloads one with files in the cloud first (startDownload, iterating BookCloudStorage.download), or stops at waiting for sync. loadDownloadedBook reads the book inside DatalessFiles.withoutDownloading once LocalBookFiles.requireOnThisMac finds every file here; a read refused because a file went back to the cloud goes through the download once more, and a second refusal stops with the reason. For an audiobook without a working copy, chapterFilesReadyToPlay downloads the book before a chapter whose file is only in the cloud plays, the reader showing Playing meanwhile, and a failure stops at that chapter with an alert offering Try Again. Play/Pause pauses a reader waiting so (isWaitingForChapterDownload). A pause, whether from Play/Pause or a sleep timer that runs out, stops the waiting (stopAudioWaitingToPlay(because:), through stopWaitingForChapterDownload), so the chapter never starts by itself afterwards; a move of the book's package stops it too, but sets the audio aside (audioPausedForMove), so the chapter plays, or downloads again, from the package's new place once the move ends. Cancel, Download, Try Again, and closing the reader end or restart the work.
  - How others observe it: the download's progress is recorded in BookCloudStates.shared between beginDownload and endDownload, so the library list shows it too, and after a cancel, a pause, or a close the state found afterwards is recorded. A part of the one file left that the sync service publishes gives the bar its percentage, and with it the milestones: VoiceOver hears the download start, 25, 50, and 75 percent, or the time waited every 30 seconds while there is no percentage, and the end. ReaderCloudWording words everything the view shows and speaks.
  - Files: ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+CloudDownload.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, Views/Reader/ReaderView.swift, Services/ReaderWorkingCopy.swift.
- The working copy: the files an open audiobook in a library in a cloud location plays.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.cloudSession.workingCopy, a ReaderWorkingCopy: a copy of the book's package in a folder of its own (the book's ID and a UUID) in MachineLocalPaths.workingCopies, made by ReaderWorkingCopies.shared.
  - Who may write it: loadDownloadedBook makes it, off the main thread, once the book is on this Mac: FileManager.copyItem, which clones the package on one APFS volume, one copy at a time under a lock, the session's first copy sweeping away any a crash left. A package on another volume, a failed clone, or a clone holding a file only in the cloud gets no copy (WORKING_COPY_SKIPPED), and the book plays from its package with each chapter checked first. A move of the book's files calls sourceMoved(to:), through followMovedFiles, and restarts nothing. Closing the reader deletes the copy off the main thread.
  - How others observe it: playback reads the copy through playbackURL(for:), which maps a path in the package to the same place in the copy, where each chapter's files are chosen (chapterAudioSource(forChapterAt:) and audioClipChain(forChapterAt:)); the audiobook's tags are read from it too. pauseAudioForPackageMove leaves audio playing from it alone. Positions, bookmarks, and settings still save to the package through the library.
  - Files: Services/ReaderWorkingCopy.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+CloudDownload.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+LibraryUpdates.swift.
- The playback generation.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.chapterPlaybackGeneration, and the ChapterPlay token of each play: the chapter it plays and the generation it began in.
  - Who may write it: beginChapterPlay(), which every start of a chapter's speech or audio calls (Play, a resume that starts the chapter again, a chapter advance, or a loop), increases it and returns the new play's token; a move to another chapter, by navigation or a skip, and a loop increase it as well.
  - How others observe it: each play hands its speech or audio service a completion callback made for that play (listenForSpeechEnd, listenForAudioEnd). The end the service reports moves the reader on from the chapter that played only while that play's generation is still current (endsPlayUnderWay); the end of a play the reader has left, or one reported after the reader closed, is logged as one trace line and ignored. Look-ahead renders are kept only within the generation they began in.
  - Files: ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Navigation.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift.
- Prepared speech: what the reader will say for each chapter.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.chapterSpeechCache, a ChapterSpeechCache, on the main actor: each prepared ChapterSpeech with the ChapterSpeechInputs it was prepared from (the chapter's text, compared byte for byte, its skipped sections, the repeated-symbol rule, the punctuation pause level, and the substitution pass), for the four chapters prepared last (ChapterSpeechCache.capacity), and the look-ahead's speech, carried until its chapter begins.
  - Who may write it: preparedSpeech(for:), through which the reader asks for a chapter's speech: speech kept for exactly the current inputs is used again, and anything else is prepared and kept, replacing the chapter's older speech. ChapterSpeechInputs.prepare() is pure, so the look-ahead prepares a chapter longer than SpeechRenderCoordinator.maximumTextLength off the main actor instead (prepareInBackground), never twice at once, and chooses again once it is ready; speech whose inputs changed meanwhile is not kept. Play's own chapter is prepared at once when nothing is kept for it, unless it is long and a background preparation of exactly its current inputs is under way: Play then claims playback, waits (awaitedByPlay, isWaitingForChapterSpeech) showing Playing, and finishPreparingInBackground starts it from the cursor (continueWaitingPlay), unless Where Am I? is speaking then: the play goes on waiting, showing Playing, until the announcement ends, by itself (whereAmIAnnouncementFinished, after the end sound) or cut short by Where Am I?, a skip, or a jump (startPlayHeldByWhereAmI); continueWaitingPlay() waits while isWhereAmISpeaking. Play/Pause, even during the announcement, Stop, the sleep timer's stop, and close end the wait, while a cancel of the announcement the reader did not ask for starts the waiting play, as the announcement's end would have (a Where Am I? announcement, below). A closed reader empties the cache as close() stops it, and keeps nothing a background preparation finishes afterwards. When a text book finishes loading without playing, showLoadedContent calls prepareOpenedChapterSpeech(), which prepares the chapter Play will start in (after a cover without words, the first chapter with words) off the main actor through prepareInBackground(_:forLookAhead: false); finishPreparingInBackground keeps the result only while its inputs are still current.
  - How others observe it: Play, each chapter's start, the look-ahead, and refreshLookAhead() read it, so a chapter is prepared once for as long as its inputs stay the same, and a speed or pitch step prepares nothing.
  - Files: ViewModels/ChapterSpeechCache.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift.
- The look-ahead and its render signature.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.lookAhead: the next speakable chapter's index, the SpeechRenderSignature its render was asked for (a hash and length of the exact spoken text, and the voice), and the AudioEngineService that holds the audio.
  - Who may write it: preRenderNextChapter(), after each chapter starts; refreshLookAhead(), whenever the voice, the substitution rules or their switch, the repeated-symbol settings, the punctuation pause level, or the skipped sections change (speed and pitch do not count, because they are applied as the audio plays, so applyLocalSpeechOverrides, which every playing reader runs at each step of a global setting, refreshes it only when the voice in effect is not the one the look-ahead renders in); and discardLookAhead(). The look-ahead carries its chapter's speech to that chapter's start (ChapterSpeechCache.lookAheadSpeech), so the chapter's end prepares nothing. Renders wait their turn on SpeechRenderCoordinator.shared, which never lets two calls of AVSpeechSynthesizer.write() overlap.
  - How others observe it: at a chapter's end, takeLookAheadEngine(for:) hands over the engine only when it holds finished audio whose renderedSignature equals the signature of the chapter's speech prepared now. Otherwise the chapter starts from its current text.
  - Files: ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+VoiceAndSpeed.swift, ViewModels/ChapterSpeechCache.swift, Services/AudioEngineService.swift, Services/SpeechService.swift.
- A Where Am I? announcement: the reader's summary of where it is, spoken in the app's own voice.
  - Owner: the reader: a synthesizer of its own, apart from the speech service's (whereAmISynth), the announcement it speaks (liveWhereAmIUtterance), which summary it says (whereAmIDetail, a SummaryDetail, brief or full, FR12's branch C), whether one speaks (isWhereAmISpeaking), whether it paused a playing book (wasPlayingBeforeWhereAmI), whether one that ended by itself still owes that book its resume, until its end sound has played (isWhereAmIResumePending), whether it holds a Play pressed during it (isPlayHeldByWhereAmI), for a book it did not pause or, since October 10, 2026, for the one it paused, so the Play survives a cancel, and whether it holds an audiobook's chapter whose files arrived, or whose package's move ended, during it (isChapterHeldByWhereAmI, which counts in isWaitingForChapterDownload). isWhereAmIUnderWay is any of the four: an announcement speaking, a resume owed, a Play held, or a chapter held. whereAmIWillPlayBook is whether Where Am I? will play the book once it is over: a book the announcement speaking paused, a resume owed, a Play held, or a chapter held.
  - Who may write it: ReaderViewModel+WhereAmI. whereAmI(_:) takes Where Am I? (.brief, W) and Where Am I? Full Summary (.full, W twice), which one handler in ReaderViewModel hears for both notifications, and does what SummaryPress.outcome(pressing:while:sameSubject:) (Models/SummaryPress.swift) decides: speak, after the start sound, pausing the book if it plays; stop, through cancelWhereAmIIfActive(), resuming the book it paused or starting the play it held, for a brief press while any summary speaks or a full press while the full one does; or replace the brief summary with the full one, on a fresh synthesizer (replaceWhereAmISynthesizer()), the book still paused and with no second start sound. The reader toolbar's ReaderWhereAmIToolbarButton (Views/Reader/ReaderView.swift) asks for the brief summary when pressed, and for the full one through its VoiceOver action, named ShortcutAction.whereAmIFull.title. No press depends on when the last one came: the three-second rule is retired. speakWhereAmI(_:) hands the guarded text to the synthesizer, or, in a test, to _whereAmIUtteranceRecorderForTesting. The reader's own stops, a pause (pause(playingPauseSound:)), a press while an announcement speaks, a command that interrupts it, a new announcement, and closing, go through stopWhereAmISynthesizer(), which forgets the announcement before it silences the synthesizer, so the cancel the synthesizer then reports changes nothing. An announcement that ends by itself (whereAmIAnnouncementFinished()) plays the end sound, makes the resume of the book it paused owed (isWhereAmIResumePending), and runs resumeAfterWhereAmIEndSound() after whereAmIEndSoundDelay, 0.3 seconds (afterWhereAmIEndSound(_:); tests take the work through _afterWhereAmIEndSoundForTesting and run it with a WhereAmIEndSoundClock), which resumes that book, unless whatever settled the book meanwhile took the resume, and starts a held Play. resumeBookPausedForWhereAmI() plays the book afresh through restartPlaybackAtCursor() when restartPlaybackBackendOnNextResume is set, as when AirPods connected or the library moved a paused audiobook's files during the announcement; otherwise it tells Now Playing that the book plays only when the backend's resume returns true, and settles the pause when it does not (WHERE_AM_I_RESUME_REJECTED); a resumed book lets a Play held for it go. While isWhereAmIUnderWay, play() and playOrResume() call holdPlayForWhereAmI(), which holds a Play (isPlayHeldByWhereAmI) for the book the speaking announcement paused, so the Play survives a cancel, or for a book the announcement will not play anyway and that is not playing or waiting to play; a chapter whose files arrive during an announcement waits for it too (isChapterHeldByWhereAmI, from downloadDidFinish), and so does audio set aside for a package move that ends during one (resumeAudioAfterPackageMove(), at the volume it was set aside at). startPlayHeldByWhereAmI() plays a held chapter first, through playAudioChapter(), letting a Play held beside it go with it, since a held chapter counts in isWhereAmIUnderWay and the Play would wait on it; otherwise it starts a held Play through playOrResume(), with the play sound. pauseBookForWhereAmI() takes an owed resume over, so Where Am I? pressed in the moment after an announcement ended keeps the book paused until the new one ends, and cancelWhereAmIIfActive() resumes an owed book at once, as a skip in that moment needs. navigateTo(chapterIndex:characterOffset:), every jump's path (Next and Previous Heading, a heading, bookmark, or search result picked from its list, Go to a percent or a time, Return to previous position, and an audiobook's jump into another chapter), ends an announcement first through cancelWhereAmIIfActive(), as the skips do, so the jump moves a book that plays, and it plays on from where the jump lands (REV12). silenceWhereAmI() silences an announcement speaking and records that none speaks, the one place cancelWhereAmIIfActive(), stopWhereAmIWithoutResuming(), and endWhereAmIAsTheBookStops() do it. stop(), which Release Media Controls, a chapter's download cancelled or failed, and closing the window go through, and a playback failure end an announcement with the book (endWhereAmIAsTheBookStops(): the synthesizer silenced, and wasPlayingBeforeWhereAmI, isWhereAmIResumePending, isPlayHeldByWhereAmI, and isChapterHeldByWhereAmI cleared), so the announcement's end neither resumes the stopped book nor tells Now Playing that it plays, which once took back the AirPods Release Media Controls had let go (REV11).
  - How others observe it: WhereAmIDelegateHelper hands each finish and each cancel to the main queue with its utterance (ReportedUtterance), which the reader compares with liveWhereAmIUtterance. A finish of the announcement speaking ends it with the end sound and then resumes the book it paused, or starts the play it held (whereAmIAnnouncementFinished()); a finish of one the reader stopped or replaced since is ignored. A cancel nobody asked for, as the engine's refusal of its text makes (WHERE_AM_I_CANCEL_UNEXPECTED; whereAmISynthesizerCancelled(_:)), ends it without the end sound or the resume, through endWhereAmIWithoutResuming(), so the book it paused stays paused unless a Play was pressed for it, and replaces whereAmISynth, since a synthesizer the engine refused never speaks again; it then calls startPlayHeldByWhereAmI(), as the announcement's end does, so a held Play, a held chapter, and a play waiting for its chapter's speech start, while a play waiting for its chapter's files goes on waiting, where a cancel once dropped a held Play (REV12) and paused a waiting play (REV13). endWhereAmIWithoutResuming() only records that no announcement speaks and settles the pause the announcement left, or the one a resume owed would have undone (settlePauseLeftByWhereAmI(): the listening session ended, the reader's sleep timer countdown held, the stop recorded for rewind on resume, and an audiobook shown paused), since the announcement paused the book without the rest of a pause, expecting to resume it; what else the announcement held is its caller's to settle. pause(playingPauseSound:) and togglePlayPause() end whatever Where Am I? holds of the book through stopWhereAmIWithoutResuming(), which silences an announcement speaking, calls endWhereAmIWithoutResuming(), and lets a held Play go, the book left as it was before the Play, so a pause made in the moment after an announcement ended takes the resume it owed and stands; each then pauses whatever is under way (isPlaybackUnderWay: a play waiting for its chapter's speech or files, a held chapter, or audio set aside for a package move), togglePlayPause() in silence, while a Pause that pauses something makes the pause sound; Next and Previous in Reading List (goThroughReadingList(_:)) end it through that pause, for a book that was not playing too, and count a book Where Am I? will play as playing. A Play from the AirPods or a media key reaches playOrResume(), so during an announcement it is held, as above, and starts the book as the announcement ends. The handler the reader gives its NowPlayingSession as isCurrentlyPlaying is whereAmIWillPlayBook || isPlaybackUnderWay, so a book Where Am I? will play, and a play waiting for its chapter's speech or files, count as playing: another book starting (prepareForPlayback(ownerID:)), a disconnect, or a recording starting pauses such a book, ending what Where Am I? holds without resuming it, and a waiting play never starts by itself once ready; over a book that was paused before, with no Play held, the announcement goes on. playUnlessUnderWay() plays as Play does unless a play is under way (isPlaybackUnderWay), for Auto-play on navigate (autoPlayIfEnabled(), after a heading, bookmark, or search result picked from its list) and a sleep timer set to start playback (startSleepTimer(duration:startPlaybackIfNeeded:)), both of which once went through Play/Pause, pausing a play waiting for its chapter and ending an announcement in place of playing. WhereAmIInterruptionPolicy, whose blocksBookPlayback(isAnnouncementSpeaking:) no longer decided anything, is gone. showEditedText(_:) restarts a book Where Am I? will play as it restarts a playing one. pauseAudioForPackageMove() leaves a chapter held for Where Am I? held, and resumeAudioAfterPackageMove() leaves it to the announcement while it is held; an announcement that ends during the move lets the chapter go in startPlayHeldByWhereAmI(), leaving it to the move's end (audioPausedForMove), which plays it from the package's new place. The summaries, briefWhereAmISummary and fullWhereAmISummary, and Copy Reading Position's readingPositionSummary say "Finished" in place of the percent and the times left while the cursor rests at its end (isFinished, from cursorRestsAtEnd, P07); the full summary still gives the book's whole time.

- The report of a speech refusal (B05): what a reader keeps of the speech engine's refusals while its window is open, and the sheet that shows it.
  - Owner: ReaderViewModel.speechRefusalAlert, a SpeechRefusalAlert (Models/SpeechRefusalReport.swift): the report its sheet shows, a SpeechRefusalReport, nil while none shows, and how many times each place (SpeechRefusalPlace: the chapter, and the character in its text as the book has it) was refused, kept while the window is open. A report is a value: the book's title and format; the position as the brief Where Am I? says it without the title; the chapter, counted from 0 as the logs count it, and its title; where speech stopped in the chapter's text as the book has it, and that text's length; what speech handed over (SpeechRefusal, below); two passages (SpeechRefusalPassage: at most 100 UTF-16 units before the stop and 400 from it, clamped to the text and never splitting a character), the chapter as the book has it and the refused text as it was handed to the engine; the system's version (ProcessInfo.processInfo.operatingSystemVersionString); the build (BuildIdentity.current); the time; and the count. SpeechRefusalReport.lines words every line, and SpeechRefusalWording every word of the sheet and of what VoiceOver hears, so a test pins each without a window.
  - Who may write it: ReaderViewModel+SpeechRefusal alone. Speech hands over what it refused: SpeechService.transitionLog keeps every line it writes, routine trace lines included, in recentTransitionLines (RecentTransitionLines, the newest 40, offsets and lengths only), so the report never reads a log file on the main thread, and endDirectSpeechRefused(at:refusing:spanning:) calls onSpeechRefused with a SpeechRefusal: where speech stopped, the refused utterance's range and its text exactly as handed to the engine, the strictly guarded range, the voice, rate, pitch, and volume it was handed over with, the speed in words per minute, and those lines, ending with DIRECT_RETRY_FAILED. showPlaybackStoppedAfterRefusal(_:) ends with showSpeechRefusalReport(_:), which first reads where the window stands (speechRefusalWindowStanding, a SpeechRefusalWindowStanding read from AppKit: inFront, the key window of the active app with nothing over it; underASheet, a sheet over it; or behind), builds the report from the cursor's chapter through the offset map's toOriginal, keeps it with SpeechRefusalAlert.show(_:), which replaces any report shown and counts its place, announces SpeechRefusalWording.announcement unless the window stands inFront, and writes SPEECH_REFUSAL_REPORTED to speech's log (the chapter, the offsets, the chunk's range, the count, and whether it announced). closeSpeechRefusalReport() lets the report go and keeps the counts; nothing else closes it, Play included. A reader without the chapter's text makes no report and announces SpeechService.engineRefusedAnnouncement, and a closed reader makes none.
  - How others observe it: SpeechRefusalReportPresenter, attached in ReaderView, presents SpeechRefusalReportSheet while showsReport(keepingOne:panels:transcriptionDialogShows:errorAlertShows:) holds: a report is kept, none of the reader's panels shows (ReaderPanels.isShowingAny: Headings, Bookmarks, Sleep Timer, Book Info, Voice, Search, and the Export dialog, ReaderPanels.export, which ReaderBookCommandsSupport opens through a binding), none of its transcription dialogs shows (TranscriptionPresenter.isShowingAny; ReaderView keeps the presenter), and its error alert doesn't; SwiftUI's putting the sheet away changes nothing, so a panel or dialog opened over it only sets it aside. The sheet follows a ReaderSnapshot of the report (PERF30), so a playing reader's publishes never redraw it, and decides once, as it is made, whether InitialVoiceOverFocusModifier targets its heading and summary, one element (sheetTakesVoiceOverFocus: anywhere but behind). It shows the report's text in FeedReadOnlyText labelled Details, and offers Copy Details (copyDetails(of:to:), the general pasteboard in the app, which announces SpeechRefusalWording.copiedAnnouncement), Show Logs in Finder (LogsSettingsSection.showLogs(for:)), and Close, the default action, which Escape presses through SilentKeyEquivalent.

- Autoplay requests: Continue to Next Book.
  - Owner: ReaderAutoplayRequests.shared, a ReaderRequestStore (Services/ReaderRequestStore.swift) of ReaderAutoplayRequest, the one shape the reading list's requests share (below): at most one request per book, keyed by the book, claimed at most once (consume(for:)), and thrown away once older than ReaderRequestStore.lifetime, 30 seconds, unless its book's reader holds its requests (hold(for:) and release(for:)), each told as it is made (requestsMade), and dropped by discard(for:). ReaderAutoplayRequests and ReadingListOpenRequests are the store's two kinds, each with its shared store and its request(…) in a constrained extension.
  - Who may write it: a reader whose book ends with Continue to Next Book finds the next book with LibraryViewModel.nextBookInFolder(after:), the first of laterBooksInFolder(after:) that does not rest at its end (Book.restsAtEnd): the books after it in the folder's manual order as the list shows it (ManualBookOrder.orderedBooks), or, for a feed's item, the later items its feed's folder lists, newest first, whose files are here (laterFeedItems(after:of:), FR11), and hands over to it (handOverToNextBook(to:titled:askingWith:), which takes back the finished state, shows the book paused in Now Playing, and announces the next book); half a second later (continueToNextBookDelay), continueToNextBook(_:) leaves a request for it and posts continueToNextBook. LibraryWindowRouter then opens that book's reader, or brings it forward. With no book to hand over to, the book says "No more books in folder" when laterBooksInFolder(after:) is empty and "Every book after it in the folder is finished" (EndOfBookWording.onlyFinishedBooksInFolderNote) when only finished books follow. A book read from the reading list, and Next or Previous in Reading List from a playing book, leave a request the same way, through continueToNextBook(_:) (reading from the reading list, below).
  - How others observe it: the next book's reader claims the request when it finishes loading, or at once when it was already open and loaded, and starts playing. A reader that cannot load its book discards the request. A reader that downloads its book from the cloud to open it holds its book's requests (FR02), from startDownload(.opening) until claimAutoplayRequest(), discardAutoplayRequest(), which a failed or cancelled download and a failed load call, or endCloudWorkForClose(), after which the request ages again from when it was made; so the next book starts once it has downloaded and loaded, however long that takes, and meanwhile a Play in the finished book's window still says "Still starting". A Library window that refuses an open while the library moves or the data directory is unavailable drops both kinds of request for the book (ReaderOpenRefusal.dropRequests(for:), from ContentView's two refusals).
