- Reading from the reading list (FR09): whether a reader reads its book from the reading list, and where the book stands there.
  - Owner: the reader's readingListPlace, a ReadingListPlace (Models/ReadingListPlace.swift: the book's position in the list as the Reading List shows it, from 1, and how many books the list shows), nil while it doesn't read from the list (isOpenedFromReadingList); and ReadingListOpenRequests.shared (Services/ReadingListOpenRequests.swift), the requests waiting for readers, a ReaderRequestStore of ReadingListOpenRequest, as ReaderAutoplayRequests is of its own (autoplay requests, above): at most one a book, usable for ReaderRequestStore.lifetime, 30 seconds, claimed at most once. The two kinds stay in stores of their own because a reader claims each at a different moment. A reader window's value is only its book's ID, so the mark can't ride on the window. A refused open drops the book's request (ReaderOpenRefusal.dropRequests(for:), above).
  - Who may write it: LibraryView.openBook(_:), which a row's default action, its Open item, and the Open Selected Book key (Return) call, makes a request before onOpenBook at the Reading List alone, and not while the library moves (ReadingListOpenRequests.open(_:shownIn:libraryIsMoving:with:)); a book read from the list makes one for the book it continues to (continueThroughReadingList(to:)) or opens with Next or Previous in Reading List (openFromReadingList(_:)). The book's reader claims a request once: as it is created (observeReadingList()), so a long load or a cloud download can't outlive it, or, for a window already open, as the request is made (requestsMade). It then keeps the book's place current from LibraryViewModel.$library (followReadingList(in:)) until the book leaves the list or the library, and from then on reads as any reader does, even if the book is put back.
  - How others observe it: BookReaderWindowView.windowTitle(for:readFromReadingList:) titles the window "Reading List: <title> by <author>" in place of "Book: …". ReaderLocationHeader shows the place under the title (ReadingListReaderWording.place, "Reading list, book 3 of 12"), and the full Where Am I? says it after the times (fullWhereAmISummary). handleEndOfBook() hands a book read from the list to finishBookReadFromReadingList(sleepTimerEndsListening:sleepTimerStoppedHere:replayingAFinishedEnd:), in place of the book's own end-of-book behavior, as the user decided on October 7, 2026: the book rests at its end and is counted finished, once (finishAtTheEnd(replayingAFinishedEnd:), P07), and followReadingListPastThisBook(sleepTimerEndsListening:sleepTimerStoppedHere:sayingNothingFollows:), which Play on a finished book read from the list calls too (playFromTheEnd()), asks readingListContinuation(after:) for the book that follows, which continueThroughReadingList(to:) starts as Continue to Next Book starts the next book in a folder, through the same hand-over (handOverToNextBook(to:titled:askingWith:)), unless this reader's sleep timer is in effect; otherwise the reader stops, says why, and rests finished (ReadingListReaderWording.stopNote and endNote(_:going:)). goThroughReadingList(_:) serves ShortcutAction.nextInReadingList and .previousInReadingList, under Navigation with no default keys, which the Navigate menu enables through the readsFromReadingList scene value the reader's window publishes: it pauses a playing book without the pause sound, counting a book Where Am I? will play as playing (whereAmIWillPlayBook), and that pause ends whatever Where Am I? holds without resuming, even over a book that was not playing; it then announces the neighbor, and plays it when this book was playing and its sleep timer allows, or else opens it. EndOfBookWording words every end of a book, the book's own end-of-book behavior's with the sentences it always had.

### Media playback

- Speech, for text books.
  - Owner: each reader's SpeechService. speak() starts the text at once with AVSpeechSynthesizer.speak(), the direct mode, and, for a text of at most SpeechRenderCoordinator.maximumTextLength UTF-16 units, renders the same text in the background into an AudioEngineService (AVAudioEngine with a time-pitch unit, for instant seeking and speed changes). The rendered audio takes over only at a natural break: a seek, a pause and resume, either watchdog's recovery, the recovery from a refused text, or the next chapter through the look-ahead; a sleep timer's fade fades the speech being heard where it is. Speech never hands over from direct mode to rendered audio while it runs uninterrupted, because the synthesizer's word callbacks run ahead of the sound by an amount that cannot be known.
    - Direct speech starts with a chunk of at most SpeechService.directModeFirstChunkSize (1,000) UTF-16 units, since the synthesizer reads a whole utterance before its first word, and hands the synthesizer each later chunk of at most directModeChunkSize (5,000) as the one before it starts (queueNextDirectChunk()), so speech goes on after the synthesizer's own pause without waiting for the main thread. A queued chunk becomes the one speaking when the one before it ends or when it starts, whichever the main queue hears first.
    - The engine is made the first time speech needs one, for its first render or the first chapter it plays from audio rendered ahead (SpeechService.audioEngine; hasAudioEngine asks without making one), so an audiobook's reader never makes one. releaseSpeech() stops and lets go of the text and the rendered audio without making an engine, for a reader that closes, and restoreEngineVolume() restores only an engine that exists.
    - Rendered audio is a RenderedSpeechAudio: the synthesizer's pieces converted to 16-bit samples as they arrive, in blocks of 128 KB, then the silence padding every render ends with. It is immutable, so the look-ahead's engine hands it to the speech service's. A play converts back only what it schedules: RenderedSpeechFeeder schedules a few slices on the player node at once, and fills and schedules the next each time the node reports one consumed, on a serial queue of its own, so a busy main thread never leaves the node without audio; a seek schedules from the slice that holds the cursor, copying nothing else.
    - The voice is loaded ahead of the first Play: SpeechRenderCoordinator.warmUp(_:) renders one word with it and drops the audio (VoiceWarmUp), once per voice in a session, on the render queue like any render, giving up its turn after 30 seconds. A text book's reader asks when it opens and when its voice changes (ReaderViewModel.warmUpVoice()).
    - Every utterance made from a book's text is handed to the synthesizer guarded, through SpeechMarkupGuard.neutralized(_:) (Services/SpeechMarkupGuard.swift): the two brackets of each tag-shaped run (tagPattern: a closing tag, even with spaces or no name; a declaration, comment, or processing instruction; an opening tag whose name, any run of characters other than brackets, spaces, and slashes, with spaces around it or not, is followed by nothing but name=value attributes; or an empty tag, <> or < >) become U+2039 and U+203A, one UTF-16 unit for one, so the guarded text keeps the original's length, and every word range the synthesizer reports, every chunk boundary, and every offset of the rendered audio's frame map still counts in the original. Direct chunks and the sleep timer's fade segments (makeDirectUtterance(speaking:at:), which also replaces every bracket of the part of an utterance that lies in strictlyGuardedRange, through SpeechMarkupGuard.neutralized(_:strictlyWithin:)), the engine's render (AudioEngineService.render, whose SpeechRenderRequest keeps its text unguarded), Where Am I?, and Speak Info (LibraryInfoSpeaker), which speaks titles, go through it; the warm-up's one word and the voice previews are constants. The speech engine of macOS 26.3.1 scans a plain AVSpeechUtterance for tags: it refuses an utterance holding a closing tag with no opening tag, as a chunk that begins after a book's opening tag can, and the synthesizer that refused never speaks again; it swallows everything after an opening tag with no closing one; and it speaks a bracket that is not part of a tag as a word. The October 7 probes in Scripts/agents/probes/ (speech-markup-probe-shapes.swift and speech-refusal-probe-queue.swift, in Samantha's compact voice and in Alex alike) found that any bracketed name without spaces swallows what follows, an emoji alone excepted, which the guard takes all the same, only silencing its brackets; that the ampersand, bare or in an entity, is never refused, so the guard leaves it alone; that an utterance queued behind another is refused only once the one ahead of it finishes; and that whatever is queued behind a refused utterance is dropped without a callback. All of this was measured offline with write(_:toBufferCallback:), which plays nothing, as SpeechMarkupGuardTests render too. neutralized(_:) also turns each NUL (U+0000) into a space, one unit for one, after guarding brackets (since October 10, 2026, #1542): a NUL ends the text for the engine, which drops the rest of the utterance without a cancel, so neither a recovery nor the report of a refusal hears it. Every other control character passes: the offline probe Scripts/agents/probes/speech-control-character-probe.swift found the 64 others, C0, DEL, and C1, spoken in full in Samantha, and nothing lost at all in Alex.
  - Who may write it: the reader, through speak, pause, resume, seek, and stop, and releaseSpeech() as it closes.
  - How others observe it: the reader sets onWordSpoken, which moves its cursor through the offset map, and, for each play, onUtteranceFinished (the playback generation, above), and mirrors whether speech is playing or paused and its speed. Every utterance a synthesizer callback reports (a word, a start, a pause, a continue, a finish, or a cancel) is held until the callback is handled on the main queue (ReportedUtterance), so a new utterance made at a reused address can never take that callback for its own; there the service reads the synthesizer's state from its own synthesizer, never the callback's. Switches between the modes are logged in textmouth_transition.log, and switchToRenderedAudio(at:) is the one way direct speech hands over to rendered audio, always where nothing is heard: at a seek, a resume, either watchdog's recovery, and a refusal's recovery. A sleep fade never switches (setSleepTimerFadeVolume(_:duration:)): direct speech fades through prepareDirectSleepFade(fromUTF16Offset:duration:startingVolume:), which starts the voice again from the last word reported in ever quieter segments, and rendered audio through the engine's volume. A seek and the watchdogs go through switchToRenderedAudioOrSpeakDirectly(at:failureTag:), which speaks directly when the engine does not start (SEEK_ENGINE_FAILED, DIRECT_WATCHDOG_ENGINE_FAILED, DIRECT_START_WATCHDOG_ENGINE_FAILED); AudioEngineService starts its engine through startEngineIfStopped(for:), which tests make fail (_refusesToStartForTesting). Two watchdogs tick on a SpeechWatchdogTimer every directWatchdogInterval (0.5 s): RunLoopWatchdogTimer, on the main run loop, in the app; _watchdogTimerForTesting gives tests timers they tick; and StoppedWatchdogTimer, which never ticks, serves a test under the utterance recorder that gives none. The start watchdog watches the live utterance from speakDirect(_:fromCharOffset:), or from a queued chunk's promotion at the previous finish, until its start or a word of it hands over to the playback watchdog. Once the live utterance has gone unstarted while shouldAwaitDirectStart holds for directStartWatchdogRequiredTicks ticks in a row, the timeout's worth and one more (directStartWatchdogTimeout, 5 s, so about 5.5 s), or for directStartWatchdogColdRequiredTicks (directStartWatchdogColdTimeout, 10 s, so about 10.5 s) when, as the watchdog began, the synthesizer had started no utterance since it was made (directSynthHasStarted, false for the service's first synthesizer and cleared by replaceDirectSynthesizer(after:)), a tick when it does not hold restarting the count, it calls recoverFromUnstartedDirectUtterance() (DIRECT_START_WATCHDOG_RECOVERY, "recovery n of 2 there"): a fresh synthesizer, then rendered audio or direct speech from the utterance's start. It recovers at most directStartWatchdogRecoveriesPerPlace (2) times at one place, the utterance's start offset (startStallRecoveries, forgotten when an utterance starts or a word of it is heard, at stop(), and as speech pauses after the stalls; a stall at another place counts from one again); the next stall there calls pauseAfterStartStalls(at:) (DIRECT_START_STALLED): a fresh synthesizer, speech paused with the place in pendingDirectSeekOffset and the cursor there, then onSpeechStalled. The reader's showPlaybackPausedAfterStartStalls() settles the pause through settleStopMadeBySpeech(), which showPlaybackStoppedAfterRefusal(_:) shares, logs PLAYBACK_PAUSED_AFTER_START_STALLS, and announces SpeechService.voiceDidNotStartAnnouncement; with no reader, speech announces it. A cancel the service did not ask for (DIRECT_CANCEL_UNEXPECTED), of the live direct utterance, playing or paused, or of a sleep fade's segment the fade still holds (every stop of the service's lets go of both before it stops the synthesizer), comes from the engine's refusal of a text, which it raises before the first word, so the playback watchdog, which the utterance's start would arm, never looks, and the start watchdog would act only once its timeout passed. It replaces directSynth first, whatever follows (replaceDirectSynthesizer(after:), DIRECT_SYNTH_REPLACED), since a synthesizer that refused never speaks again. For the live chunk, recoverFromCancelledDirectUtterance(_:) then plays the engine's audio of this speech, when renderedAudioMatchesSpeech, from the last word reported within the chunk or from its start (switchToRenderedAudio(at:), DIRECT_RETRY_ENGINE); otherwise, or when the engine does not start (DIRECT_RETRY_ENGINE_FAILED), it speaks again from there with the rest of the chunk strictly guarded (takeRetry(from:to:), which sets strictlyGuardedRange, kept until stop(), and lastDirectRetryOffset, forgotten once a word past it is reported, forgetRetryPlace(passedBy:), with the trace line DIRECT_RETRY_PLACE_PASSED; DIRECT_RETRY), once for each place until speech passes it. Paused, the retry waits for Play, through pendingDirectSeekOffset, and counts as the place's one retry (retryCancelledDirectUtteranceAtResume(_:), DIRECT_RETRY_AT_RESUME). A refused fade segment plans the fade anew from its place, starting no louder than the segment was, since the quieter segments queued behind it are dropped (recoverFromRefusedFadeSegment(_:at:), SLEEP_FADE_DIRECT_SEGMENT_REFUSED). A cancel at the offset of the last retry, before a word past it was reported, with no rendered audio to play, ends speech there (endDirectSpeechRefused(at:refusing:spanning:), DIRECT_RETRY_FAILED, naming the refused chunk's range, or a refused fade segment's own): stopped, not paused, the text kept and the cursor reported at that offset, where Play starts again and the engine usually refuses the same text again, until the listener moves past it. The three recoveries (recoverFromCancelledDirectUtterance(_:), retryCancelledDirectUtteranceAtResume(_:), and recoverFromRefusedFadeSegment(_:at:)) hand it the utterance refused, and onSpeechRefused hands the reader what was refused, a SpeechRefusal (the report of a speech refusal, under the reader, above), which the reader answers with showPlaybackStoppedAfterRefusal(_:) (ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, PLAYBACK_STOPPED_AFTER_REFUSAL in the debug log), a pause in all but the speech, which has stopped already: its flags show nothing playing, its listening session ends, Now Playing is told it is paused, so the next press of the AirPods or a media key is Play and reaches this reader, its sleep timer's countdown halts until Play, and the stop is recorded and the position saved, the chapter left as it is; it then shows the report, which tells the listener. A service no reader listens to announces SpeechService.engineRefusedAnnouncement, "Speech stopped: the Mac's speech engine refused this text.", itself. AudioEngineService.render makes its synthesizer's delegate a RenderCancellationObserver, which wakes the render queue's wait when the synthesizer cancels, since a refused write delivers no audio at all and the wait used to run its whole two minutes: a cancel the engine did not ask for (RENDER_REFUSED) is followed by one more write with every bracket silent (RenderGuard.everyBracket, through SpeechMarkupGuard.neutralizedStrictly(_:)) on a fresh synthesizer, under the same render (write(_:voice:for:asRetry:)), whose audio is installed with the request's signature (RENDER_RETRY_RENDERED), so a look-ahead render gets it too; refused again (a second RENDER_REFUSED), nothing is installed, and speech, which began directly, goes on directly. render(_:completion:) runs its job on the engine's renderQueue (the global queue unless a test passes its own), and a job whose engine is gone reports false on the main queue, so SpeechRenderCoordinator's queue, which waits for each report, is never held.
  - Files: Services/SpeechService.swift, Services/AudioEngineService.swift, Services/SpeechMarkupGuard.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+VoiceAndSpeed.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+WhereAmI.swift.
- The installed voices.
  - Owner: VoiceCatalog.shared, on the main actor: the voices and their languages. The system keeps no list, and each AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices() call enumerates every installed voice afresh, so the catalog lists them off the main thread.
  - Who may write it: loadIfNeeded(), which the first Library window, the Settings window, and the reader's voice sheet call as they appear, lists the voices once; listNow() lists them at once, on the main thread, only at the very first launch, when SettingsViewModel must choose a default voice; and reload() lists them again on AVSpeechSynthesizer.availableVoicesDidChangeNotification, as when a voice finishes downloading.
  - How others observe it: SettingsViewModel offers the list for the chosen language each time the catalog lists (observeVoiceCatalog, through didList), and the reader's voice sheet observes the catalog; neither lists voices itself.
  - Files: Services/VoiceCatalog.swift, ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift, Views/Reader/ReaderView.swift, Views/Settings/SettingsView.swift, App/TextMouthApp.swift.
- Audiobook playback, and its failures.
  - Owner: each reader's AudioPlaybackService, which plays files with an AVQueuePlayer at a speed held to the steps AudioSpeed.normalized(_:) allows. A chapter is a whole file, a time range within one, or, in a segmented book such as a DAISY audiobook, a section (AudioSegment) holding a list of clips, each a time range of a file. playClips(_:fromChapterTime:) plays a section's clips one after another as one chain (AudioClipChain), timed from the chapter's start whichever clip and file are playing. DAISYAudioConverter (Parsers/DAISYParser+Audio.swift) gathers every clip a heading plays, up to the next heading and across files, when the book is imported, and the content index records and validates every clip. setFileAfterCurrent(_:) names the file that plays after the loaded one, so that at the loaded file's end the player rolls straight on into it rather than starting a new player; the name is forgotten on any load, clip end, stop, or failure, and never queued during a clip chain. The reader names the next chapter's file as each chapter's audio starts, and again as this reader's sleep timer is set or cancelled (nameFileAfterCurrentChapter()), in a multi-file audiobook only, whose chapters are whole files, and only while its own chapter's file plays. It names none where playback may have to stop or wait at the chapter's end (fileAfterCurrentChapter): at the last chapter, while this reader's sleep timer stops playback at the chapter's end, as one set for the end of the heading does in an audiobook, whose every chapter begins with its heading (SleepTimerService.stopsPlaybackAtChapterEnd(endingTheHeading:endingTheBook:ownedBy:)), or when the next chapter's file is only in the cloud (chapterHasFileOnlyInCloud(_:)). The reader takes the next chapter in the same turn of the main queue in which the service reports the end and adopts the queued file, and the service ignores that file's clock until then, so the cursor never runs back to the start of the chapter that ended. A file named while it was on this Mac that has gone back to the cloud by its turn is stopped as its chapter begins, and the chapter downloads first, as any chapter only in the cloud does.
  - Who may write it: the reader (playAudioChapter, setAudioSpeed, retryAudioPlayback).
  - How others observe it: what plays ends once. Every load, play from a time, seek, stop, and finish begins a new play (AudioPlaybackGeneration), and AudioEndNoticePolicy judges each of AVFoundation's end-of-file notices by the generation and the playhead read when it was posted. The notice ends the play under way; or it is held while playback is paused, and finishes the play when it resumes; or it is ignored, when it was posted during an earlier play; or, as the late end of an earlier playthrough of the same file, it is ignored while the play under way starts again on a fresh player. onPlaybackFinished is called once for each play that runs to its end, never while paused. A file that cannot be played is reported through one channel, AudioPlaybackService.failure (an AudioPlaybackFailure naming the file) and onPlaybackFailed, and never also as finished. Playback stops where it was, and the reader publishes audioPlaybackFailure, which shows an alert with Retry.
  - Files: Services/AudioPlaybackService.swift, Models/AudioSegment.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+CloudDownload.swift, Parsers/AudioBookParser.swift, Parsers/AudioMediaProbe.swift, Parsers/DAISYParser+Audio.swift.
- Now Playing and media keys.
  - Owner: NowPlayingService.shared, the one coordinator of the system's remote commands and Now Playing information for the whole process. It also blocks the play command macOS sends by mistake when Bluetooth headphones disconnect, and feeds the experimental restart-timer gesture (MediaGestureDetector). Each reader talks to it through its own NowPlayingSession, whose owner is the reader's readerID, as the sleep timer's is. It takes part in the system's media session (MediaPlayer's command targets, the system's Now Playing center, and CoreAudio's device listeners) only outside a process hosting tests (installsSystemIntegrations(in:); the system's media session, below). A reader made with a coordinator of its own (ReaderViewModel's nowPlaying parameter) talks to that one instead, as the tests' readers do.
  - Who may write it: readers, through their sessions. Only the reader whose item the system's Now Playing center shows (shownOwnerID) takes that item down, as it stops, releases media controls, or closes (clearNowPlaying(ownerID:) and unregister(ownerID:)), so a window that never played can't tell the system that TextMouth stopped while another book waits paused; pausing never takes it down, so macOS keeps sending the headphones' commands to TextMouth. clearNowPlaying(ownerID:) and unregister(ownerID:) take the reader's item down whenever the system shows it, whether or not that reader is the active owner, then show the active reader's own item, if it has one, in its place, and forget the reader's kept item; a closing reader that held the focus leaves it to the remaining reader focused or registered most recently. As a recording starts or resumes, the Record window asks pauseEveryPlayingReader(), which pauses each reader that is playing, a book a Where Am I? announcement holds paused included, through its own Pause, after invalidating a queued Play, and returns how many it paused, for the window's announcement; nothing resumes them. A reader whose book ends listening at its end (handleEndOfBook, through finishListening) keeps its session, its item shown paused at the end, never stopped, which is what keeps macOS sending the AirPods' and the media keys' commands to TextMouth; which reader takes each command is activeOwnerID's choice, the playing reader, else focusedOwnerID, except Play, which goes to focusedOwnerID first. focusedOwnerID is whichever came last of the reader whose window became key (focus(ownerID:)), the reader about to play or resume (prepareForPlayback(ownerID:), which play() and every resume reach through NowPlayingSession.activateRemoteCommandsIfNeeded()), and the playing reader that stops playing, wherever its window is (playingOwnerPaused(_:), from updateNowPlayingInfo(ownerID:title:author:isPlaying:) with isPlaying false, which every pause that pauses something reports, as do the book's end (finishListening(announcing:)), the hand-over (handOverToNextBook(to:titled:askingWith:)), Where Am I? pausing the book, and speech's own stops (settleStopMadeBySpeech()), and from the coordinator's own Pause and Play/Pause and pauseEveryPlayingReader()), so a book that pauses or ends behind another book's window is shown paused and the next Play reaches it. prepareForPlayback(ownerID:) gives the reader about to play the focus before it pauses the playing reader, which is no longer the playing one as it pauses, so that reader's report of its pause takes nothing back; it then pauses every other reader whose isCurrentlyPlaying() holds, such as a book a Where Am I? announcement holds paused, or one waiting to play for its chapter's speech or files ("exclusive pause other="). ReaderViewModel.hasFinishedBook marks a book resting at its end, in memory, and showLoadedContent sets it as a book saved exactly at its end opens (cursorRestsAtEnd, P07), so a finished book stays finished after a relaunch; showLoadedContent first brings the saved place into the loaded text (openingPlace(of:savedGlobalOffset:in:), by TextPlace.opening, T06's question 7): an offset past its chapter's end opens at that end, the book's end in the last chapter, so a book whose last chapter shortened opens finished, as its row, by Book.restsAtEnd, says; a chapter past the last opens at the end only when its saved global offset reaches the total: handleEndOfBook sets it whatever the end-of-book behavior, and its loop branch clears it again, as does handOverToNextBook(to:titled:askingWith:), the one hand-over of Continue to Next Book and of the reading list's continuation, which also shows the book paused in Now Playing as it announces the next book, so that book's start (prepareForPlayback(ownerID:)) finds nothing playing to pause. The hand-over keeps a NextBookHandOver in ReaderViewModel.nextBookHandOver: the next book's ID and title (nextBookTitle), asked for exactly once, half a second later (continueToNextBookDelay) or at once by a Play, and under way until that book's ReaderAutoplayRequests request is no longer waiting, claimed, dropped, or too old. play() asks playGoesToTheHandOver() first, so a Play of the finished book during the hand-over asks for the next book, or, once it has been asked for, says EndOfBookWording.stillStarting(_:), "Still starting: Dune", and never plays the finished book. A book set to continue that has no next book, or whose sleep timer keeps the next one from starting, rests finished. play() as it plays, navigateTo(chapterIndex:characterOffset:), stop(), which Release Media Controls and closing the window call, and a backward skip made while another reader is the active owner clear it too; a Play on a book whose cursor rests at its end plays nothing from there, and does what the end did instead (playFromTheEnd(), P07).
  - How others observe it: remote commands go to the active owner, the reader that is playing, or else the focused reader (focusedOwnerID, above), but Play to the focused reader first. MediaPlayer doesn't say which thread it calls a command's handler on, so the handlers are made outside the main actor (remoteCommandHandler(for:of:)), where Swift 6's check that main-actor code runs on the main thread can't stop the app: receiveRemoteCommand(_:by:) accepts a command at once on the main thread, and from any other thread queues it to the main queue, in the order it came, and tells MediaPlayer it succeeded. Everything the system's Now Playing center is told goes through one writer (writeSystemNowPlaying), which a test can replace to see it. The skip and track commands go to the active owner (dispatchSkipCommandToActiveOwner), whose session's handlers call the reader's skipBackward() and skipForward(), as its keyboard shortcuts and playback bar do, and performSkip(unit:forward:) plays a finished book again from where a backward skip lands, as a skip during playback plays on, with Play's own start sound after the skip's, but only while its reader is the active owner (NowPlayingSession.isActiveOwner): while another reader is, as the one playing is, a backward skip moves the finished book's cursor only, as any stopped reader's skip does, and the book rests finished no longer. A forward skip by time at the end, or any skip that could not move, leaves the book finished and silent, while a forward skip that moves, by sentence, paragraph, heading, or bookmark, goes through navigateTo and leaves an ordinary stopped reader. canSeekLoadedSpeech seeks only speech that is speaking or paused, so a skip of a stopped reader moves its cursor without starting speech outside Play. Release Media Controls is offered while canReleaseMediaControls is true: NowPlayingSession.holdsMediaControls, which is NowPlayingService.holdsMediaControls(ownerID:), whether the reader is the active owner and the system shows its item, playing or paused, exactly what clearNowPlaying(ownerID:) would take down, unless the reader has released the controls and not played since (mediaControlsReleased). So it is offered for a book playing or paused, a finished book, and a book stopped where the speech engine refused its text (showPlaybackStoppedAfterRefusal(_:)) or where its sleep timer stopped it, and not for a book whose item another reader's playing book has replaced, even after that book pauses; releaseMediaControls() changes nothing where it isn't offered and says MediaControlsWording.notHeld, "Media controls not released: this book does not hold them.", which begins as its other sentence, MediaControlsWording.released, does (both in NowPlayingService.swift). The reader's two metadata saves, and a library change to its book's metadata (showEditedMetadata()), publish Now Playing through showSavedMetadataInNowPlaying(), only while holdsMediaControls, so a save in a stopped book's window never takes the AirPods (#1498); and pause(playingPauseSound:) first ends a Where Am I? announcement (stopWhereAmISynthesizer(), endWhereAmIWithoutResuming()), makes the pause sound only when isPlaybackUnderWay was true before it paused, and tells Now Playing only then or while holdsMediaControls. The reader's window publishes it as a focused value, which PlaybackCommands reads to show the menu item, and asks again as it becomes key: BookReaderWindowView calls windowBecameKey(), which focuses the reader's session and republishes the reader, since nothing the reader publishes follows another reader's taking the controls while its window is in the background.
  - Files: Services/NowPlayingService.swift, Services/MediaGestureDetector.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Navigation.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Bookmarks.swift, Views/Reader/BookReaderWindowView.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift.

### Timers and statistics

- The sleep timer.
  - Owner: SleepTimerService.shared, the app's one timer, and its owner: the readerID of the reader that started it, an identity of each reader window's own (ReaderViewModel.readerID), which also owns the reader's Now Playing session, so two windows of the same book are told apart. What the timer waits for is a SleepTimerDuration: a number of minutes, the end of the heading (endOfHeading, the one place-based timer for any document since October 6, 2026, when End of chapter folded into it: where the next heading of any level begins or the book ends; an audiobook's chapters, tracks, and marker segments each begin with a heading, so its heading ends with its chapter), or the end of the book (endOfBook).
  - Who may write it: a reader starts, cancels, or restarts the timer, passing its readerID, and starting a timer replaces any other. Pausing and resuming the countdown, and the end of the heading an End of heading timer waits for, count only when they come from the owning reader, so another window's playback, even of the same book, never affects the timer. That timer waits for the heading after the cursor when it was set (ReaderViewModel.sleepTimerNextHeading, found once a loading book has its headings; nil after the last heading, which ends with the book): the reader's refresh, at most every 0.12 s, asks whether playback reached it (stopForSleepTimerIfPlaybackReachedHeadingEnd, through waitsForHeadingEnd(ownedBy:)) and, if so, stops playback, puts the cursor at that heading, and ends the timer (expireAtHeadingEnd(by:)); a move forward to or past it ends the timer the same way, after the move's own announcement (endSleepTimerIfMoveReachedHeadingEnd); a move back leaves it waiting. Where a chapter's speech or audio ends, the reader asks whether its own timer stops playback there and, if so, ends it (expireAtChapterEnd(endingTheHeading:endingTheBook:by:), deciding through stopsPlaybackAtChapterEnd(endingTheHeading:endingTheBook:ownedBy:)): End of heading stops there when the next chapter opens with the next heading (ReaderViewModel.chapterEnding(beforeChapter:), through headingHasBegun(_:atStartOfChapter:text:)), which is where End of chapter and End of heading differed, since a next chapter with text before its first heading, or none, plays on under the heading; End of heading and End of book both stop at the last chapter's end, the book's. notifyHeadingBoundary and notifyDocumentEnd are gone. A timer ended so stops as stop() stops it, with no fade left to run and no expiry callback. The last timer started is saved, as sleepTimer.lastUsedDuration, for Restart Sleep Timer, and a saved endOfChapter or endOfDocument restores as End of heading. A reader that closes stops the timer if it owns it, and stops being its last owner (forgetLastOwner(_:)), so Restart Sleep Timer from a media gesture goes to the focused reader instead.
  - How others observe it: every sleep timer sheet shows the shared state, with End of heading and End of book in its Stop At section, after the minute presets and the custom minutes (SleepTimerView.endOfHeadingTitle, endOfHeadingLabel, and endOfHeadingHint, and the End of book constants), and VoiceOver reads the timer's status, in the sheet and in the reader, as spokenLabel, such as "Sleep timer: 4:59 remaining" or "Sleep timer: stops at the end of this heading". The owning reader's callbacks fade out and pause its playback when a timer counting minutes runs out (sleepTimerExpiredAnnouncement): an audiobook through its volume, and speech through setSleepTimerFadeVolume(_:duration:), which never switches direct speech to rendered audio (speech, under media playback, above). A stop at the heading's end, within a chapter or with one, announces sleepTimerStoppedAtHeadingEndAnnouncement, "Sleep timer expired at the end of the heading. Playback paused."; at a chapter's end the reader stops before the next chapter starts (stopForSleepTimerAtChapterEnd(of:nextChapterIndex:)): its cursor waits, saved, at the next chapter's start, and a text book discards the chapter rendered ahead unplayed. At the book's end, handleEndOfBook starts the book again only when no timer of the reader's stops there, and continues to the next book only when no timer of the reader's is in effect at all, since the timer could never pause the next book's reader (sleepTimerEndsListeningHere); VoiceOver hears why nothing follows. An audiobook fades out toward a stop at a chapter's end over the timer's fade-out time, its volume following the time left in the chapter at the playback speed (fadeOutBeforeSleepTimerStop(), from AudioPlaybackService.timeLeftToPlay), so a pause holds the volume where it is and cancelling or replacing the timer restores it; speech is not faded toward such a stop. An m4b's chapter marker inside one file stops the timer through the heading path, a moment past the marker, at the next refresh after an audio position update, without a fade, since AudioPlaybackService.timeLeftToPlay measures the file, not the chapter. textmouth_timer.log records each timer from start to expiry.
  - Files: Services/SleepTimerService.swift, Models/DocumentContent.swift, Views/Reader/SleepTimerView.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel+Playback.swift, ViewModels/ReaderViewModel.swift, Services/AudioPlaybackService.swift.
- Listening checkpoints.
  - Owner: ReadingStatsService.shared: the statistics, saved as reading_stats.json, and the open listening session.
  - Who may write it: a reader starts a session when it plays and ends it when it pauses or stops. Every 60 seconds a timer checkpoints the open session, crediting the time since the previous checkpoint and saving, so a crash loses at most a minute. markBookFinished(bookID:title:) counts each book once (P07): it writes an entry only for a book Books Finished counts for the first time, returns whether it did, and posts bookDidFinish at every end it is told of, so a feed item marked unplayed and heard again is played again; a rewind's replay of a finished end tells it nothing (finishAtTheEnd(replayingAFinishedEnd:), the reader, above). The entry records the book with its kind (FinishedBookEntry.kind, a FinishedBookKind: a text book, which an article a feed downloaded is too, an audiobook, or a podcast episode, an audiobook a feed downloaded), told while the library still holds the book, since a played episode's download is soon deleted: the feed engine installs the resolver on the statistics service (ReadingStatsService.finishedBookKind, answered by FeedCenter.finishedBookKind(of:)), which knows its episodes, an audiobook Move to Library… made of an episode among them, even once its feed's record is gone, by its package record's feed link, read once without downloading (cameFromAFeed(_:)), and reads any other book's format from the library. A kind a later version writes reads as none. No save is written while reading_stats.json is only in the cloud (files several Macs write, above).
  - How others observe it: the Books Finished figures count ReadingStats.countedFinishes, each book once, by the first entry naming it, so the repeats an earlier version wrote at each end of a book stay in the file and count once (booksFinishedAllTime, booksFinishedThisYear(at:), in the year of that first finish), and Recently Finished lists recentlyFinished(limit:), the last ten of them, newest first, its rows keyed by entry (FinishedBookEntry is Identifiable by its book and date). The Stats tab reads every figure at one instant on each tick (ReadingStatsFigures), including the open session's time since its last checkpoint, and the books finished by kind (booksFinishedThisYearByKind(at:) and booksFinishedAllTimeByKind, as FinishedBookCounts), a book finished before kinds were recorded counting by the library's answer while the library holds it, and in no kind after; the two Books Finished tiles show the kinds under the count and speak them with it (ReadingStatsTileWording.books(_:byKind:finished:)).
  - Files: Services/ReadingStatsService.swift, Models/ReadingStatsTile.swift, Views/Settings/ReadingStatsView.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedLifecycle.swift.
- Day keys.
  - Owner: ListeningDays. A day key such as "2026-10-02" names one local day, written in the Gregorian calendar, the POSIX locale, and ASCII digits whatever the user's settings, in the time zone current when the time is credited. Time already credited never moves.
  - Who may write it: ReadingStatsService credits time under these keys, and ListeningDayKeyMigration converts, once, keys saved under the system's own calendar (ReadingStats.dayKeyVersion).
  - Files: Services/ListeningDays.swift, Services/ListeningDayKeyMigration.swift, Services/ReadingStatsService.swift.

### Windows

- App-wide actions.
  - Owner: LibraryWindowRouter.shared. It keeps weak references to every open Library window, each with its way of taking an action, and to every reader window, each with its way of opening another reader, and decides once which window takes each GlobalAction: Open Book…, New from Text…, Import DAISY Folder…, Go to Library, opening files from Finder, opening a book's reader, and Continue to Next Book. An action that no open window can take waits while a Library window opens.
  - Who may write it: Library windows register and unregister themselves (ContentView); reader windows register (BookReaderWindowView); AppDelegate supplies the way to open a Library window. Menu commands, keyboard shortcuts, finished books, and imports that open their book (LibraryViewModel.readerOpener) post notifications that the router listens for on the default center from launch (GlobalAction.routedNotificationNames), and AppDelegate hands over the files Finder opens; each becomes one call of route(_:).
  - How others observe it: only the chosen window performs the action. While the library is unavailable or moving, a Library window refuses the actions that add or open books and says why, on its Library Not Available screen or in the move's sheet (DataDirectoryUnavailableText.refusal(of:) and DataDirectoryMoveWording.refusal(of:)); files opened from Finder during a move are imported once it ends.
  - Files: Services/LibraryWindowRouter.swift, App/AppDelegate.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift, Views/ContentView.swift, Views/Reader/BookReaderWindowView.swift.
- Each Library window's place.
  - Owner: the window's own views: its folder path, category, search, selection, and back and forward history (FolderNavigationHistory). A second Library window keeps its own. The category is a LibraryCategory (ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift): Folders, All Items, Unopened, Recently Opened, and Reading List, in the picker's order. It is ContentView's @State, Folders as every window opens, since nothing saves it. Unopened (LibraryCategory.unopened) replaced No Folder (noFolder), which listed the root's books, on October 7, 2026, and the type's decoder reads a category saved under No Folder's name as Unopened. LibraryCategory.recent is named "Recently Opened" since FR13 (October 10, 2026), in the picker and the window's title alike; the decoder reads the former name, Recent (formerRecentName), as it, and its sort is still saved under __recent__. VoiceOver reads the picker as LibraryView.categoryPickerLabel, "Library category", and each segment by its category's name.
  - Who may write it: the window's own navigation, and, for its folder, the library's folders changing elsewhere: ShownFolderFollowing (Views/Library/LibraryView.swift) notes, by ID, the folder the window shows each time it shows another, and when the library's folders change, as another window deletes, renames, or moves that folder, puts the window where FolderSheetNavigation.folderPath(showing:folderID:in:) says: the same path while a folder is there, the folder's new path when it is there under another, and otherwise the nearest folder above it still in the library, or the top level (the feed follow-ups after FR14). Nothing moves while the library's index is being recovered, when the list of folders is provisional.
  - Files: Views/ContentView.swift, Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, Models/FolderNavigationHistory.swift.
- The list a Library window shows.
  - Owner: LibraryView, which reads its list from the model once per draw (the lists Library windows show, above) and passes it down. Each row is a LibraryBookListRow, the unit SwiftUI compares: equal on everything it shows (the book, whether a reader has it open, the speed, edit mode and selection, the books its commands act on, the cloud state and commands, whether it is kept on this Mac, the manual-order moves, the removal preferences, the audio commands (LibraryAudioCommand) and the transcription note, the commands of the feed item it came from, whether it says its book is in the reading list, its reading list commands, and its book's live position object, compared by identity), never on its actions (LibraryBookRowActions), which read the current state when they run. So one changed book redraws one row, and SwiftUI builds a row's context menu only when the row has something new to show. A book resting at its end (Book.restsAtEnd, P07) says EndOfBookWording.finishedProgress, "Finished", on screen and to VoiceOver, in place of its percent, with no time left (BookRow.progressText(for:), spokenProgress(for:besideDownloadPercentage:), and timeRemaining(for:wpm:)), and Speak Info's facts take the row's words (BookInfoFacts, from LibraryInfoRow.swift). Move Book Up and Move Down come from the order the list shows (LibraryManualMoves), a folder's manual order or the reading list's. LibraryManualMove names a row's Move Up and Move Down once, for its context menu items and its VoiceOver actions; LibraryManualMoves.offered lists those a single book's row offers, only the moves its book can make, which BookRow puts first among its actions (FR09), running them through LibraryBookRowActions.moveSayingPlace, while the context menu's run moveInManualOrder. A window's two selections, the list's own and edit mode's checks, select together the listed books LibrarySelectionPolicy.selected(in:native:edit:) returns, in the list's order: those Move, Remove, and the reading list's commands act on (selectedBooks), which the edit bar (EditModeBar) and the Edit button's hint both count, folder and feed rows and books no longer listed left out (REV13). EditModeBar.offersDeselectAll(selectedCount:totalCount:) makes its Select All a Deselect All only while a book is listed and every one is selected, and Select All is dimmed while none is.
  - Files: Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift.
- The library's sheets: the folder edits and the Book Info a Library window asks in a sheet.
  - Owner: the window's views. Views/Library/LibrarySheetViews.swift holds the folder sheets (NewFolderSheet, MoveFolderSheet, RenameFolderSheet, MoveFolderToParentSheet, and DeleteFolderMoveContentsSheet, for Delete Folder's Move Contents) and LibraryBookInfoSheet, the Book Info of a plain text, Markdown, or HTML book of the library's own, which a reader shows for such a book too (an article in its feed has the read-only FeedItemBookInfoSheet, as an episode does; hand edits, above), whose edit(from:title:author:) trims the title and author, refuses a blank title, and pairs them with the title and author the sheet opened with, trimmed the same way, for Save's availability and its action alike, and whose fields say "edited by hand" for a field the book's record names (hand edits, above); OperationFailureNotice, whose run(_:isWorking:failureMessage:) clears the sheet's failure as an edit that moves books starts, keeps the sheet working until the edit answers, then shows any failure, which message(after:) announces once; and LibraryFolderPicker, the folder chooser the Move sheet, Add Feed, Feed Info, and Import OPML share.
  - Who may write it: the sheets' own controls. The picker's Create takes the Return key (keyboardShortcut(.defaultAction)) while its New Folder fields are shown, and its optional isMakingFolder binding tells an owner with a Return-key button of its own to stand that button down meanwhile, as the Move sheet does with Move. Add Feed and Import OPML pass the binding too, each holding the state the picker keeps current, and withdraw Subscribe's and Import's shortcut while the fields are shown, so no button takes Return until a name is typed and Create can act (measured in hosted windows, not in the live app). Add Feed resets that state as the picker disappears (onDisappear) when a page naming several feeds replaces the address step, so Subscribe takes Return back at the choice step; Import OPML's Done, shown once the import has ended, keeps Return, which the picker's disabled fields leave to it. Feed Info passes none: it has no Return-key button (Done takes Escape), so Create takes Return there while the fields are shown, from a number field too, whose number is entered as focus leaves it. Each feed sheet's init takes makingFolder: for the tests of the Return key (FeedUIAddFeedTests, FeedUIOPMLTests, FeedUICommandsTests), which are synchronous and start the engine through a Task they wait out, since an async test's run loop cannot run the main actor's work. The fields' Cancel is Cancel New Folder (cancelNewFolderTitle, cancelNewFolderHint), so VoiceOver tells it from the sheet's Cancel, which closes the whole sheet. It takes Escape (keyboardShortcut(.cancelAction)) while the fields are shown, closes them, clears the name, keeps the place chosen, and announces LibraryFolderPicker.newFolderCancelledAnnouncement(pickerLabel:chosen:) ("New Folder cancelled. Destination folder: Reports."); its Create announces LibraryFolderPicker.newFolderCreatedAnnouncement(name:pickerLabel:chosen:) ("Folder Q3 created. Destination folder: Reports/Q3.") at once, the new folder chosen in the pop-up, and the New Folder sheet's Create has the window say NewFolderSheet.createdAnnouncement(name:parentPath:) ("Folder Q3 created in Reports.", or "in Library Root.") once the sheet has closed (VoiceOverAnnouncer.announceAfterSheetCloses). The Move sheet's, Add Feed's, and Import OPML's own Cancel follow isMakingFolder and stand down meanwhile, so a second Escape closes the sheet; Feed Info's Done does the same since the feed follow-ups after FR14 (FeedInfoSheet binds the picker's isMakingFolder, and takes makingFolder: to open with the fields shown, as its test, which hosts Feed Info itself, does), so there a second Escape presses Done. On main before October 10, Escape offered as a key equivalent reached the Move sheet's own Cancel and closed the whole sheet, with the keyboard in the name field or out of it. The name field takes the keyboard as the fields appear (focusNameField(), from onAppear, on the next turn of the run loop), so VoiceOver, which follows the keyboard, lands there. createTakesReturn, true unless an owner says otherwise, lets an owner hand Return to a button of its own: Add Feed passes !searchFocused, so while the Search field has the keyboard, Search alone is the default button and Return never makes a folder, as it did on main before.
  - How others observe it: a failure is read as text labelled as an error, and the sheet stays open with the input. Measured in hosted windows (LibraryFolderPickerTests): a Return offered as a key equivalent (performKeyEquivalent) reaches the default button alone and a focused field's onSubmit stays silent, while the key-down path alone (sendEvent) runs onSubmit, so one press does one thing. The key and typing helpers of these tests (pressKey(_:in:), pressReturnKey(in:), typeReplacingText(_:in:of:), typeNewFolderName(_:in:), newFolderNameFields(in:), editableTextFields(in:), isEditing(_:in:), and settleHostedView(_:)) live in TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupport.swift (#1241), and startTurningTheRunLoop(_:) in FeedUITestSupport.swift.
  - Files: Views/Library/LibrarySheetViews.swift, Views/Feeds/AddFeedSheet.swift, Views/Feeds/FeedInfoSheet.swift, Views/Feeds/OPMLImportSheet.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/LibraryFolderPickerTests.swift.
- Speak Info (FR07): the selected row's information, spoken in a Library window, brief or full, and Command-I for every kind of row.
  - Owner: LibraryInfoSpeaker (ViewModels/LibraryInfoSpeaker.swift), on the main actor, one in each LibraryView's @State, never observed, since it draws nothing: the announcement speaking (its summary, brief or full, the row it describes, by LibraryInfoRow.id, and its utterance), and a synthesizer of its own, made at the first press by the factory passed to init(makingSynthesizersWith:) (LibraryInfoSpeaker.makeSynthesizer unless a test passes another, behind LibraryInfoSynthesizing, which tests replace with a recorder that plays nothing). It speaks in the voice and at the speed of the global settings, with prefersAssistiveTechnologySettings off, its text guarded by SpeechMarkupGuard, as Where Am I? speaks in a reader, and press(_:about:voice:wordsPerMinute:now:subject:) follows SummaryPress, as Where Am I? does (FR12's branch C): a brief press stops a summary speaking, a full press switches the same row's brief summary to the full one and stops the full one, and a press about another row speaks that row's summary in place of the one speaking; no press depends on when the last came. LibraryInfoSpeechDelegate hands each finish and each cancel over with its utterance (ReportedUtterance), at once when the synthesizer calls it on the main thread, as it does, and otherwise on the next turn of the main queue: the end of an announcement stopped or replaced since changes nothing, and a cancel of the one speaking that nobody asked for, as the engine's refusal of its text makes, ends it and replaces the synthesizer, which would never speak again (SPEAK_INFO_CANCEL_UNEXPECTED). A summary that takes the place of one still speaking speaks on a fresh synthesizer too (replaceSynthesizer()).
  - Who may write it: ShortcutAction.speakInfo (I) and speakFullInfo (I twice), working by default in every window but an audio editor's (ShortcutScope.app), which the Works in pop-up can change, and listed under Information after Where Am I? and Where Am I? Full Summary, and Library > Speak Info and Speak Full Info, which post speakInfo and speakFullInfo for the Library window that takes the command (WindowCommandRouting, through LibraryView's takesCommand(_:)). KeyboardShortcutManager leaves a plain key to a focused text field, so typing an I never speaks, and an action the user gave the plain I keeps it, Speak Info then having no key until that action lets it go (keyboard shortcuts, above). Book Info, Command-I, posts showBookInfo, which LibraryView routes by LibraryInfoRow.infoCommand(feedInFolder:): Book Info for a book, Feed Info for a feed's row or a feed's folder, and the speaker's full summary for any other folder, which has no sheet; with nothing selected it does nothing. Book Info's menu item, which carries no shortcut, is the Navigate menu's, which a Library window hides; Command-I reaches every window but an audio editor's, where it is the editor's Audio Info, through KeyboardShortcutManager.
  - How others observe it: LibraryInfoRow.selected (Views/Library/LibraryInfoRow.swift) resolves the row both commands act on: the first selected row in the list's order, a folder's, of the folders the Folders section shows (LibraryFeedFolders.folderRows, the feed reader's interface, below), then a feed's, then a book's, since the List selects a folder's or a feed's row by its ID as it does a book's; or else the edit selection's first book. LibraryInfoSubject's initializer, in the same file, gathers that row's facts at the press, as the list shows them then: a book's (BookInfoFacts) with its reader's live position where that is newer, its cloud note and kept state as its row has them, and its file size, measured as the File Size sort measures it, only for the full summary and never for a book in the cloud; for an episode or article in its feed, its feed's title (BookInfoFacts.feedTitle, from LibraryViewModel.feedTitle(of:)), which LibraryInfoWording.placeNote(of:) says in place of a folder, "In The Digital Antiquarian, a feed"; for a book in the reading list, its place there (BookInfoFacts.readingListPlace, said after the place note: the reading list, above); a folder's (FolderInfoFacts) with the books and unopened books of its whole tree, the feeds the Feeds section lists inside it, its own subfolders, and the time left in all its books; a feed's (FeedInfoFacts) with its counts and last check as its row shows them, its folder, and its address; and a feed's folder as its feed. LibraryInfoWording (Models/LibraryInfoWording.swift) makes every sentence from those facts (shared helpers, below).
