- A closed window.
  - Owner: the window's views. SwiftUI keeps a closed window and its views for the rest of the session, so a closed window must cost nothing. A closed Library window shows ClosedLibraryWindowPlaceholder, the word Library, in place of its list, from NSWindow.willCloseNotification until the window is key or visible again, as when SwiftUI shows it again for a book opened from Finder; a closed reader lets go of its book (a closed reader, above). A VoiceOver focus request holds its window weakly and searches only a window still showing content, so a window closed within the request's delay is neither kept alive nor searched.
  - Files: Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, Views/Reader/BookReaderWindowView.swift, Views/VoiceOverFocus.swift.
- Menus.
  - Owner: MenuVisibilityManager, owned by AppDelegate. It hides the context-specific menus, such as Playback and Navigate, when the key window cannot use them, so VoiceOver never lands in an empty menu. The Record window counts as Settings does: its identifier, "new-recording", names neither a Library nor a reader window (detectWindowType returns nil for it), so every context-specific menu is hidden while it is in front; the audio editor's windows, whose identifier is "audio-editor", count the same way. BookCommands (App/MenuCommands.swift) adds the Book menu, Edit Text in Default Editor, Edit Text With…, Transcribe Audio…, Clean Up Transcript (Experimental)…, Delete Transcript…, after a divider, Edit Audio…, and, after another, Move to Library… (FR11's branch D), one of the reader's menus (readerMenuTitles); its commands are enabled through the scene values the key reader publishes (canEditBookText, canTranscribeBookAudio, canCleanUpBookTranscript, canDeleteBookTranscript, canEditBookAudio, and canMoveBookToLibrary), so they stay available while VoiceOver reads the text without keyboard focus. NavigationCommands enables the Navigate menu's Next in Reading List and Previous in Reading List the same way, through the readsFromReadingList scene value (reading from the reading list, above). BookCommands also adds Export… to the File menu, after its import and export group: ExportCommandRouting.exportFromWindowInFront posts exportBooks when a Library or reader window, or a sheet over one, is key, and that window exports its selection or its book (WindowCommandRouting); otherwise VoiceOver hears BookExportWording.noWindowOpen or noWindowInFront. LibraryCommands adds the feed commands to the Library menu, Add Feed…, Refresh All Feeds, Import OPML…, and Export OPML…, which the Library window that takes commands runs (the feed reader's interface, below), Speak Info and Speak Full Info, which post speakInfo and speakFullInfo (Speak Info, above), Speak Import Progress, which posts speakImportProgress (import progress, below), Speak Download Progress, which posts speakDownloadProgress (the feed downloads' progress, below), and Add to Reading List and Remove from Reading List, which post addToReadingList and removeFromReadingList (the reading list, above). PlaybackCommands offers Where Am I? Full Summary after Where Am I?, posting whereAmIFull (a Where Am I? announcement, under the reader, above); no menu item shows a double press, so the two full summaries' menu items show no key. FileCommands, PlaybackCommands, and LibraryCommands give each menu command whose key is a shortcut action (ShortcutAction.keyEquivalentActions: Open Book…, New from Text…, New Recording…, Copy Reading Position, Search in Book, Import DAISY Folder…, and Add Feed…) View.menuCommandKey(_:shortcuts:frontKind:): the item shows the key in force while its action's scope admits the kind of window in front (FrontWindowKind.shared, ShortcutScope.admitsMenuCommand(in:), which admits every scope while no window is in front), and otherwise shows no key and is dimmed, so the key reaches that window's own shortcut. The Record window's Record, Pause or Resume, and Stop buttons take their keys from StoredKeyEquivalents, and their hints name the keys in force (keyboard shortcuts, under preferences, above). The File and Library menus' New Recording… and the Library menu's Add Feed… are titled by their actions (ShortcutAction.newRecording.title, ShortcutAction.addFeed.title), one name for the menu, the Shortcuts tab, and config.json. The Edit menu stays AppKit's: in an audio editor window its Undo, Redo, Cut, Copy, Paste, and Select All are the editor's (the editor window, above).
  - Files: Services/MenuVisibilityManager.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWindowController.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptCleanupCommands.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorCommands.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorEditMenu.swift, Views/AudioEditor/AudioEditorWindowController.swift, Views/Export/BookExportPresentation.swift, Views/Feeds/LibraryFeedsWindowSupport.swift.
- Key presses: which action a key press is, single or double, and the keys a text field keeps (B03).
  - Owner: KeyboardShortcutManager (Services/KeyboardShortcutManager.swift), which every window shares: its local event monitor sees each key press before the menus do and hands it to its ShortcutPressDispatcher (Services/ShortcutPressDispatcher.swift, FR12's branch B), which matches it against the manager's bindings, every action's keys in force (keyboard shortcuts, above). A press it takes never reaches the menus, but a key equivalent's key is never the manager's: no other action holds it, once or twice, in a kind of window where its command works (KeyClaims), and elsewhere its menu item shows no key and is dimmed (menus, above), so Shift-Command-T is New from Text…'s in a Library window and Show Sleep Timer's in a reader (board #1451, ended by FR12's branch E).
  - Who may write it: no one but the manager. handleKeyEvent(_:keyWindow:firstResponder:) takes each key-down with the key window and its first responder and, unless a recorder of the Shortcuts tab records or Try a Double Press runs, hands it to the dispatcher as a Press: its stroke (KeyBinding.from(event:)), its timestamp, the key window, the kind of window it was pressed in (windowKind(ofKeyWindow:): an audio editor's or the Record window by identifier; a panel of its own or a modal session's window .other; a reader or a Library window as MenuVisibilityManager.detectWindowType tells them apart, and any other window, Settings among them, .other; a sheet, and a sheet over a sheet, as the kind of the window under them all, found as menu commands are routed (WindowCommandRouting.rootWindow(for:)), where a sheet over a sheet once counted as a Library window (REV11), but nil over an audio editor, where no shortcut matches (pressKind(window:sheetParent:)); and .other with no key window), whether the key window is a dialog (isDialog(_:): a sheet, a window styled as one, or a modal session's window), whether text input has the keyboard, and whether it is a held key's repeat. The dispatcher posts back a Trigger, at once or, for a single press that waits, when its PressTimer fires (MainQueuePressTimer in the app; the clock and the timer are injected, so its tests never wait). Its Index, built in ShortcutAction.allCases order and rebuilt whenever the bindings in force change (rebuild(from:), from the manager's take(_:), which also runs a waiting single press at once), gives each kind of window's keys a single and a double action, each action's entered in every kind where its keys act (ShortcutScope.windowKinds(of:)), and a key equivalent's noted there (isKeyEquivalent(_:in:)) and given to no action, and marks singleWaits where the double action is neither the single one nor the action it escalates (ShortcutAction.escalates, read from the table ShortcutAction.escalations, in ShortcutPressDispatcher.swift: since FR12's branch C, Where Am I? Full Summary escalates Where Am I?, and Speak Full Info escalates Speak Info, whatever keys they have, so the first W or I acts at once). Its rules: a second press of the same key, with the same modifiers, in the same kind of window and window, less than the double-press time after the first by the events' own timestamps, is the double press, and one at exactly the time a single press; another key, another window, or a key a text field keeps ends a wait at once, running the waiting single press; a held key's repeats are ignored for a key with a double action, and act as its presses do for any other; once a double press has acted, each further press of its key within the time of the one before is ignored; and a waiting single press acts in its own window, held weakly, or nowhere once that window is gone. A press with Shift that matches no binding of its own matches its binding without Shift, and posts shiftHeld, only for an action that takes Shift along (ShortcutAction.actionsTakingShiftAlong, in ShortcutPressDispatcher.swift: Skip Forward, Skip Backward, and Increase Speed), and never while text input has the keyboard. No press matches a key equivalent's key in a kind of window where its action works, nor, while the key window is a dialog, one of KeyBinding.dialogKeys (Return, the keypad's Enter, Escape, and Command-Period, each with no other modifier), which press the dialog's own buttons. The dispatcher asks shouldHandle(binding:whenTextInputIsFocused:) of each press's stroke. While a text field or text view has the keyboard (isTextInput(_:): any text view, editable or only selectable, the field editor of a text field or a search field included, or a text field; SwiftUI's selectable text, which refuses the keyboard, is none of these), the field keeps every key with neither Command nor Control, and every key TextEditingKeys (Models/TextEditingKeys.swift) lists: Delete, Forward Delete, the arrows, Home, End, Page Up, and Page Down with any modifiers; the Edit menu's Command-A, C, V, X, and Z and Shift-Command-Z, matched by the binding's stroke (TextEditingKeys.contains(_:)), so a double press of such a key stays the field's; and Control, without Command, with the letters AppKit's standard key bindings give a command. For those the manager posts nothing and returns the event, so the Edit menu and then the field get it. Every other Command or Control binding acts from a field too, as Command-I's Book Info does, and Shift-Command-T, which closes the sleep timer's panel from its own minutes field.
  - How others observe it: a press the manager takes posts its action's notification, with the window it was pressed in as its source (WindowCommandRouting) and shiftHeld when Shift was held beyond the binding's own, on the notification center the manager was given, the default one in the app; a reader's Skip Forward and Skip Backward then go by the Playback tab's Shift units. TextFieldEditingKeysTests gives it one of its own and presses made-up key events with a stand-in first responder, so no key is really pressed and no window hears one, and ShortcutPressDispatcherTests drive the dispatcher with a clock and a timer of their own.
  - Files: Services/KeyboardShortcutManager.swift, Services/ShortcutPressDispatcher.swift, Models/TextEditingKeys.swift, Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/TextFieldEditingKeysTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/ShortcutPressDispatcherTests.swift.

### Saving failures

- Outstanding failed writes.
  - Owner: PersistenceFailureCenter.shared, which keeps the latest failure of each file (of each book, for package records) with its path, error, time, and a retry when the write can run again with the same data.
  - Who may write it: every service that saves a store reports failures and successes from its own queue, without waiting: the library index and package records (LibraryPersistenceService), statistics (ReadingStatsService), settings (ConfigService), substitution rules (TextSubstitutionService), feed records (FeedStore), and an import's cleanup of the original file. A later success for the same file settles its failure.
  - How others observe it: the Library window's banner shows one row for each failing subsystem, with Retry and Dismiss, and VoiceOver announces a subsystem once when it starts to fail. While anything is unsaved, hasUnsavedChanges makes quitting ask first (UnsavedChangesTermination, with Retry, Quit Anyway, and Cancel).
  - Files: Services/PersistenceFailureCenter.swift, Views/Library/PersistenceFailureBanner.swift, App/UnsavedChangesTermination.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedStore.swift.

### Imports

- An import in progress.
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel's SerialImportQueue, which runs one import at a time off the main thread, each as storage work that a data-directory move waits for or holds back (StorageBoundary.perform), and reports activity on the main queue, and ImportCancellation, which the queued imports share and the progress overlay's Cancel button sets.
  - Who may write it: FileImportService does the work. Open Book offers the types it reads (FileImportService.supportedTypes), video among them, since an audiobook may be an MPEG-4, M4V, or QuickTime file whose sound alone plays (BookFormat.audio's mp4, m4v, and mov; public.mpeg-4, com.apple.m4v-video, and com.apple.quicktime-movie), so a video episode's package imports again too; a feed still tells a video enclosure from audio for itself (FeedMediaKind, whose audioExtensions leave video's out). It identifies the source, builds the package under a hidden staging name (ImportStaging, .import- followed by a UUID and .tmb), in this Mac's Import Staging folder while the data directory is on its volume and beside the package's destination otherwise (ImportScratchSpace), copies the content, reads the book as the reader will, writes bookInfo.json and reads it back, and only then publishes the package with one rename. A TextMouth package is staged as every import is (REV13): ImportStaging.makeEmpty makes the staged package, then the .copying step copies the source package's items into it (copyContents(of:)), so the import overlay names the copy, and a package whose own folder is read-only imports, since the staged folder is TextMouth's own. A failure removes whatever was staged and throws an ImportFailure naming the file and the step, and the original file is never touched after a failure. A ZIP is inspected and extracted within an ArchiveBudget into an Archive Extraction folder on the same condition, this Mac's or the data directory's (ArchiveExtraction), and every format reader takes entries out of archives through ArchiveEntryReader, which holds each entry to its limits. A reader that looks up more than a member or two, as the EPUB, Word, and DAISY readers do, finds them in an IndexedArchive, the archive's entries read into an index by path once, since ZIPFoundation finds each by reading the archive's central directory from its start; the index answers exactly as the archive would, the first entry at a path winning. DAISY audio conversion copies each audio file through DAISYFileProvider.copyFile(at:to:): out of a ZIP a megabyte at a time (ArchiveEntryReader.stream), never whole, and from a folder as a clone. A ZIP batch reads each book's checksum once, for its duplicate check, and hands it to the book's import (importFileWithReport(from:checksum:toFolder:observer:)).
  - How others observe it: LibraryViewModel adds the published book to the library, unless the library already holds a copy, and the overlay shows how far the import has got (import progress, below); every question an import raises waits its turn in one queue (import questions, below).
  - Files: Services/FileImportService.swift, Services/FileImportService+Packages.swift, Services/ImportStaging.swift, Services/ImportScratchSpace.swift, Services/ImportFailure.swift, Services/ArchiveBudget.swift, Services/ArchiveExtraction.swift, Parsers/ArchiveEntryReader.swift, Parsers/DAISYParser.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Import.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Duplicates.swift.
- Import progress (FR10): what the import overlay shows, and what VoiceOver hears of an import.
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel.importProgress, an ImportProgress (ViewModels/ImportProgress.swift) on the main actor, whose state is an ImportProgressState: a notice, the books done of the total, the book under way (its name, its number in a batch, its ImportStep, when it began, and, for an archive holding one audiobook, how far its extraction has got, BookUnderWay.extraction, which its line says while it extracts), what the books done took (timing, a WorkTiming), the sizes the batch's books are timed by (bookSizes), the time left, whether Cancel was pressed, and the imports waiting behind the one shown (waitingImports), which a new import's state keeps. The duplicates a batch skipped are counted once, in its ZipImportResult (skippedDuplicates). Whether the overlay shows at all is still the model's isImporting.
  - Who may write it: the imports, on the main queue. ArchiveExtractor reports an archive's extraction as an ArchiveExtractionProgress, its entries and the bytes they declare, both totals from the archive's central directory, which the budget reads anyway: at the start, and whenever the whole percentage of either changes, between the chunks of a large entry as well as between entries. A ZIP batch reports ZipImportProgress.extracting, then .importingBook as each book begins and as each step of it begins (through the importer's ImportStepObserver), and .importing(done:total:skippedDuplicate:) as each book is imported, fails, or is skipped as a duplicate; a batch also reports .foundBooks(sizes:) once, before its first book, each size from FileImportService.timedSize(of:): a text book's file size (an EPUB, PDF, Word document, or plain text, Markdown, or HTML file), and nil for an audiobook's file, whose import reads its chapters rather than its every byte, and for a DAISY book. A single file, or an archive holding one book, reports through ImportProgress.observeBook(_:startingAt:then:), which tells the overlay of each step before the queue's test hook, and so do a TextMouth package opened from the Finder (openTMBPackage(from:), from .checkingPackage), New from Text (createBookFromText, from .preparingPackage, by its title), and a recording's save (LibraryViewModel.saveRecording, from .preparingPackage, by its title, REV13), whose line is cleared (clear()) rather than ended, so it announces nothing, since the New Recording window says how the save ended; an archive holding one audiobook reports its extraction through observeExtraction(). TimeLeftEstimate (Models/TimeLeftEstimate.swift, shared with the feed downloads' progress) gives the time left from the WorkTiming of the books imported or failed, duplicates not timed: each book still to come of known size takes its bytes at the rate the sized books took theirs, and any other the average time of the books timed without a size, or of every book timed before any of those, less the time it has run; TimeLeftEstimate.rounded(_:) rounds it to the minute under 10 minutes, to 5 minutes under an hour, and to 15 beyond. The library follows the import queue's count of imports queued or running (importActivityChanged(to:), which observeImportActivity() starts, and which assigns isImporting only when it changes, so the library model publishes as imports start and end and not with each import queued or done, REV13), and the progress counts those behind the one shown as waiting (noteImportsQueued(_:)). Cancel calls cancel(), and end(_:) ends the import shown.
  - How others observe it: only ProgressObserver, a generic view in Views/ContentView.swift, observes it, as it observes the text cache's progress (PERF12, #1461), drawing the overlay in a TimelineView that ticks every 5 seconds from the book's start, so the time on a book keeps up between reports while the library model is never republished. ProgressOverlay takes detail lines and a spoken summary from its caller; the text cache overlay keeps its own. ImportProgressWording words every line, the overlay's VoiceOver label, the announcements, and Speak Import Progress, the imports waiting included (waiting(_:), "2 more imports waiting", left out once Cancel is pressed, since queued ZIP archives never start). An import that has run 10 seconds (ImportProgress.announcedAfter) announces each quarter of a batch's books once, at medium priority, and its end, unless Cancel was pressed, when its alert says where it stopped; Cancel is announced at once. A batch that found no new book and in which nothing failed (ZipImportResult.foundNoNewBook) is announced however short, by noNewBooks(in:alreadyInLibrary:), since no alert or question follows it. The reading list's join after an import made at the Reading List is announced at medium priority (addImportedBooksToReadingList(_:request:)), after the import's own words rather than over them. ShortcutAction.speakImportProgress, under Information with no default key, and Library > Speak Import Progress post speakImportProgress, which the library model hears from any window (observeImportProgressRequests()) and answers through VoiceOver.
  - Files: ViewModels/ImportProgress.swift, Models/ImportProgressWording.swift, Models/TimeLeftEstimate.swift, Services/ArchiveExtraction.swift, Services/FileImportService.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Import.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift, Views/ContentView.swift, Models/CountWording.swift, Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift.
- Import questions.
  - Owner: LibraryViewModel.importDecisions, an ImportDecisionQueue of the questions imports are waiting to ask (whether to replace or keep a duplicate, whether to delete an original file, whether to put a ZIP archive's books in a new folder, and whether to transcribe an audiobook just imported), asked one at a time in the order they arose. Each ImportDecision holds everything its answer acts on, captured when its import finished (a duplicate's new copy and the copy it matched, an original file, a ZIP archive's books and folder, or an audiobook's ID, title, and length), and the ImportRequest its import was made with: the Library window it started in (ImportOrigin), whether to open the book afterwards, and the cancellation its batch shares.
  - Who may write it: LibraryViewModel+Import and LibraryViewModel+Duplicates. ask(_:) queues a question behind those waiting, and a question an answer raises about the same file, such as whether to delete the original of a duplicate just kept, is asked next. An offer to transcribe (ImportDecision.Kind.transcriptionOffer, FR05) is queued after an import's own questions, for each audiobook it added, when TranscriptionOffers.offersTranscription(of:) allows it: an audiobook in a package of its own, Offer to transcribe after importing an audiobook on, and a Mac that can transcribe. Its Transcribe sends LibraryViewModel.acceptedTranscriptionOffers to the Library window that asked (LibraryTranscriptionSupport), which opens the Transcribe Audio sheet, starting at once when it can. answerImportDecision(_:with:) acts only on the question being asked, named by its ID, so a second press can never answer the next one; an answer that fails, such as a folder name the library refuses, keeps the question open with the reason. While the data directory moves (dataDirectoryMove is set), it refuses every answer with DataDirectoryMoveWording.importAnswerRefusal, shown in the sheet and spoken, and the question stays until the move ends, since a window asking one when a move begins keeps that sheet instead of the move's. cancelImports(), the progress overlay's Cancel, withdraws the questions of its batch, those waiting and those raised later: a duplicate's new copy is discarded, an original file is kept, a ZIP archive's books stay where they are, and an offer to transcribe is dropped.
  - How others observe it: each Library window's ImportDecisionPresenter watches the queue, and exactly one window asks the question at its front (ImportDecisionQueue.askingWindow): the window its import started in while that is open, or else the first open Library window, the question waiting while none is open. That window shows ImportDecisionSheet, whose words, the screen's and VoiceOver's alike, come from ImportDecisionWording, with a title that gives the question's place when there is more than one, as in "Duplicate Book Found, 2 of 5". The choice that removes nothing comes first; Return and Escape press the marked choices, and Replace Existing and deleting an original file are never on a key. A question that appears while the user is in another window waits without taking focus or being announced. A book whose import asked to open it, as one opened from Finder does, opens through LibraryViewModel.readerOpener once it is in the library and its questions are answered.
  - Files: Models/ImportDecision.swift, Models/ImportDecisionQueue.swift, ViewModels/TranscriptionOffers.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Duplicates.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Import.swift, ViewModels/DataDirectoryMoveProgress.swift, Views/Library/ImportDecisionPresenter.swift, Views/Library/ImportDecisionSheet.swift, Views/ContentView.swift.

### Feeds

- Feed records (FR03): each subscription and the episodes and articles it keeps.
  - Owner: FeedStore, on the main actor, inside FeedCenter.shared. Each subscription's record (FeedRecord, in format FeedRecord.currentFormatVersion, 1) holds the Feed (its address, title, folder by ID, the newest item date, after which an item is new, whether it uses plain http, its size as its last read found it, its own List and download choice and delete-after override, the date the listener unsubscribed, nil while subscribed (Feed.unsubscribedDate, written only once set, read through Feed.isSubscribed; FR11's branch D), and, until the move to the choice clears it, a retired kept-entries number) and its items, newest first (FeedItem, each with its download's state, who asked for its download last, when it played, when its download was deleted, and when the item was deleted). An item's state may be inLibrary(bookID:), once Move to Library… made its download a book of the library's own: FeedItemState.libraryBookID reads it, linkedBookID either kind, and bookID stays a download's alone. An item In Library is always listed, never new, not counted as a download, and counted among the newest N. The size, Feed.entryTotal, a FeedEntryTotal, counts the entries the feed lists, each once, the ones the listener deleted among them, and says whether the feed lists more than the parser reads (listsMore, past FeedParser.maximumItems, 2,000); it is optional, so a record written before October 7, 2026 loads without it. It is one file in the data directory's Feeds folder, Feeds/<feed ID>.json (FeedPaths), pretty-printed with sorted keys and ISO 8601 dates. The file's name decides the feed's ID, so a sync service's conflict copy is never read. Beside it, Feeds/<feed ID>/ holds the feed's downloaded items' packages and its own index of them, which the library model owns (feed items, under the library index, above), and, hidden, a kept package set aside while a download replaces it (.replaced- and the package's name) and, in a library in the cloud, the notes of launches that found the record missing (.record-absent- followed by a Mac's ID and a launch's, FeedRecordAbsence).
  - Who may write it: FeedCenter's operations (subscribing, unsubscribing and resubscribing, a feed's folder and its own settings, marking an item played or unplayed, or every item played, deleting a download or an item, and deleting the feed, below), FeedReconciler's merges of a refresh, each of which records the feed's size before the kept-entries rule, whose number is now the feed's listing (the List and download choice, below), chooses the items kept, keeping beside the newest every item downloaded or downloading, every item played (board #1412), and every item whose row the library keeps after its file went (FeedReconciler.kept, keepingRecordsOf:, FR11's branch A2), the download manager, the lifecycle (played downloads, below), the newest-N rule and the move to the List and download choice (below), and Move to Library…, which marks an item In Library (feed items, under the library index, above). Each durable change is written atomically on the store's serial queue, as storage work (StorageWork.savingFeeds), so a data-directory move waits for it; a download's progress is never written, an item downloading or waiting to being written as not downloaded (FeedRecord.durable). While the data directory is unavailable, or after a write fails (FEED_RECORD_SAVE_FAILED), the change waits in memory, written with the feed's next change or once the data directory is back. A failed write is reported to PersistenceFailureCenter under feed subscriptions (feedRecords, keyed by the feed), naming the feed, so the banner offers Retry, which writes the record as it is now on the write queue, after every write asked for before, and waits for it (retryWrite(of:failureID:)), and quitting asks first; the feed's next write that succeeds settles it, and removing the feed's record withdraws it (FeedStore.remove(_:), which Delete Feed… calls). The engine's environment names the center (FeedEnvironment.failureCenter), so tests use one of their own. Delete Feed… (FeedCenter.deleteFeed(_:), Services/Feeds/FeedCenter+DeleteFeed.swift, FR14) is the one command that deletes a feed, subscribed or not, in an order whose every stopping point is safe. It refuses while one of the feed's downloads is in use, open in a reader, being transcribed, or being exported (FeedLibraryHost.feedBookUse(of:); FeedDeleteError.downloadInUse(title:use:), FEED_DELETE_REFUSED). Then it closes the feed's downloads (FeedDownloadManager.closeFeed(_:), below) and waits for the imports and rule removals already on the import queue (runBesideImports); removes the feed's downloads and the packages it kept for removed downloads as Delete Episode removes one, the Trash or for good as the removal setting says, every row of the feed (LibraryViewModel.everyRow(ofFeed:), ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+DeleteFeed.swift) leaving the library and its reading list and the indexes written before any file goes (FeedLibraryHost.removeFeedFiles(of:keepingPackages:remove:)), so a failed write deletes nothing (FeedDeleteError.indexNotWritten, FEED_DELETE_PUT_OFF) and a package that can't go keeps the feed with what stayed (filesStayed, FEED_DELETE_STOPPED); forgets the feed's index for good (forgetFeedIndex(of:)), so no save makes its folder anew, and removes Feeds/<feed ID>/ (FEED_DELETE_FOLDER_LEFT when it can't, for the next launch, which finds the folder without its record and, since it holds no package, deletes it, returning nothing); removes the record and this Mac's fetch details last (FeedStore.remove(_:), FeedFetchStates.remove(_:)), since a launch that found the folder with its record gone would return the downloads as books; and removes the feed's library folder only when nothing else is in it, no book of the library's own, folder, or other feed's items (removeFolderOfDeletedFeed(_:), FEED_DELETE_FOLDER_KEPT when it can't). It returns a FeedDeleteOutcome, the downloads and kept packages deleted and the folder's fate (FeedFolderFate), and writes FEED_DELETED. A book without the feed's link, as an item moved into the library, stays.
  - How others observe it: FeedCenter republishes every change of the records and the fetch details, publishes the feeds refreshing (refreshingFeedIDs), and sends FeedEvent values (a subscription, a refresh's end, the end of a refresh of every feed, a download's start, end, or failure, and a change of the List and download choice taking effect, itemPolicyApplied(global:feedIDs:)), which the interface reads (below). A record that can't be read is preserved beside itself (SavedFile.preserveUnusable, FEED_RECORD_UNREADABLE) and its feed left out; one only in the cloud is asked of the sync service and read at the next refresh of every feed (FEED_RECORD_IN_CLOUD).
  - Files: Models/Feed.swift, Models/FeedItem.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedStore.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedReconciler.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedCenter.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedCenter+DeleteFeed.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Feeds.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+DeleteFeed.swift.
- This Mac's fetch details.
  - Owner: FeedFetchStates, on the main actor: for each feed, the ETag and Last-Modified date a refresh sends back, the SHA-256 digest of the body last read, the number of entries kept when it was merged, the last check and the last success, the last failure's sentence, and whether the server said the feed is gone. One file per feed in this Mac's Caches folder (MachineLocalPaths.feedCaches, Fetch State/<feed ID>.json), written on a serial queue of its own and never synced, since a refresh changes them every time. A file that can't be read counts as missing, and macOS emptying Caches only means each feed is read whole once.
  - Who may write it: the refresher after each check, and subscribing. Unsubscribe keeps them, for Resubscribe's check (FR11's branch D); Delete Feed… removes them with the feed's record (FeedFetchStates.remove(_:), FR14).
  - How others observe it: the refresher's conditional requests, and FeedCenter.fetchState(of:).
  - Files: Services/Feeds/FeedFetchState.swift.
- A refresh.
  - Owner: FeedRefresher, inside FeedCenter.shared, which fetches through FeedNetwork: two ephemeral URLSessions, one for feeds and web pages and one for downloads, which cache nothing on disk, keep no cookies, and send TextMouth's own User-Agent. They are the app's only network connections, a search of the podcast directory going through them too, and App Transport Security allows arbitrary loads (Info.plist), since many feeds and episode hosts still use plain http; a feed that uses http is flagged (Feed.usesInsecureTransport).
  - Who may write it: FeedCenter: at launch when feedRefreshAtLaunch is on, every feedRefreshIntervalHours hours while TextMouth runs (never while that is 0, and a turn that falls in Low Power Mode is skipped), and when the listener asks (refresh(_:) and refreshAll(origin:)). The refreshes TextMouth makes by itself never use an expensive or constrained network, and skip a feed the server called gone (FeedRefreshOrigin.isDiscretionary). At most four feeds refresh at once, and one feed's refreshes never overlap. A feed the listener unsubscribed from is never refreshed (FR11's branch D; FeedRefresher.isChecked(_:)): refreshAll(origin:) passes over it, with no report, refresh(_:origin:) returns nil before fetching, and a fetch under way as the listener unsubscribed changes nothing once it ends; Check Feed chosen from a menu drawn before says so (FeedWording.notChecked(title:)).
  - How others observe it: a fetch sends the last ETag and Last-Modified date, and a 304, or a body whose digest matches the last, is unchanged, the feed's size kept as it was, unless the number of entries kept has changed since, or the record does not know its feed's size (Feed.entryTotal nil), when FeedRefresher.fetchAndRead sends no validators and reads the feed whole once. FeedParser reads RSS 0.9x and 2.0, RSS 1.0, and Atom, with Apple's podcast elements and Podcasting 2.0's, up to 25 MB and 2,000 items, with XMLParser, and reads a document that isn't well-formed again through XMLDocument's tidy mode (FEED_PARSE_REPAIRED). A document whose first character is "{" is JSON Feed, 1.1 or 1.0, which JSONFeedParser reads, through FeedJSON, into the same ParsedFeed (format json), so it is merged, listed, and downloaded as any other; JSON that names no JSON Feed version is no feed. The readers share the rules for choosing an enclosure (FeedEnclosure.preferred(among:otherwise:)), an untitled item's stand-in title (ParsedItem.standInTitle(from:isHTML:)), and dropping content that only repeats the summary (ParsedItem.isRepeating(_:_:)). FeedDiscovery takes a page's application/feed+json alternate too, never plain application/json. The fetch, the parse, and a first merge (FeedReconciler) run off the main actor; a second merge on the main actor folds the result into the record as it is by then, so what the listener did meanwhile is kept. A feed that moved for good, by a permanent redirect or itunes:new-feed-url, is fetched from its new address from then on (FEED_MOVED). Each refresh ends in a FeedRefreshReport, and a refresh of every feed in a FeedRefreshSummary, which FeedCenter sends as events; each refresh's end (refreshEnded) then asks for a sweep of played downloads and a pass of the newest-N rule over its feed, which downloads what the feed's List and download choice keeps downloaded (below).
  - Files: Services/Feeds/FeedRefresher.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedNetwork.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedParser.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedDates.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedReconciler.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedDiscovery.swift, Services/Feeds/JSONFeedParser.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedJSON.swift, Resources/Info.plist.
- Downloads.
  - Owner: FeedDownloadManager, inside FeedCenter.shared, which downloads items one at a time, in the order asked, an item the listener opens coming first.
  - Who may write it: FeedCenter's download, open, and cancelDownload, and the newest-N rule (the List and download choice, below), whose discretionary downloads never use a network with data limits. A download records who asked for it as it finishes, or fails (FeedItem.downloadOrigin): the listener whenever the listener asked for it at any point, or made the item theirs meanwhile, otherwise the rule (FeedDownloadOrigin.after(_:given:), which never overwrites the listener's). A download stopped as its file arrives, by Cancel Download or the downloads section's Cancel, stops before its import (Task.checkCancellation() in downloadEpisode; feed review item 15), as an article stops before its book is written; Cancel Download makes its item the listener's, while the section's Cancel leaves each item whoever's it was (FR14). Delete Feed… closes the feed's downloads first (closeFeed(_:), closedFeedIDs): the one under way stops, those waiting to start and those put off go, every resume data file of the feed goes with the partial file it names, download(_:of:origin:opening:) asks for none of its items, commit(_:for:restoredKeptPackage:) discards the package of an import that ends meanwhile, and a restored kept package (FeedEpisodeImporter.restoresKeptPackage(_:)), through discardPackage(of:asTheListenersDeletion:because:) as Removal Behavior says, closeFeed(_:) marks the items it drops not downloaded (markNotDownloaded(_:of:), shared with cancelEveryDownload() and withdrawRuleDownloads), and the sweep of played downloads and the newest-N rule leave the feed alone, until reopenFeed(_:), once the feed is deleted or left as it was. FeedPartialDownloads (Services/Feeds/FeedDownloadManager+PartialDownloads.swift, FR14) removes resume data only together with the partial file it names, which the system keeps in the process's temporary folder: it reads every string of the resume data's property list, without decoding the system's undocumented layout, and removes only a plain file directly in that folder named as the system names a partial file, CFNetworkDownload_ and letters and digits then .tmp. Cancel Download, a transfer stopped for good, the launch's sweep of week-old resume data, the section's Cancel, and Delete Feed… all remove resume data through it.
  - How others observe it: an episode's file comes through a URLSessionDownloadTask, whose bytes go to the downloads' progress alone (the feed downloads' progress, below), never to its item's state or its record. A download that stops partway keeps its resume data in this Mac's Caches folder (Resume Data, a week), and the finished file waits in Feed Downloads until FeedEpisodeImporter imports it, as storage work, through the import pipeline (ImportStaging and FileImportService.publishReadableBook), publishing the package in the feed's own folder, Feeds/<feed ID>/ (destinationPackageURL), and adding the book's row, with its feed link and no library folder, to the library on the main actor before that work ends (addFeedBook(_:); feed items, under the library index, above). A download needs the feed's library folder all the same, which lists it: a feed whose folder is gone downloads nothing. An episode, audio or video, becomes an audiobook, a video one playing its sound, and an article an HTML book written from its text, or from its web page's article when its own text runs under 200 words and it links a page. The book's row and record name its feed and item (Book.feedLink and BookMetadata.feedLink, a FeedBookLink), which its feed's index holds and library.json never does, and every later save of the record keeps the link (Book.bookInfo(replacing:contentIndex:revisionFloor:)); writeVerifiedRecord checks it on read-back. Whatever an interrupted download left in Feed Downloads is swept away at the next launch. A rule download that a network with data limits refused waits (FeedItemState.waiting, FeedDownloadWait.network, with one FEED_DOWNLOAD_WAITING line) and is put off (putOff); the next refresh TextMouth makes by itself that wasn't skipped, which shows the network has no data limits now, asks for the downloads put off again (retryPutOffDownloads, from FeedCenter's refreshEnded). One that lacks room waits too (FeedDownloadWait.room), for the rule's next pass, which never removes anything to make room; a download the listener asked for still fails for want of room. As TextMouth quits, once the unsaved-changes guard lets it, AppDelegate asks FeedQuitHold.hold, which answers terminateLater and replies once, when FeedDownloadManager.stopForQuit() ends or after three seconds (longestWait), whichever comes first: stopForQuit stops every download (isStoppedForQuit), lists each one asked for, the one under way first and those put off last, in this Mac's resume folder (Downloads at Quit.json, as SavedDownload, each with who asked for it), and then stops the transfer under way, keeping its resume data when its server allows (FeedTransferHandle, through cancelByProducingResumeData). The next launch asks for them again (resumeDownloadsSavedAtQuit, after sweepLeftovers), only where the engine works on its own.

- The feed downloads' progress (FR10's feed half): what each Library window's feed downloads section shows, and what VoiceOver hears of a run of feed downloads.
  - Owner: FeedDownloadManager.progress, a FeedDownloadProgress (ViewModels/FeedDownloadProgress.swift) on the main actor, whose state is a FeedDownloadProgressState: one run, from the moment the manager has work until it has none. It holds the downloads done and, of those, failed; the download under way (its item's and its feed's titles, when it began, its file's BookDownloadProgress, nil for an article, whose page comes uncounted, and whether its book is being added to the library); the sizes of the downloads waiting to start, as their feeds give them; the newest-N rule's downloads waiting apart (FeedDownloadWaits: those put off for a network, FeedDownloadManager.putOff, and those waiting for room, which only their items' waiting states say), which outlast the run; what the run's downloads took (WorkTiming, each timed from its start to its book in the library, a failure untimed); when the run began; and whether Cancel was pressed.
  - Who may write it: the manager, on the main actor. It tells the progress the queue after every change (publishQueue()), each download's start (beginProgress(of:)), every report of its bytes (receive(_:), through downloaded(_:of:estimate:for:), which changes the state without publishing), its book being added (importAndCommit), and how run(_:) ended (a FeedDownloadEnding: downloaded or failed, which are done, or waiting or stopped, which leave the run). A change that leaves nothing to run ends the run and forgets it, keeping the waits. Quitting clears it (stopForQuit(), through clear()), and it stays clear while the quit waits: publishQueue() and the end of the download under way tell it nothing once isStoppedForQuit (feed review item 21). Cancel is FeedCenter.cancelEveryDownload(), through LibraryViewModel.cancelFeedDownloads() (LibraryViewModel+FeedDownloadProgress.swift), a pause rather than a refusal (FR14). It calls FeedDownloadManager.cancelEveryDownload(), which cancels the task under way, unless its book is being added, which finishes; empties the queue and the downloads put off; sets every item it stopped back to not downloaded, the rule's waiting for room among them, which only their items' states say, in one change of each feed's record, each keeping who asked for it (FeedItem.downloadOrigin); deletes every resume data file with the partial file it names (FeedPartialDownloads.discardAll(in:prefix:temporaryFolder:)) and, unless a book is being added, the files waiting in the holding folder; tells the progress (cancel(), FEED_DOWNLOADS_CANCELLED); and returns a Cancellation (stopped, finishing, and feedIDs). The center then pauses the newest-N rule for each of those feeds (rulePauses, a FeedRulePause: untilNextCheck, or untilTheCheckAfterTheOneUnderWay for a feed being checked as Cancel was pressed), so applyItemPolicy asks for none of its items until refreshEnded lifts the pause (endRulePause(afterCheckOf:)) at the end of a check of the feed that began after Cancel, the regular check, Check Feed, or Refresh All Feeds; Unsubscribe and Delete Feed… clear a feed's pause, a change of its List and download choice doesn't, and none outlives the process, so the next launch's pass covers every feed. The listener's own Download is never held back. The section's Cancel goes through LibraryWindowView.cancelFeedDownloads(of:in:), which moves VoiceOver's focus to the Library window's list once the section has gone, VoiceOverAnnouncer.afterSheetDelay later (focusListAfterCancel; feed review item 19), and only once those focus attempts have been posted announces what Cancel did (FeedDownloadProgressWording.cancelled(stopped:finishing:comingBack:), REV13 item 3). cancelTargets and Cancellation.Stopped (feed, item, title) back cancelEveryDownload() and downloadsCancelWouldStop; FeedCenter.comingBack(of:) counts as a FeedCancelReturn those the rule downloads again by itself (ruleWouldDownload(_:) among countedItemIDs(in:under:)), so FeedDownloadProgressWording.comingBack(_:) says only what will happen, in cancelled(stopped:finishing:comingBack:) and cancelButtonHint(_:); FeedCenter.cancelButtonHint() feeds FeedDownloadProgressSection's hint. A download that begins after Cancel, as one the listener asks for while the one Cancel let finish is being added, ends the cancelling (FeedDownloadProgressState.begin(_:); feed review item 13). downloadArticle checks for cancellation before it writes its book, so an article stopped while its page was fetched is not written from its summary, by Cancel Download either.
  - How others observe it: only FeedDownloadProgressObserver (Views/Feeds/FeedDownloadProgressSection.swift) observes it, through a ProgressObserver (Views/ContentView.swift), as the import and text overlays observe theirs, and LibraryWindowView (Views/ContentView.swift) attaches it below LibraryView with safeAreaInset, so it insets the list rather than covering it. It draws FeedDownloadProgressSection only while downloads run, inside a TimelineView that shows it anew every 5 seconds from the run's start (FeedDownloadProgressWording.refreshInterval), which brings the bytes the state keeps without publishing. So a download's bytes never compare a feed's record (#1410) or redraw a list or a feed row (PERF12), and FeedItemState.downloading carries no progress: an item's row says "Downloading" without a percent, and a kept place beside it is spoken as "42 percent read" (FeedKeptProgress). The section is a container labelled with all its lines (spokenStatus(at:)), holding its heading, its bar (overallBarLabel), its lines, and Cancel (cancelButtonLabel, "Cancel Feed Downloads", and cancelButtonHint), dimmed once pressed. TimeLeftEstimate (Models/TimeLeftEstimate.swift) gives the time left once one download has reached the library (secondsLeft(at:)), and none once Cancel is pressed. FeedDownloadProgressWording (Models/FeedDownloadProgressWording.swift) words every line, announcement, and control, reusing ImportProgressWording's sizes, its time left, its cancellingLine, and spokenStatus(title:lines:). A run the newest-N rule began, which nobody heard begin, is announced once as it has lasted ImportProgress.announcedAfter, at medium priority, with the downloads under way and the time left (FeedDownloadProgressWording.underWay(_:secondsLeft:); announceStartIfDue(at:), the run's first download begun byItself), unless a quarter was heard or Cancel pressed first (feed review item 17). A run that has lasted ImportProgress.announcedAfter, 10 seconds, announces each quarter of its downloads once, at medium priority, and its end, naming the download of a run of one, unless Cancel was pressed or nothing downloaded, failed, or waits; what Cancel stopped is said by the window it was pressed in, once its focus has moved (above): the downloads stopped, the download that finishes all the same, and when TextMouth downloads again by itself (FeedDownloadProgressWording.retryAfterCancel), which the Cancel button's hint says too. ShortcutAction.speakDownloadProgress, under Information with no default key, working in every window but an audio editor's, and Library > Speak Download Progress post speakDownloadProgress, which the library model hears (observeDownloadProgressRequests()) and answers with FeedDownloadManager.spokenProgress(), which counts the waits anew first, since a wait for room lives only in its item's state.

- Played downloads, and their deletion.
  - Owner: each item's playedDate and downloadDeletedDate, in its feed's record.
  - Who may write it: ReadingStatsService.markBookFinished posts bookDidFinish, and FeedCenter marks the item whose download finished played (FEED_ITEM_PLAYED), so a finished episode or article counts among Books Finished too; markPlayed(_:of:) and markUnplayed(_:of:) set or clear it for the listener, and markAllPlayed(of:) sets it for every item of a feed not played yet, in one change of its record. A sweep (requestSweep(), at launch, after each refresh, when an item plays to its end or is marked played, and whenever the books open in readers change, as when a reader closes) deletes the file of each download played at least feedDeleteDownloadsDaysAfterPlayed days ago, or the feed's own number (0 at once, -1 never), for good, with dataless files left in the cloud, keeping its package, with an article's content.md in it (FeedItemPackage.staysWithoutTheFile(_:)), while it holds the listener's state, in the order the feed items entry gives (feed items, under the library index, above): never a book in use, which the sweep leaves for its next pass (FeedLibraryHost.feedBooksInUse: one open in a reader, one an export is reading, BookExportModel.booksBeingExported, and the one being transcribed, TranscribedBooks.shared.transcribingBookID; FeedDueRemovalOutcome.inUse and the reports' inUseBookIDs; REV11 F1), never while the library's index is being recovered, never a download made before FR11 that has not moved into its feed's folder, and, in a library in the cloud, only a package this Mac holds whole, as the packages' cloud states, read off the main thread first, say (packagesHeldWhole, FEED_SWEEP_LEFT_IN_CLOUD). The removal goes through removeDue(_:from:by:at:putOffEvent:), which the newest-N rule shares (the List and download choice, below): the records and the feeds' indexes are written and waited for on the main actor (LibraryViewModel.readyFeedFilesForRemoval(of:keepingPackages:)), the files are removed off the main thread, on the download manager's import queue (FeedDownloadManager.runBesideImports(_:)), and the outcome is settled on the main actor (settleFeedFileRemovals(_:failures:)). No removal races a download restoring an item into its kept package, though not for the thread it runs on: a due download's row is among the books, while a restore is asked for only once its row is on the feed's shelf, and the restore's import then waits on that queue behind the removal. Each removal writes FEED_DOWNLOAD_DELETED, saying whether the package was kept and which rule removed it; a failed index write puts the sweep off (FEED_SWEEP_PUT_OFF), and the newest-N rule's removals (FEED_RULE_PUT_OFF), whose passes log what they asked for (FEED_RULE_DOWNLOADS) and the downloads they left in the cloud (FEED_RULE_LEFT_IN_CLOUD); and an item whose book the library no longer lists is noted not downloaded (FEED_DOWNLOAD_FORGOTTEN); a rule's removal notes its item not downloaded only while the item still names the book it removed (FeedCenter.noteDownloadGone(_:of:at:byListener:removing:), REV11 F5), so an item asked for again meanwhile, as Open asks for one, says so for its whole transfer, as is an item In Library whose book has left the library (FEED_LIBRARY_BOOK_GONE, FR11's branch D); a book In Library is the library's own, which no rule of its feed removes. The listener's Delete Download (deleteDownload(_:of:)) and the library's Remove of a feed item (deleteDownload(ofBook:in:)) remove the file in the same order, keeping the package, but all on the main actor, through removeFeedFiles(of:keepingPackages:remove:), and take an article's content.md with the file, and Delete Episode (deleteItem(_:of:)) removes the package whole, kept or not, each following the removal setting's choice of the Trash and refusing a download in use (the feed follow-ups after FR14, item 1): FeedLibraryHost.feedBookUse(of:) says how one is in use (FeedBookUse, Models/FeedBookUse.swift: open in a reader, being transcribed, or being exported), reading the one list feedBooksInUse reads too (LibraryViewModel's feedBookUses), and the removal throws FeedDownloadInUseError, whose sentence names the download and what to do (FEED_DOWNLOAD_DELETE_REFUSED). The listener's deletions refuse rather than wait, as the rules do, since a deletion carried out minutes later, unannounced, could take a download the listener had since decided to keep; Delete Feed… refuses the same way (FeedDeleteError.downloadInUse(title:use:)), and LibraryFeedsPresenter.refusal(for:) words the alert.
  - How others observe it: the item stays in its feed's record, not downloaded, and a kept package's row on its feed's shelf; and an item deleted (deleteItem(_:of:)) stays in the record too, out of every list, so no refresh brings it back.
  - Files: Services/Feeds/FeedLifecycle.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedItemPackage.swift, Services/ReadingStatsService.swift, ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Feeds.swift, ViewModels/BookExportModel.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/FeedItemRecordTests.swift.
- The List and download choice, and the newest-N rule (FR11's branch B): which of each feed's items the library lists, and which download by themselves.
  - Owner: FeedItemPolicy (Models/FeedItemPolicy.swift), a value holding a Choice (everyListedNewestDownloaded, the default, everyListedDownloadsByHand, or onlyNewestListed, saved by those raw values) and newestCount (a number kept within SettingsPolicy.feedNewestCount, a WholeNumberSettingRule: 1 to 2,000, 5 by default), from which it derives listing (every, or newest(n), whose keptEntries, -1 or n, the refresher hands the reconciler where the retired kept-entries number went, and FeedFetchState.keptEntriesOfLastBody records) and downloading (newest(n), or byHand), so no fourth combination can be stored. The choice for every feed is FeedSettings.itemPolicy, kept in the preferences under feedItems and feedNewestCount (FeedSettings.itemsPreference and newestCountPreference, read together by FeedSettings.itemPolicy(in:)) and mirrored in config.json; a feed's own is Feed.itemPolicyOverride, nil to follow it, written into the feed's record only once set, as itemPolicyOverride holding items and newestCount, and read leniently, a value it can't read counting as the default, so one bad value never makes a record unreadable. Who asked last for an item's download is FeedItem.downloadOrigin (FeedDownloadOrigin: listener, or window, the rule's discretionary downloads), nil for an item no download has been asked for since the rule came, whose download counts as the listener's. A rule download that a network with data limits refused, or that lacks room for the size its feed gives beside the 1 GB TextMouth keeps free, waits (FeedItemState.waiting, with the reason FeedDownloadWait gives: network, or room(bytes:)), which FeedRecord.durable writes as not downloaded, so a feed's record never holds a wait; the waits for a network are kept, in order, in FeedDownloadManager.putOff, which quitting lists in this Mac's Downloads at Quit.json (downloads, above).
  - Who may write it: the Feeds tab of Settings (FeedItemPolicyControls, through SettingsViewModel.feedSettings) and config.json, for the choice for every feed; Feed Info, through FeedCenter.setItemPolicy(_:of:), which sets a feed's own, a change of which the list follows at once while the rule waits the ten seconds, and itemPolicy(of:) reads the one a feed follows. The retired settings move once. FeedSettings.moveToItemPolicyIfNeeded(in:), first thing in FeedCenter.start(library:), writes the choice from feedKeepEntries and feedAutoDownload through FeedItemPolicy.migrated(keepEntries:autoDownload:): kept as shipped, or with automatic downloads on, the default; every entry kept, everyListedDownloadsByHand; any other number n, onlyNewestListed with n. Writing the choice's key marks the move done, and a number the store already holds is kept. FeedCenter.moveKeptEntriesOverrides(), as the records load and again at each refreshAll(origin:), gives each feed whose record still holds the retired Feed.keepEntriesOverride the choice it asks for, through migrated(keepEntriesOverride:under:) (a number n, onlyNewestListed with n; every entry, the global choice while that lists every item, and everyListedDownloadsByHand otherwise), and clears it. An item becomes the listener's (downloadOrigin listener) when the listener asks for its download, even while the rule's waits or runs, cancels it (cancelDownload(_:of:)), deletes it (Delete Download, the library's Remove, and Delete Episode, through noteDownloadRemoved), or when a download the listener asked for fails.
  - How others observe it: FeedCenter.listedItems(of:) lists a feed's items by its listing (FeedRecord.listedItems(under:hasFile:): every item not deleted, or the newest n less those played without their file here, beside every item whose file is here or that downloads or waits, wherever it falls), and a feed's folder and its counts read it. The newest-N rule (Services/Feeds/FeedNewestRule.swift: FeedCenter.applyItemPolicy(to:retrying:), which returns a FeedItemPolicyPassReport) serves each feed whose choice downloads by itself: it takes back its downloads not yet under way whose items no longer count (FeedDownloadManager.withdrawRuleDownloads), asks for each of the newest n not played that it may download (ruleMayDownload(_:): never an item the listener asked for, deleted, or stopped, nor one whose download made before the rule went; and an item that failed only in a pass that follows a refresh of its feed), and, holding more of its own downloads than n, removes the files of its oldest outside the newest until it holds n, for good, through removeDue(_:from:by:at:putOffEvent:), as the after-playing sweep does (above), passing over a download the listener asked for, one on the reading list, one in use (feedBooksInUse, as the sweep), and, in a library in the cloud, one this Mac doesn't hold whole, until a later pass; a download that waits or fails removes nothing. For a feed that downloads by hand it takes back the rule's downloads not yet under way and removes nothing, and no pass runs while the library's index is being recovered. For a feed the listener unsubscribed from (FR11's branch D), whose rules stay in force while checking stops, it takes back its downloads not yet under way and asks for none, while its removals act as above, so its downloads beyond the newest still go; an item In Library counts among the newest and is never asked for (ruleDownloads, FeedItemState.libraryBookID). FeedCenter.countedItemIDs(in:under:) (Services/Feeds/FeedNewestRule.swift) defines what the rule counts: the newest n not played, while the feed is subscribed to and its choice downloads by itself. Each pass withdraws every rule download it no longer counts, those queued, put off, or waiting for room, and those that failed (FeedDownloadManager.withdrawRuleDownloads(of:where:), FeedItem.hasRulesFailedDownload; REV11 F4), so no row says Download Failed of a download nobody asked for; the download under way, and the listener's, are never taken back. Mark Played and Mark All Played request a pass for their feed, and a refresh's end requests one before it asks again for the downloads put off for a network, only those the rule still counts (retryCountedPutOffDownloads(); REV11 F3); a pass requested runs as soon as any sweep or pass under way has finished. Being offline is a wait for a network (FeedDownloadWait.network) for the rule's downloads alone, its row giving the one reason that covers both, FeedDownloadWait.network's "it downloads once this Mac is on a network without data limits", as the downloads section counts both as "2 waiting until this Mac is on a network without data limits" (FeedDownloadProgressWording.waits(_:), REV13 item 4); and a rule's article whose own text is a short summary, whose page a network with data limits or no network refuses, waits likewise rather than being written from its summary (FeedDownloadManager.waitsForANetwork(_:); feed review item 14). Passes run one at a time, after the sweep (requestItemPolicyPass(for:retryingFailures:), the passes asked for meanwhile running as one), and only in an engine that works on its own: at launch, after subscribing, after each refresh (trying failed downloads again when the refresh was not skipped, and, after one TextMouth made by itself that was not skipped, first asking again for the waits for a network, retryPutOffDownloads()), when a rule download finishes, and once a change of the choice or the number has stayed unchanged for FeedCenter.itemPolicyQuietTime, ten seconds (itemPolicyChanged(for:)); never when a reader closes, which asks for a sweep alone. An import whose item was deleted while it ran adds no row and deletes the package it had just published, for good, only inside its feed's folder (FeedDownloadManager.commit, reached at that moment in tests through beforeCommit).

- The feed reader's interface in a Library window (FR03).
  - Owner: each Library window's views. LibraryView observes only LibraryFeedFolders.shared, which knows which library folders hold feeds and which feeds the listener unsubscribed from (unsubscribedFeedIDs), and publishes only when a feed is subscribed, unsubscribed from, resubscribed, given another folder, or deleted, never for a download's progress or a refresh; the Feeds section (LibraryFeedsSection), a feed folder's rows (FeedFolderSections), and its summary bar (FeedFolderSummaryBar) observe FeedCenter themselves, so a download's start and end redraw them and nothing else of the list, and its bytes, which reach the feed downloads' progress alone (above), redraw none of them. Which feeds the section lists at a location is LibraryFeedsSectionRows.feeds(at:center:)'s rule: every feed at the top of the library and in All Items, in a folder the feeds whose folders are inside it, in Unopened a feed only while it has a new item (FeedItemCounts.new above zero, which counts the items listed that FeedCenter.isNew calls new: published, or, undated, first seen, after the subscription began, never played, and never opened, whether its file is here or its row was kept after its file went; FR11, question 8), and none in Recently Opened or the Reading List. Which folders the Folders section shows, and what VoiceOver's Folders rotor offers, is LibraryFeedFolders.folderRows(_:at:bookCount:)'s rule, which Speak Info's selection reads too: a LibraryFolderRows, the folders shown and a FolderRotorEntry, a label and the ID of a row the list shows, for each folder directly inside the location. Outside the Folders category there are neither; until October 7, 2026, the rotor listed folders there that the list didn't show. A feed's folder whose feed the Feeds section lists there has no row, the feed's row standing for it, and its rotor entry names the feed as entering its folder does and carries the feed's ID, which is that row's; any other folder keeps its row, which, for a feed's folder, FeedFolderRow draws, observing the feed engine, as FolderRow(feedCounts:), counting the feed's items, never its downloads as books, and which, for any other folder, names the feeds whose folders are below it beside its books (FolderRow's feedsBelow, from LibraryFeedFolders.feedCount(below:), FR11). In Folders the section lists every feed whose folder is inside the location, so no feed's folder keeps a row today. LibraryFeedsSection is generic over a folderMenu the window supplies, so a feed's row's context menu ends with its folder's Rename Folder… and Move Folder…: a feed's own folder holding only its items offers no Delete Folder… (LibraryViewModel.offersDeleteFolder(_:)), since Delete Feed…, among the feed's own commands, deletes the feed and, when nothing else is in it, its folder, so a feed has one command that deletes it (FR14); one that also holds books or folders offers it, its question naming the feed as staying without a folder (feedTitles(of:) fills FolderRemovalWording.ownFeedTitles). Delete Folder… on a folder holding feeds' folders at any depth asks first (LibraryViewModel.folderHasContents(_:)), its two questions naming each feed and what becomes of it (FolderRemovalWording.feedTitles, from feedTitles(inside:)), and never deletes a feed: Move Contents Out moves the feed's folder with the rest, and Delete Everything leaves the feed, with its downloads, without a folder until Feed Info gives it another. LibraryFeedsPresenter, one for each window, kept in @State and never observed, runs the commands (FeedCommandRunner.swift) and holds the sheets (Add Feed, Feed Info, an item's description, and Import OPML), the questions asked before a command that can't be undone, and the notices that follow the commands, which LibraryFeedsWindowSupport shows on the window, applying its parts in turn, each type-checked on its own (body(content:): withArrivalsAlert, withMenuCommands, withSheets, and withAlerts).
  - Who may write it: the listener, through the rows' context menus and VoiceOver actions, under the same names: FeedAction, for a feed, on its row and on its folder's row, where it has one, and FeedItemAction, for an item, on its row or, beside the book's own commands, on its book's row. LibraryFeedsWindowSupport also takes the Library menu's Add Feed… (Shift-Command-F unless changed, ShortcutAction.addFeed, a menu command's key that works in Library windows), Refresh All Feeds, Import OPML…, and Export OPML…, which post addFeed, refreshAllFeeds, importOPML, and exportOPML, for the window that takes commands (WindowCommandRouting, through LibraryView's takesCommand(_:)). Subscribing makes the feed's folder only once the feed has been read: FeedCenter.subscribe(to:into:), with FeedFolderChoice.newFolder(inParentPath:), asks FeedLibraryHost.makeFeedFolder, which LibraryViewModel answers with createFolderReturningFolder, naming the folder after the feed and numbering it while the name is taken (FeedFolderNaming); subscribe(to:folderID:) remains for an existing folder. Mark All Played changes one record (FeedCenter.markAllPlayed(of:)). Unsubscribe asks first when it would delete what the feed kept for removed downloads (FR14): LibraryFeedsPresenter.requestUnsubscribe(_:in:) decides as it is chosen, from FeedCenter.keptRowsUnsubscribeDeletes(of:), the kept rows but those whose items wait in the download queue or download, unsubscribing at once when there are none and otherwise returning the question; FeedItemCounts.keptForRemovedDownloads gives the menus the same answer, titling the command "Unsubscribe…", destructive, or "Unsubscribe" (FeedAction.actions(for:counts:), FeedWording.unsubscribeTitle(asksFirst:)), and Feed Info's one button follows the feed's state, Unsubscribe, Unsubscribe…, or Resubscribe, its title, role, and hint from FeedSubscriptionToggle, so the button VoiceOver is on stays as the state changes (REV13 item 2). Unsubscribing stops checking and keeps the feed (FeedCenter.unsubscribe(_:deletingKeptOf:), FR11's branch D: Feed.unsubscribedDate set; the rule's downloads not yet under way withdrawn, FeedDownloadManager.withdrawRuleDownloads, while the one under way and the listener's go on; and the packages kept for removed downloads deleted through FeedCenter.deleteKeptPackages(_:of:), in Services/Feeds/FeedLifecycle.swift, as Delete Episode deletes one, the Trash or for good as the removal setting says, a package whose item downloads again staying; FEED_UNSUBSCRIBED), and returns a FeedUnsubscribeOutcome, which FeedWording.unsubscribed(title:outcome:keptParts:noun:) words, naming the parts the kept packages held as keptRowsUnsubscribeDeletes(of:) gave them just before (FeedWording.KeptParts: place, bookmarks, own settings, transcript; REV13 item 5); its question is FeedWording.unsubscribeMessage(downloadCount:keptCount:keptParts:noun:), counting and naming the rows keptRowsUnsubscribeDeletes(of:) gives as it is asked, and Feed Info stays open across it. FeedConfirmation.Kind.unsubscribe(feedID:keptItemIDs:) carries the items the question named; LibraryFeedsPresenter.unsubscribe(_:deletingKeptOf:in:afterAlert:) and FeedCenter.unsubscribe(_:deletingKeptOf:) delete only those, the rows decided by keptRowsUnsubscribeDeletes(of:among:) before withdrawRuleDownloads, through deleteKeptPackages(_:of:). Feed Info's onUnsubscribe passes the confirmed question to confirm(_:in:). What a confirmed command did is said once its alert has closed, VoiceOverAnnouncer.afterSheetDelay later, and at once when nothing was asked (LibraryFeedsPresenter's say(_:afterAlert:in:), through VoiceOverAnnouncer.afterSheetCloses(_:); REV13 item 1): Unsubscribe, Delete Episode, and Delete Feed…. Delete Feed…, last among a feed's commands (FeedAction.actions(for:noun:isSubscribed:unsubscribeAsks:)) and in Feed Info, asks first (deleteFeedConfirmation(for:in:): FeedWording.deleteFeedQuestion(title:) and deleteFeedMessage(downloads:kept:keptParts:noun:folderPath:mode:), naming what goes, to the Trash or for good, the folder that goes when nothing else is in it, and Move to Library… for a download to keep), then runs FeedCenter.deleteFeed(_:) (feed records, above) through deleteFeed(_:in:), which first closes Feed Info should it show the feed; its FeedDeleteOutcome is worded by FeedWording.deletedFeed(_:noun:keptParts:), with keptParts(ofFeed:in:) serving both the question and the announcement, and a FeedDeleteError by deleteFeedRefused(_:title:mode:) in an alert (the presenter's notice) alone: refuse(message:) and the OPML notices set notice only, as an alert speaks for itself. Resubscribe (FeedCenter.resubscribe(_:)) clears the date and checks the feed at once, as Check Feed does (FEED_RESUBSCRIBED, FeedWording.resubscribed(title:)); FeedAction.actions(for:in:) offers one or the other by the feed's state, and Check Feed only while subscribed. Add Feed with an unsubscribed feed's address is refused with FeedSubscribeError.unsubscribedAlready, which points to Resubscribe. Delete Episode's question (deleteItemMessage(isArticle:keepsListenerState:isInLibrary:)) says when the kept place goes too, or, for an item In Library, that its book stays (itemDeleted(title:isInLibrary:) says so again after). FeedOPML reads OPML with XMLParser, loading no external entity, reads a damaged file again through XMLDocument's tidying, and writes OPML 2.0; OPMLImportModel subscribes to each feed it lists in turn, as Add Feed does, and names apart those in the library already that the listener unsubscribed from, leaving them unsubscribed (FeedWording.importFoundUnsubscribed(_:)); Export OPML writes only the feeds subscribed to, saying how many it left out (FeedWording.exported(count:fileName:unsubscribedCount:), or nothingSubscribedToExport when none is left).
  - How others observe it: FeedWording words every row, sheet, command, and announcement, each label made by a function the hosted tests pin, since SwiftUI builds the spoken labels of its own text and buttons only for a connected assistive app. FeedWording.countsPhrase is the one count of a feed's items: its size first (sizePhrase(listed:total:noun:), the items listed, FeedItemCounts.listed, of FeedItemCounts.total, with the word FeedWording.listedNote: "10 of 381 episodes listed", "10 of more than 2000 episodes listed", or, while every entry is listed or the size is unknown, "12 episodes"), then the items downloaded, downloading, and new, and last those not played (unplayedNote: "unread" for articles), each count of 0 left out but the size. The Feeds rows, FeedCenter.folderSummary for the summary bar, entering the folder, Feed Info's items line, Speak Info (LibraryInfoWording adds nothing of its own), FolderRow(feedCounts:), and the Folders rotor all say it, and FeedWording.subscribed(title:counts:) says the size alone. A feed the listener unsubscribed from says so (FeedItemCounts.isSubscribed): "unsubscribed" after Feed on its row, its folder's row, entering its folder, and the Folders rotor (FeedWording.unsubscribedNote, also an Unsubscribed badge on screen), "Unsubscribed" in Speak Info, and its last check as not checked since then (FeedWording.check(_:isRefreshing:isSubscribed:now:calendar:locale:), "Not checked since October 9 at 3:05 PM"); Feed Info adds the date it was unsubscribed (unsubscribedLabel) and offers Resubscribe in place of Unsubscribe. An item In Library says "In Library" (FeedItemState.visibleNote), and its row's hint is "Opens its book in your library" (itemRowHint(_:keptPlace:)). FeedWording.sectionTitle(_:count:total:) heads an open feed's items, "Episodes (10 of 381 listed)", given FeedFolderListing.total, which is nil while a search narrows them: "Episodes (2)". An item's row content (FeedItemRowContent) carries the item's kept row as FeedKeptProgress (from feedItemRows(of:).kept, by item identity): its progress and time left in the book row's own words (BookRow.progressText, spokenProgress, remainingText, and timeRemaining(for:wpm:), which are nonisolated), so a kept item at its end says "Finished" alone, unless the item is played, and its bookmark count (CountWording.bookmarks); itemRowLabel adds FeedItemState.reason, a failure's or a wait's, and the kept parts, and itemRowHint(_:keptPlace:) gives the hint. FeedFolderSections and FeedFolderListing.make take SettingsViewModel.wordsPerMinute, at which book rows estimate an article's time left. FeedItemAction.actions offers Download and Cancel Download for a waiting item, and the runner words a waiting item's cancel (waitingDownloadCancelled(title:)) and a kept package's Delete Download (downloadDeleted(title:kept:), naming only what the kept package holds, FeedWording.KeptParts.of(_:package:); feed review item 18). Show Description's Close answers Escape through it while its one SwiftUI shortcut is Return (the default action): a SwiftUI button carries one shortcut, and onExitCommand, sent from an AppKit text view's responder chain, never reaches SwiftUI (measured in a hosted test). Any sheet with one button can use it, and the report of a speech refusal (the reader, above) uses both it and FeedReadOnlyText. A feed's folder's row, where it has one, and its rotor entry say the note Feed and the feed's count (FolderRow.accessibilityLabel(name:bookCount:feedCounts:), which is FeedWording.folderAnnouncement(name:counts:)), as entering the folder announces it (FolderNavigationHistory.announcement(for:library:feedFolders:)).

- Searching the podcast directory (FR03): the Add Feed sheet's search.
  - Owner: AddFeedModel, one for each Add Feed sheet, on the main actor: the words typed (searchTerm), the podcasts the last search found (searchResults, each a FeedDirectoryResult: its title, publisher, episode count, and feed), and what the sheet says when it found none (searchNote) or failed (searchFailure).
  - Who may write it: search(), from the Search button or Return in the Search field, through FeedCenter.searchDirectory(for:) and the directory the engine's environment names (FeedEnvironment.directory: FeedDirectory.apple, Apple's iTunes Search API, or a test's stub server). FeedDirectory asks for podcasts alone, at most 50 (media=podcast, entity=podcast, limit=50), with the term percent-encoded beyond the unreserved characters, through the feed engine's connections, as a request the listener made, on any network, and reads the answer through FeedJSON (results(in:)), keeping each podcast that names a web feed once (FeedAddress.comparisonKey). A failure is a FeedSearchError, worded by FeedFetchError.searchMessage, as when the directory refuses more than about 20 searches a minute. subscribe(to:) puts a result's feed in the address field and subscribes as Subscribe does.
  - How others observe it: AddFeedSheet lists the results, each read as one line beside a Subscribe button of its own, and VoiceOver hears how many were found, or why none were (FeedWording).

  - Owner: FeedArrivals.shared, on the main actor: the alert waiting to be shown (pendingAlert, a FeedArrivalAlert), and the Library window showing it (alertWindow).
  - Who may write it: FeedArrivals itself, from FeedCenter.events. A refresh of every feed that TextMouth made by itself (FeedRefreshOrigin.isDiscretionary: at launch or on the interval) and that brought new items makes the alert, while feedRefreshAlert is on, and posts a notification for each feed through its FeedNotifying, while feedNotifications is on and notifications are allowed. A refresh the listener asked for is answered where it was asked, by VoiceOver (FeedWording.refreshedAll and FeedWording.checked). Turning notifications on asks permission the first time (FeedArrivals.settingTurnedOn). FeedArrivals also speaks FeedEvent.itemPolicyApplied(global:feedIDs:), which FeedCenter.itemPolicyChanged(for:) sends as its ten-second wait ends, after a change in Settings, Feed Info, or config.json: FeedWording.itemPolicyApplied(global:feedIDs:in:) gives the global setting's sentence when it changed, then one for each feed whose own changed within that wait that the library still holds, an unsubscribed one included, since its rules still act (feed review item 20; FeedCenter keeps globalItemPolicyChanged apart from the feeds changed, so neither hides the other); its announce is injectable, for the tests.
  - How others observe it: the first Library window in front to ask takes the alert (claimAlert(by:isKeyWindow:)), so it shows once, and waits while no Library window is in front; dismissing it forgets it. SystemFeedNotifier posts through the system's notification center, whose delegate (FeedNotificationPresenter) shows banners while TextMouth is in front too; a process hosting tests uses InertFeedNotifier, which posts nothing and never asks.

### Recordings

- A take being recorded (FR04).
  - Owner: RecordingService, on the main actor, one for each Record window and each audio editor window, recording one take at a time.
