Its capture backend, CaptureSessionRecordingBackend, owns the AVCaptureSession, the microphone's input, and the AVCaptureAudioFileOutput, which writes the take into a CAF file in this Mac's Recordings folder (MachineLocalPaths.recordings), named for when it started: 16-bit PCM, one channel, at the microphone's own sample rate, or, should capture refuse that, in the microphone's own PCM format (RECORDING_FORMAT_FALLBACK). A CAF file stays readable when a crash cuts the recording off.
  - Who may write it: RecordingWindowModel, through start, pause, resume, stop, discard, and reset, and an audio editor's punch-in (AudioEditorModel+PunchIn), which plays a count-in before capture begins and keeps the take as an edit. AudioInputPermission.ensureAccess() runs first, so a refusal records nothing.
  - How others observe it: the service publishes its state (idle, starting, recording, paused, stopping, finished with its take, or failed with why and the take, if any), the time recorded, pauses left out, and the levels, read every tenth of a second while a take is under way; the window shows them once a second. A lost microphone, a disk with less than 512 MB free (minimumFreeDiskSpace), or a failed capture arrives as a RecordingCaptureEvent: the take ends, kept, and the window says why and saves it. RecordingWording words everything the window shows and speaks, its buttons' hints naming their keys in force (keyboard shortcuts, under preferences, above). A quit asks first: AppDelegate calls RecordingWindowController.holdQuit after the data-directory move and before the audio editors. While the window holds a take (recording, paused, starting, or unsaved), the quit is cancelled and the window, brought forward, asks its close question (CloseQuestion, CloseAnswer); carryOut does the answer (stopAndSave or discard) before TextMouth asks to quit again. A question already on screen answers the quit too, a save that fails keeps TextMouth open, and a start or save under way is waited out (settle). Tests hand in askQuestion and bringForward.
  - Files: Services/RecordingService.swift, Services/RecordingCaptureBackend.swift, Services/RecordingTake.swift, Services/AudioInputPermission.swift, ViewModels/RecordingWindowModel.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWindowView.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWording.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWindowController.swift, App/AppDelegate.swift.
- Takes left behind (FR04): takes in this Mac's Recordings folder that no window holds, as a crash, a forced quit, or a save that failed after its window closed leaves them.
  - Owner: the folder itself. RecordingTakeFiles.leftovers lists, oldest first, the take files no window holds, as RecoveredTake values, each with its start, read from its name, and its length, read from the file (readLeftovers, off the main thread). A take is held while any live recorder has it, starting, under way, or ended and not yet handed on (RecordingService.takesInUse, a weak list of every recorder, the Record window's and each audio editor's), or while the Record window saves it or keeps it to save again (RecordingWindowModel.ownedTakes).
  - Who may write it: the Record window, which saves one through Stop's save, titled for when it was recorded, or discards it with any encoding beside it, only while nothing records, starts, or is being saved, and no take waits to be saved again (canHandleRecoveredTakes). Nothing deletes them on its own.
  - How others observe it: the window lists them whenever it opens, saying once how many. RecordingWindowController looks once, as the first Library window attaches the library, and opens the window on them, VoiceOver on the first one's Save as Audiobook (RECORDING_TAKES_RECOVERED; never in a test host). A recovered take's origin.json names no microphone, since its file doesn't say which recorded it.
  - Files: Services/RecordingTake.swift, Services/RecordingService.swift, ViewModels/RecordingWindowModel.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWindowController.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWindowView.swift, Views/Recording/RecordingWording.swift.
- Microphones, and which one records.
  - Owner: SystemRecordingInputCatalog, which lists the microphones (an AVCaptureDevice.DiscoverySession of microphones and external devices, less the virtual and aggregate devices, which carry other apps' sound) and hears them connect and disconnect, and RecordingInputChoice, which owns recordingInputDeviceID through StoredPreference and resolves it: the remembered microphone when it is connected, otherwise macOS's default input, never written in its place, so the remembered one is used again once it is back. Microphones of the same name are numbered (distinctlyNamed).
  - Who may write it: RecordingWindowModel.chooseDevice, from the Microphone pop-up, AudioEditorModel.chooseMicrophone(_:), from the editor's Microphone for Punch In, and config.json.
  - How others observe it: the Record window lists the microphones and says when it falls back to another; while nothing records, it also says when one connects: that recordings now use it, the remembered one or the first at all, or that others are in the pop-up (refreshDevices(announcingChanges:)); while a take is under way, the list still follows, the microphone in use stays, and the recorder reports a lost one itself.
  - Files: Services/RecordingInputDevices.swift, Services/RecordingPreferences.swift, ViewModels/RecordingWindowModel.swift.
- A saved recording.
  - Owner: the library, as for any import. RecordingWindowModel.librarySaver has RecordingEncoder write the take as an M4A beside it (AAC, one channel, at the highest bit rate up to 64 kbit/s the encoder accepts at the take's sample rate, the title tagged), then LibraryViewModel.saveRecording runs FileImportService.importRecording on the import queue, as storage work: the package is staged and published with one rename, holding the M4A, original-take.caf when the original policy keeps one (audio edits, above), and origin.json (a recording's origin record, above), and the book, whose author is Unknown, is added as any import's book is.
  - Who may write it: the Record window, on Stop, on Save Again, when a take ends on its own, and on a take left behind's Save as Audiobook (takes left behind, above).
  - How others observe it: the window announces the saved book and offers Open in Reader. The take and its encoding then leave the Recordings folder; a save that fails keeps the take. Each save writes a RECORDING_SAVED line and starts a sweep of the originals (AudioOriginalSweep).
  - Files: ViewModels/LibraryViewModel+Recording.swift, Services/RecordingEncoder.swift, Services/FileImportService+Recording.swift, ViewModels/RecordingWindowModel.swift.

### Transcription

- A transcription in progress (FR05).
  - Owner: TranscriptionService.shared, an actor that runs one transcription at a time: a second is refused with TranscriptionError.busy, naming the book under way. It checks the language and its model before reading any audio (TranscriptionEngine.prepare(locale:)), plans the book's audio off the main thread (TranscriptionPlan: each file once, in the order the book plays them, measured, refusing a book with a file only in the cloud before measuring any), and transcribes file by file, fingerprinting each file just before it is read, through a TranscriptionEngine. SystemTranscriptionEngine uses SpeechAnalyzer's SpeechTranscriber on macOS 26: each file handed over as an AVAudioFile, only final results asked for, with their time ranges and confidence, and the model kept loaded between files (lingering); a file refused because its language isn't reserved is tried once more after reserving it, which downloads nothing. On an earlier system it refuses, saying that transcription needs macOS 26. TranscriptSentenceBuilder turns the results into sentences with the system's sentence tokenizer. A language nobody chose, the system's English, gives way to US English when the recognizer doesn't support it (TranscriptionLanguage.languagesToTry(in:)); a chosen one never does.
  - Who may write it: the caller of transcribe(_:language:progress:), which returns the Transcript and writes nothing, or of transcribeAndSave(_:language:currentBook:progress:), which saves it (transcripts, above) into the book as the library holds it once the transcription is done, while the service still counts as busy. Cancelling the task that awaits either stops the recognizer within a moment (cancelAndFinishNow) and throws TranscriptionError.cancelled, and nothing is written before the transcript is saved. In the app the caller is TranscriptionJobCenter (below), which calls transcribe and saves the transcript itself; transcribeAndSave serves the fixture tests (TranscriptionFixtureTests).
  - How others observe it: TranscriptionProgress (the file's index and the count of files, the seconds done and in all, and the time elapsed) arrives on no particular thread as each file starts and as results arrive. TranscriptionAvailability answers what this Mac offers without downloading a model or reserving a language: whether the system can transcribe, the supported and installed languages, and each language's model status, deciding that a model is installed by the recognizer's own list. Every TranscriptionError has a sentence ready to show and speak. The language is TranscriptionLanguage.preference (transcriptionLocale), mirrored in config.json.
  - Files: Services/TranscriptionService.swift, Services/TranscriptionEngine.swift, Services/TranscriptionAvailability.swift, Services/TranscriptSentenceBuilder.swift, Models/TranscriptionLanguage.swift.
- The transcription the app runs, for every window (FR05).
  - Owner: TranscriptionJobCenter.shared, on the main actor: current, the TranscriptionJob under way (the book, its title, when it began, and its progress as last shown), and lastEnding, how the latest one ended (finished, with its word count and seconds; cancelled; or failed, with why).
  - Who may write it: start(_:language:library:window:), from a Transcribe Audio sheet, which starts nothing while a transcription is under way. It transcribes off the main thread (TranscriptionService.transcribe), then, with the book as the library holds it by then, ends the book's Edit Text session (BookTextEditor.end(_:)) and saves the transcript through TranscriptStore.install on a global queue (OffMainWork.runThrowing). cancel(), from the sheet's Cancel and from AppDelegate.applicationWillTerminate, stops it, leaving the book as it was unless its transcript was being saved already.
  - How others observe it: the sheets showing it observe current, which takes the progress at most every 250 milliseconds (progressInterval), and at once when it moves to another file or is done, and lastEnding. VoiceOver hears the start, each quarter it passes (TranscriptionWording.milestones), and the end, from the window that started it, and the debug log records its start, with the language, and its end, with the words found and the seconds taken, or why it failed (TranscriptionLog), naming the book by its title and never quoting a word heard. Once the transcript is saved, TranscribedBooks records the book, and LibraryBookUpdates.transcriptReplaced brings the text to its readers.
  - Files: ViewModels/TranscriptionJobCenter.swift, Models/TranscriptionWording.swift, App/AppDelegate.swift.
- The Transcribe Audio sheet, and each window's transcription dialogs.
  - Owner: TranscriptionPresenter, one for each Library window (LibraryTranscriptionSupport) and each reader (kept by ReaderView, which hands it to ReaderBookCommandsSupport and to SpeechRefusalReportPresenter, whose report waits while one of its dialogs shows, isShowingAny), which holds the Transcribe Audio sheet showing, the Clean Up sheet showing (a transcript's cleanup, below), the confirmation before Delete Transcript (TranscriptDeletionRequest), and a notice saying why a command did nothing, all shown by TranscriptionPresentation; and TranscriptionSheetModel, one for each sheet, in its phase (checking, unavailable, ready, transcribing, or failed): the book, the language resolved as TranscriptionService resolves it (TranscriptionLanguage.languagesToTry(in:)), its model's status, a download under way, the book's transcript as transcript.json holds it, and another book being transcribed. TranscriptionSheetServices is everything the sheet asks of the system and the app, which tests replace.
  - Who may write it: the commands. A library row offers LibraryAudioCommand.commands(for:isTranscribed:) in its context menu and as its VoiceOver actions: Edit Audio… first, for one audiobook in a package of its own alone, never a feed's episode (LibraryAudioCommand.isAudioEditable(_:), which AudioEditorLauncher asks too), which TranscriptionPresenter.perform(_:library:window:) hands to AudioEditorLauncher (the editor window, above); then Transcribe Audio… when one of the books it acts on is an audiobook in a package of its own, and, for one transcribed audiobook alone, Clean Up Transcript (Experimental)…, where LibraryAudioCommand.isTranscriptCleanable allows (the isAudioEditable rule, never a feed's episode), and Delete Transcript…; the reader enables the Book menu's Clean Up by the same rule (canCleanUpBookTranscript). The Book menu's Transcribe Audio…, Clean Up Transcript (Experimental)…, and Delete Transcript… post transcribeBookAudio, cleanUpBookTranscript, and deleteBookTranscript to the reader that takes them. An offer accepted after an import opens the sheet too (import questions, above). TranscriptionRequest refuses several books, or a book that isn't an audiobook, with a sentence. A model downloads only when Download is pressed, through TranscriptionModelInstalling (SystemTranscriptionModelInstaller, the asset inventory's installation request on macOS 26), and closing the sheet stops a download.
  - How others observe it: TranscriptionSheet shows the model, moving VoiceOver and keyboard focus to its first control (focusTarget, through focusing(_:first:_:)), then announcing its lines once for each phase; TranscriptionWording words everything shown and spoken. A deletion the user confirms ends the book's Edit Text session and runs TranscriptStore.delete(for:) on a global queue, then records the book as untranscribed and tells its readers (LibraryBookUpdates.transcriptReplaced, with no text); while the book is being transcribed, a notice says to cancel first.
  - Files: ViewModels/TranscriptionSheetModel.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptionSheet.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptionPresentation.swift, Services/TranscriptionModelInstaller.swift, Models/LibraryAudioCommand.swift, Models/TranscriptionWording.swift, Views/Reader/ReaderBookCommands.swift, Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift.
- Which audiobooks are transcribed.
  - Owner: TranscribedBooks.shared, on the main actor: the books with a transcript (bookIDs), and the one being transcribed (transcribingBookID). A book's record notes its transcript in its package alone, never in library.json, so a library row can't carry it.
  - Who may write it: refresh(_:), which looks for each audiobook's transcript.json off the main thread, reading and downloading nothing, as a Library window appears, as the number of books changes, and as TextMouth becomes active (LibraryTranscriptionSupport), when another Mac sharing the library may have transcribed a book; record(_:isTranscribed:), when a transcription saves a transcript or a deletion removes one, which wins over a look begun before it; and setTranscribing(_:), from TranscriptionJobCenter.
  - How others observe it: each row's note (TranscriptionRowNote, Transcribed or Transcribing, drawn after the cloud notes by RowNoteBadge and spoken in the row's label), its transcription commands, and whether Edit Text is offered for an audiobook (BookTextEditor.canEditText(of:)), in its row and in the Book menu.
  - Files: ViewModels/TranscribedBooks.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptionPresentation.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift, Views/Library/BookCloudNote.swift, Services/BookTextEditor.swift.
- A transcript's cleanup (FR05, experimental): the Clean Up sheet, and what it would change.
  - Owner: TranscriptCleanupSheetModel, one for each Clean Up sheet, on the main actor, in its phase (checking, unavailable, ready, working, or failed): the rules in use and where they came from (TranscriptCleanupRuleFile.Lookup: transcript cleanup rules, below), what they would change (TranscriptCleanup.Preview: the transcript as read, the transcript cleaned, each rule's corrections with up to three examples, and how many sentences changed), and whether Undo Cleanup is offered: the transcript records a cleanup and transcript.json.before-cleanup is there, by a look that never downloads. TranscriptCleanup applies the rules in order to each sentence: a whole-word rule matches whole words, in any case and with any apostrophe, keeping the punctuation around them and a possessive after them, and any other rule matches inside words too, the replacement taking the case of what it replaces; it reads and writes nothing. TranscriptCleanupServices is everything the sheet asks of the app, which tests replace.
  - Who may write it: TranscriptionPresenter.cleanUp(_:library:window:), for a row's command or the Book menu's, opens one sheet at a time in a window. The model reads the rules and the transcript off the main thread as the sheet opens (load()), and again whenever TextMouth becomes active (refresh()), as after the user edits the rules, VoiceOver hearing the sheet's lines again when anything changed. Apply and Undo Cleanup refuse while the book is being transcribed (TranscriptionJobCenter.current), end the book's Edit Text session (BookTextEditor.end(_:)), and run TranscriptStore.cleanUp or undoCleanup (transcripts, above) off the main thread (OffMainWork.runThrowing), on the book as the library holds it then.
  - How others observe it: TranscriptCleanupSheet shows the model, moving VoiceOver and keyboard focus to Apply when the rules change something and to Cancel otherwise (focusTarget, through focusing(_:first:_:)), then announcing its lines once for each phase; TranscriptCleanupWording words everything shown and spoken. A cleanup or an undo saved reaches the book's readers through LibraryBookUpdates.transcriptReplaced, and VoiceOver hears it from the sheet's window.
  - Files: ViewModels/TranscriptCleanupSheetModel.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptCleanupSheet.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptCleanupCommands.swift, Views/Transcription/TranscriptionPresentation.swift, Services/TranscriptCleanup.swift, Services/TranscriptCleanupStore.swift, Models/TranscriptCleanupWording.swift, Models/LibraryAudioCommand.swift.
- Transcript cleanup rules (FR05, experimental): TextMouth's own, and the user's copy.
  - Owner: TranscriptCleanupRuleFile. TextMouth's own rules are transcript-cleanup.json in the app's bundle (Resources/transcript-cleanup.json: format 1, version 1 of 2026-10-04, its rule list empty, with an about and two worked examples that nothing applies); the user's copy, transcript-cleanup.json at the data directory's root beside substitutions.json (TextMouthPaths.transcriptCleanupRulesFile), is used instead whenever it is there and usable. TranscriptCleanupRuleSet holds a file's format, version, date, and rules (TranscriptCleanupRule: pattern, replacement, wholeWord, evidence, and note), and refuses a file, naming the first field at fault and never quoting a rule, whose format isn't 1, whose version is below 1, whose date isn't a day written as 2026-10-04, or one of whose rules has an empty pattern or replacement, a pattern without a letter or figure, a whole-word pattern with a word of punctuation alone, negative evidence, or a replacement its own pattern matches, which every cleanup would change again.
  - Who may write it: the user, in their own editor. TextMouth writes the copy only through prepareUserCopy, for the Clean Up sheet's Edit Rules…, as storage work, and only when there is none: it writes its own file there exactly, staged beside it and moved into place, which fails rather than replace a copy that arrived meanwhile. A copy that is there, usable or not, or only in the cloud, is never written over, and nothing is written while the data directory is unavailable.
  - How others observe it: load(userCopyAt:reader:builtIn:), off the main thread, as the sheet opens and as TextMouth becomes active, reads the copy as substitutions.json is read (SavedFile, with dataless files left in the cloud): a copy only in the cloud is asked of the sync service, and one that can't be read or used is preserved beside itself (SavedFile.preserveUnusable), TextMouth's own rules standing in for either while the sheet says why; while the data directory is unavailable, nothing is read and TextMouth's own rules are used. A TRANSCRIPT_CLEANUP_RULES line tells of each wait and preserved copy, and the sheet says when TextMouth's own rules have a newer version than the copy. A cleaned transcript records the rules' version, date, and source. TextMouth's own rules were derived with TranscriptAccuracy from speech whose text is known (TRANSCRIPT-CLEANUP-RESEARCH-2026-10-04.md), which the opt-in TranscriptAccuracyMeasurementTests measure again.
  - Files: Services/TranscriptCleanupRuleFile.swift, Models/TranscriptCleanupRules.swift, Resources/transcript-cleanup.json, Services/TranscriptAccuracy.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/TranscriptAccuracyMeasurementTests.swift.

### Exports

- An export (FR05): what the Export dialog offers, what is checked, where it goes, and how far it has got.
  - Owner: BookExportModel, one for each Export dialog, on the main actor: the books, what they offer (BookExportOffer), a choice for each kind any of them holds (BookExportChoice), the kinds checked, the destination, and the phase (listing, ready, exporting, or finished). The destination starts at the folder last exported to this session, kept only in memory, or else at Documents, in the home TextMouthRuntime names, so a test exports into its sandbox.
  - Who may write it: the dialog's controls, and BookExportService, which never downloads (MaintenanceFileAccess). offer(for:access:) lists, off the main thread, what a book's package holds, from what the file system says: for a folder of tracks it also reads the saved content index (audioFileOrder(listed:index:): the files in the order the chapters and their clips play them, then any others in Finder's order), and for a DAISY book kept as loose files its inventory (DAISYPackageInventory). It lists, too, the originals the audio editor keeps in the package's audioEdits/originals folder (AudioEditPackage.folder, originalsFolderName) as BookExportKind.originalAudio: one file as "<title> - Original Audio" with its own extension, or a book's tracks' originals in a folder of that name, each under its path in the book's audio folder, copied as they are; Export never makes one, and a recording's take stays its Original recording (originalRecording). plan(_:kinds:in:fileExists:) names every file: one book's items go in the destination, its tracks and a loose DAISY book's files in a folder named after it; several books get a folder each; and a name already taken is followed by a number (UniqueNames, compared without case), so nothing is replaced. When Export is pressed, the model lists the books again as the library holds them, and runs export(_:kinds:to:access:cancellation:progress:) off the main thread. It leaves out books and items only in the cloud, copies each file with dataless files left in the cloud, and writes the plain text (plainText(fromMarkdown:at:title:): the chapters as the reader parses them) without replacing anything. It removes any partial file it made, and stops before its next file once Stop is pressed (BookExportCancellation). A destination inside the data directory is refused (BookExportModel.isInsideDataDirectory).
  - How others observe it: BookExportSheet shows the model, VoiceOver's focus on its first checkbox. LibraryExportSupport presents it on a Library window, for the selection File > Export… finds or the books a row's context menu acts on, and ReaderBookCommandsSupport on a reader, for its book, from File > Export… or the toolbar's Export button. The end is announced once (BookExportWording.finished); the dialog closes when everything was exported (didEverything), and keeps its summary otherwise. Each export writes one BOOK_EXPORT line, and each file that couldn't be written a BOOK_EXPORT_FAILED line.
  - Files: Services/BookExportService.swift, ViewModels/BookExportModel.swift, Models/BookExportWording.swift, Models/LibraryCloudWording.swift, Views/Export/BookExportSheet.swift, Views/Export/BookExportPresentation.swift, Views/Reader/ReaderBookCommands.swift, Views/Library/LibraryView.swift, Views/Library/LibrarySubviews.swift, App/MenuCommands.swift.

### Logs

- The diagnostic logs.
  - Owner: DiagnosticLog, the one policy for what a log line may say and which lines are written. It alone calls TextMouthPaths.appendLog, which hands each line, by its log's name (DiagnosticLog.File), to LogWriter.shared. The writer writes on one serial queue, never blocking or crashing the app, opens each file for its line only, and keeps each file to two generations of at most 2 MB: a full log becomes its .previous by one rename(2) that replaces the older generation in the same step; a rename refused twice, as a File Provider folder can refuse one, starts the log over in place with a LOG_ROTATION line; and a log only in the cloud is never downloaded (every write runs inside DatalessFiles.withoutDownloading), but kept as the older generation while a fresh file starts.
  - Where they are: TextMouthPaths.logDestination(for:dataDirectory:thisMacsFolder:isUnavailable:), the one resolution of the logs' folder, which the writer runs for every line (LogWriter.Environment.app) and Storage settings run to show it. Under the default choice (LogLocation.dataDirectory) it is the data directory's Logs folder, in the current storage generation's root; under the other (LogLocation.machine), this Mac's ~/Library/Logs/TextMouth (MachineLocalPaths.logs). While the data directory can't take lines (its gate is closed, its folder or its Logs folder is missing, as FolderState reads them without downloading, or a write there fails), lines go to this Mac's folder with the reason (LogDestination, LogFallback), and back once it can. Each time lines change place, the writer begins the debug log they arrive in with one LOG_LOCATION line saying why, a change of the choice included. It never makes a folder in the data directory: ensureDirectoryStructure makes Logs with the data directory's other folders, so a line never brings back a data directory that is gone. A move copies Logs with the rest, and lines go into the moved folder once the move publishes; a line written during the copy, after Logs was copied, stays in the old folder, which the move leaves where it was.
  - Who may write it: any code, through DiagnosticLog.event, for a line that is always written and echoed to the console, or DiagnosticLog.trace, for routine lines written only while tracing is on and at most 20 a second in each category; DiagnosticLog.log takes the level as an argument. Every call names its category: playback, focus, import, storage, statistics, feeds, or app. The app category, for the app as a whole, holds the BUILD_IDENTITY line written at launch (which also goes to the unified log) and LibraryWindowRouter's routing, whose failures are events and whose routine waits are trace. Trace lines are marked with their category. A line goes to the debug log unless it is one of the sleep timer's or speech's own (DiagnosticLog.File). Lines never hold book text or substitution rules, name files by their place inside the data directory or this Mac's own TextMouth folder (ImportScratchSpace.loggablePath), name a data directory by its folder and the one holding it (DataDirectoryPaths.shortName(of:)), name a feed by its host, never its whole address, which can carry a private feed's token, and give book titles only in event lines. LogContentPolicyTests scans the source, read through AppSourceFile (TextMouthProductionTests/SourceScanTestSupport.swift) as UncheckedConcurrencyPolicyTests reads it, for calls that break these rules, and fails when any file but DiagnosticLog.swift calls TextMouthPaths.appendLog, or any file but TextMouthPaths.swift calls LogWriter.shared.append; no file is exempt from either check. A CONTENT_INDEX_REJECTED line carries the reason BookContentIndex gives, out of the scan's sight, which names a relabeled heading by where its labels first differ and how long each is, never by its words (alignmentFailure(with:countingWords:)).
  - How others observe it: by reading textmouth_debug.log, textmouth_timer.log, and textmouth_transition.log in the folder in use. Storage settings' Logs group (LogsSettingsSection, worded by LogsSettingsWording) names that folder, says why while the logs fall back, and opens it in Finder (TextMouthPaths.preparedLogsFolder). These groups of events have tags of their own, which LogContentPolicyTests pins with every other event tag:
    - The logs themselves: LOG_LOCATION, each time lines change place, and LOG_ROTATION, for a log started over.
    - The book service's downloads and removals: CLOUD_DOWNLOAD_STARTED, CLOUD_DOWNLOAD_FINISHED, CLOUD_DOWNLOAD_FAILED, CLOUD_DOWNLOAD_STOPPED, CLOUD_DOWNLOAD_REMOVED, and CLOUD_DOWNLOAD_REMOVAL_FAILED.
    - The list's commands: DOWNLOAD_NOW_STARTED, DOWNLOAD_NOW_FINISHED, DOWNLOAD_NOW_FAILED, REMOVE_DOWNLOAD_STARTED, REMOVE_DOWNLOAD_FINISHED, and REMOVE_DOWNLOAD_REFUSED.
    - Keep on This Mac: KEEP_ON_MAC, for keeping and stopping and for a Kept Books.json that can't be read or saved, and KEPT_BOOK_DOWNLOAD_STARTED, KEPT_BOOK_DOWNLOAD_FAILED, and KEPT_BOOK_DOWNLOAD_FINISHED, for the downloads a keep or a refresh starts.
    - The reader: READER_DOWNLOAD_STARTED, READER_DOWNLOAD_FINISHED, READER_DOWNLOAD_FAILED, READER_DOWNLOAD_CANCELLED, READER_READ_REFUSED, WORKING_COPY_MADE, WORKING_COPY_SKIPPED, WORKING_COPY_REMOVED, WORKING_COPY_LEFT, and WORKING_COPIES_SWEPT.
    - Maintenance: MAINTENANCE_SKIPPED_IN_CLOUD, one line for each pass that left books in the cloud.
    - Package records: BOOK_RECORD_IN_CLOUD, once for each book whose saves go to library.json alone while its record is only in the cloud.
    - The data directory: DATA_DIRECTORY_FOUND, DATA_DIRECTORY_AVAILABLE, DATA_DIRECTORY_UNAVAILABLE, DATA_DIRECTORY_UNMOUNTED, DATA_DIRECTORY_LOST, DATA_DIRECTORY_MOVE, DATA_DIRECTORY_SWITCH, LIBRARY_IDENTITY, LIBRARY_SESSION, SUBSTITUTIONS_FILE, and READING_STATS_FILE.
    - Transcripts: TRANSCRIPT_FILE, for a transcript.json that can't be used, preserved beside itself; TRANSCRIPT_STALE, for a transcript whose audio changed since it was made; TRANSCRIPT_TEXT_REBUILT, for content.md and its layer written again from transcript.json; TRANSCRIPT_CLEANUP_RULES, for the user's copy of the cleanup rules, only in the cloud or preserved beside itself; TRANSCRIPT_CLEANED_UP, for a cleanup saved, with its counts and its rules' source and version; TRANSCRIPT_CLEANUP_UNDONE, for a cleanup undone; and TRANSCRIPTION_STARTED, TRANSCRIPTION_FINISHED, TRANSCRIPTION_CANCELLED, and TRANSCRIPTION_FAILED, for a transcription's start and its end. None quotes the transcript or a rule.
    - Recordings: RECORDING_STARTED, RECORDING_START_FAILED, RECORDING_STOPPED, RECORDING_STOP_FAILED, RECORDING_INTERRUPTED, RECORDING_CAPTURE_FAILED, and RECORDING_FORMAT_FALLBACK, for a take starting, stopping, or ending on its own, and a capture in the microphone's own format; RECORDING_SAVED, for a take saved as a book; RECORDING_TAKES_RECOVERED, for the takes left in the Recordings folder that launch found; and RECORDING_WINDOW_REFUSED, for a Record window asked for before there was a library to save into.
    - Audio editing: AUDIO_EDITS_FILE, for an edit history that can't be used, preserved beside itself, which never quotes the history, since it can hold chapter titles; AUDIO_EDITS_SAVED, for edits saved into a book's audio; AUDIO_ORIGINALS_SWEPT and AUDIO_ORIGINAL_REMOVAL_FAILED, for the originals of recordings and edited audiobooks the policy deleted or couldn't; AUDIO_FILTERS_FILE, for the filter presets' file, unusable or only in the cloud; AUDIO_EDIT_CACHES_SWEPT, for the folders a crash, or a quit with editors open, left in this Mac's audio editing caches, removed at launch; and AUDIO_EDITOR, for a book the editor couldn't open, a save or an export it couldn't finish, and markers it couldn't keep, naming an error by its kind, never by a path.
    - Feeds, every one pinned since the feed review of October 10, 2026: FEED_RECORD_IN_CLOUD, FEED_RECORD_SAVE_FAILED, FEED_RECORD_UNREADABLE, FEED_FETCH_STATE_SAVE_FAILED, and FEED_ITEM_POLICY_MOVED, for the records, this Mac's fetch details, and the move to the List and download choice; FEED_SUBSCRIBED, FEED_UNSUBSCRIBED, FEED_RESUBSCRIBED, FEED_REFRESHED, FEED_REFRESH_FAILED, FEED_MOVED, and FEED_PARSE_REPAIRED, for subscribing, unsubscribing, and refreshing; FEED_DOWNLOAD_STARTED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_FINISHED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_FAILED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_CANCELLED, FEED_DOWNLOADS_CANCELLED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_WAITING, FEED_DOWNLOAD_RESTORED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_DISCARDED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_LEFT, and FEED_ARTICLE_TEXT_KEPT, for downloads; FEED_PACKAGE_SWAP_FAILED, FEED_PACKAGE_PUT_BACK, FEED_PACKAGE_SET_ASIDE_REMOVED, and FEED_PACKAGE_SET_ASIDE_LEFT, for the kept package a download replaces; FEED_ITEM_PLAYED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_DELETED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_DELETE_FAILED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_DELETE_REFUSED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_CACHE_LEFT, FEED_DOWNLOAD_HELD_ASIDE, FEED_HELD_ASIDE_SWEPT, FEED_HELD_ASIDE_LEFT, FEED_DOWNLOAD_FORGOTTEN, FEED_LIBRARY_BOOK_GONE, FEED_SWEEP_LEFT_IN_CLOUD, FEED_SWEEP_PUT_OFF, FEED_KEPT_PACKAGE_DELETE_FAILED, and FEED_KEPT_PACKAGES_PUT_OFF, for played items and the removal of downloads and kept packages; FEED_RULE_DOWNLOADS, FEED_RULE_LEFT_IN_CLOUD, and FEED_RULE_PUT_OFF, for the newest-N rule; FEED_INDEX, FEED_INDEX_IN_CLOUD, FEED_INDEX_NEWER, and FEED_INDEX_UNREADABLE, for the feeds' own indexes; FEED_DOWNLOADS_MOVE, FEED_DOWNLOADS_MOVED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_MOVE_FAILED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_MOVE_REFUSED, FEED_ITEMS_KEPT, FEED_ITEMS_DOWNLOADED_AGAIN, FEED_ITEM_MOVED_TO_LIBRARY, FEED_MOVE_TO_LIBRARY_FAILED, FEED_MOVE_TO_LIBRARY_PUT_OFF, FEED_DOWNLOADS_RETURN, FEED_DOWNLOADS_RETURNED, FEED_DOWNLOAD_RETURN_FAILED, FEED_RECORD_ABSENT, and FEED_RECORD_BACK, for moves of downloads into their feeds, into the library, and back from a gone feed; and FEED_DELETED, FEED_DELETE_PUT_OFF, FEED_DELETE_STOPPED, FEED_DELETE_REFUSED, FEED_DELETE_FOLDER_LEFT, and FEED_DELETE_FOLDER_KEPT, for Delete Feed… (FR14).
    - Export and edited text: BOOK_EXPORT and BOOK_EXPORT_FAILED, for how an export ended and each file it couldn't write; TEXT_EDIT_ADOPTED, TEXT_EDIT_REFUSED, TEXT_EDIT_SOURCE_CHANGED, and TEXT_EDIT_FAILED, for an edit taken, refused, left because the book's own file or its transcript changed too, or one that couldn't be read or committed; TEXT_EDIT_HELD, for a rebuild refused over an edit not taken; and TEXT_EDIT_KEPT, for text kept aside before a rebuild, or a transcript's text written again, replaced it.
  - Files: Services/DiagnosticLog.swift, Services/LogWriter.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift, Services/DataDirectoryAvailability.swift, Models/LogLocation.swift, Models/CloudStorageContract.swift, ViewModels/SettingsViewModel.swift, Views/Settings/LogsSettingsSection.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/LogContentPolicyTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/SourceScanTestSupport.swift.

### The hosted-test sandbox

- The process's runtime.
  - Owner: TextMouthRuntime.current, decided once at first use: whether the process hosts the tests (the XCTestConfigurationFilePath environment variable is set), the directory that stands for the user's home, and the preferences store. In a test host they are a TextMouthTests- folder named after the process number, in the temporary directory, and a preferences store inside it, emptied at the start. A new process clears whatever an earlier process with its number left in that folder (clearEarlierSandbox(at:)), moving a kept one aside under its name followed by -kept- and a short suffix.
  - Who may write it: no one; it is immutable. A test that needs the app's behavior builds a runtime value of its own.
  - How others observe it: everything that could reach the user's data follows it: TextMouthPaths.root (which ignores a custom data directory in a test host), this Mac's own folders (MachineLocalPaths), the legacy data locations, AppDelegate's window-restoration setting, and every preference owner. SpeechService's convenience initializer still uses UserDefaults.standard directly. In a test host the cloud refresher never refreshes, TextMouthRelauncher never opens TextMouth again, the Now Playing coordinator takes no part in the system's media session, and the feed engine neither works on its own nor reaches the network (both below); tests install fake cloud services through CloudServices. The opt-in real-drive tests (CloudDriveIntegrationTests) touch only the folders TEXTMOUTH_CLOUD_TEST_ROOTS names, each named TextMouth Cloud Tests, and never move the app's library out of the sandbox.
  - Files: App/TextMouthRuntime.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift.
- Sandboxes left behind.
  - Owner: HostedSandboxCleanup, which the test bundle's principal class, TextMouthTestBundle, registers. As the bundle starts, it deletes, off the main thread, the sandboxes earlier processes left behind (TextMouthRuntime.removeAbandonedSandboxes): folders named for another process that has ended, as isFolderMakerRunning judges it, the one rule of both sweeps of folders that ended processes leave behind, this one and the archive extraction sweep at launch. The system reuses process numbers, so a running process with the folder's number counts as its maker only when it started no later than an hour after the folder was made (folderMakerStartAllowance, and startDate(ofProcess:), read with sysctl); when either date is unknown, the folder is kept, and so are kept sandboxes and anything that is not a plain folder. As the bundle finishes, it waits for that sweep, at most a minute, and deletes its own sandbox (finishWithSandbox()), unless TEXTMOUTH_KEEP_SANDBOX is 1, which marks it kept with a KEPT-SANDBOX.txt file instead. A run that crashes leaves its sandbox to the next run's sweep.
  - Files: App/TextMouthRuntime.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/HostedTestObservers.swift.
- Config saves a test leaves waiting.
  - Owner: ConfigSaveFlush, which TextMouthTestBundle registers too. As each test ends, it writes a config.json save the test left waiting (ConfigService.flushScheduledSave()), so the save never lands during the next test. A test that changes the global settings makes a settings model of its own, makeIsolatedSettings(_:configFile:configWrite:) (TextMouthProductionTests/ConfigTestSupport.swift): its preferences a sandboxed store, its config.json a file of the test's own, never watched, written as the test chooses, and its notifications its own (REV13, which made four copies of it one).
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/ConfigTestSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/HostedTestObservers.swift.
- Redraw counts.
  - Owner: ViewBodyCounter, which each view the hosted tests measure calls on the first line of its body, SearchResultRow among them, so a test tells that the search list draws every row. Debug builds, which the tests run, count each view's body evaluations by name; Release builds compile the calls to nothing.
  - Files: Views/ViewBodyCounter.swift.
- Performance baselines.
  - Owner: PerformanceBaselineRun.shared (TextMouthProductionTests/PerformanceBaselineSupport.swift), the run every PerformanceBaselineTests test in a process shares, and the committed baselines it reads once, TextMouthProductionTests/performance-baselines.json (PerformanceBaselineFile), which name the Mac model, the build configuration, and the date they were recorded with. Before each test, prepareToMeasure() decides, from TEXTMOUTH_PERFORMANCE_STRICT and TEXTMOUTH_PERFORMANCE_RECORD (PerformanceRunSettings), the Mac model and build configuration, and the one-minute load average, whether the test measures, skips with a PERFORMANCE SKIPPED: line, or, when recording, first waits up to five minutes for a quiet Mac. PerformanceRules holds the rules: the median of five runs after one uncounted warm-up, a second five runs for a number more than twice its baseline, which fails only when the lower median still is, a warning at 1.5 times, and a note at half.
  - Who may write it: a recording run, which rewrites the file once every measurement in PerformanceMeasure.all has its number, printing each old and new one; make perf-baselines (Scripts/perf-baselines.sh) starts one, running the tests alone with TEST_RUNNER_TEXTMOUTH_PERFORMANCE_RECORD=1, and puts the file back as it was unless the whole run succeeds.
  - How others observe it: every line the tests print begins with PERFORMANCE. make check runs them strictly, through make test PERFORMANCE_STRICT=1, whose TEST_RUNNER_TEXTMOUTH_PERFORMANCE_STRICT=1 xcodebuild hands the test host as TEXTMOUTH_PERFORMANCE_STRICT, and repeats their PERFORMANCE WARNING: and PERFORMANCE SKIPPED: lines (Scripts/check.sh). The measured inputs are generated (PerformanceFixtures), never the user's library, and the developer's 16 substitution rules are a frozen copy, performance-substitutions.json, so a change to the standard rules never changes what a baseline measured. open.textBook measures a first open, each run first forgetting the kept text through ReadingTextCache.remove(bookID:); open.textBookCached checks that each run read the same kept file.
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/PerformanceBaselineSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/PerformanceBaselineFixtures.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/PerformanceBaselineTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/PerformanceBaselineRulesTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/performance-baselines.json, TextMouthProductionTests/performance-substitutions.json, Scripts/perf-baselines.sh, Scripts/check.sh.
- Whether hosted windows expose their controls to accessibility.
  - Owner: HostedWindowAccessibility (TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupport.swift), which holds this run's answer: whether the AppKit controls SwiftUI draws in a hosted window, its sliders, steppers, pop-up buttons, and text fields, appear among the window's accessibility elements. The development Mac exposes them; a GitHub Actions macOS runner draws the same controls but exposes none of them, so a check that reads them there would fail for want of the capability rather than for a missing control. SwiftUI's own text and buttons have elements only for a connected assistive app, except a Text that can be selected (textSelection(.enabled)), which is drawn through AppKit and appears among those controls as a static text whose value and character count a test reads (ReaderTextAreaAccessibilityTests).
  - Who may write it: the first call of hostedWindowsExposeAccessibility() in a run measures it, on a probe of its own, never on the view under test: it hosts a labelled stepper (LoneStepper) and waits, as withHostedView waits out a slow first render, for a stepper to appear among the window's accessibility elements. HostedWindowAccessibility.assuming(_:_:) puts another answer in its place for the tests of the skip itself, which must see both answers on every Mac, and then puts back the one it had.
  - How others observe it: every test that reads hosted controls through accessibleControls(in:) first calls skipUnlessHostedWindowsExposeAccessibility(), through withHostedControls, which throws for that reason, or on its own, and where the answer is no it is skipped, before its view is hosted, with the one message HostedWindowAccessibility.unavailableMessage; the run's log then has one HOSTED_ACCESSIBILITY_UNAVAILABLE line saying how many steppers the probe drew. Because the probe never looks at the view under test, a control missing from that view, or left without its label, still fails wherever the controls are exposed, as AccessibilityTestSupportTests shows with a stepper left without a label, an expected failure.
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupportTests.swift.
- A hosted view's own work while a test waits.
  - Owner: the test. An asynchronous test method runs as a job of the main queue, and the run loop a test spins while it waits (waitUntil, which withHostedView uses) never drains that queue, so a hosted view's own tasks, such as the Transcribe Audio sheet's loading and a transcription's progress, never run while an asynchronous test waits for them. A test that waits for them is synchronous, as TranscriptionSheetViewTests' are.
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/TranscriptionSheetViewTests.swift.
- SwiftUI's checkboxes and steppers in a hosted test.
  - Owner: the test. SwiftUI draws a Toggle checkbox as a button with no target and no action, so a test can read its state but not press it: what a checkbox's binding does belongs in static functions the test calls, as FeedThresholdControl's isOff(_:rule:), shownNumber(_:remembering:rule:), and value(off:remembering:rule:) and RepeatedSymbolsSettingsView's neverCollapses(_:) are. NSStepper's accessibilityPerformIncrement() on the view changes nothing, so a test raises a stepper through its accessibility element from accessibleControls(in:), as RepeatedSymbolSettingsViewTests and FeedUISettingsTests do. SwiftUI gives a menu Picker's NSPopUpButton no action of its own, so a test chooses an item through NSMenu.performActionForItem(at:), as a click does (FeedItemUIPolicyControlsTests).
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/FeedUISettingsTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/RepeatedSymbolSettingsViewTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/FeedItemUIPolicyControlsTests.swift, Views/Feeds/FeedThresholdControl.swift.
- A hosted view whose width outgrows the width it is offered.
  - Owner: the test. A hosting view sets its window's smallest and largest sizes from its content's, so content whose width follows the width it is given and adds to it, as a macOS form in the columns style did, makes the window chase it until AppKit raises "more Update Constraints in Window passes than there are views in the window", which ends the test host with a crash dialog the user sees (twice on October 10, 2026, in exploratory runs no committed test makes). Host such a view inside a frame of a fixed size, as SettingsTabFitTests hosts each Settings tab at the window's size, or measure it with NSHostingController.sizeThatFits(in:) and no window. The hosting helpers, withHostedView (TextMouthProductionTests/AccessibilityTestSupport.swift) and hostOffscreen (TextMouthProductionTests/ViewsAndWindowsTestSupport.swift), say so in their documentation.
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/SettingsTabFitTests.swift.
- VoiceOver announcements.
  - Owner: VoiceOverAnnouncer, through which the reader, the speech service (a text the speech engine refused, when no reader listens), the library and its import questions, the sleep timer, settings, transcription, the audio editor, and the feed reader announce. Its poster asks VoiceOver to speak in the app and records the announcement instead under hosted tests; a test may install a poster of its own and must restore the previous one. ContentView's folder announcement still posts directly.
  - Files: Services/VoiceOverAnnouncer.swift.
- The network.
  - Owner: FeedNetwork.app, whose sessions refuse every request in a process hosting tests (FeedNetworkRefusal), and FeedCenter.shared, which never works on its own there (FeedEnvironment.worksOnItsOwn): no launch or scheduled refresh, no downloads asked for again from the last quit, no sweep of played downloads, and no pass of the newest-N rule nor its ten-second wait, which a test runs itself (applyItemPolicy(to:retrying:)), though the move of the retired settings to the List and download choice still runs. The feed tests answer their requests, a podcast directory search's included (FeedEnvironment.directory), from a stub server of their own (FeedNetwork.stubbed(protocolClasses:reachability:)), so no test reaches the network. FeedArrivals.shared posts its notifications through InertFeedNotifier there, so no test reaches the system's notification center or shows a permission prompt. FeedTestWorld.finish(_:) stops a test's feed engine, stops its downloads as quitting does, waits for its sweeps and passes, and then waits for every write it asked for; relaunch(_:configuring:) lets a test hook the next engine, and holdImports(_:), through a WorkGate, holds the import queue, as the two formerly racing feed tests do to prove the old reading fails, its records (FeedStore.flush()), its fetch details, and the statistics saves; each world's teardown runs it while the world's folders are still there, so no record write fails in, and is announced into, a later test.
  - Files: Services/Feeds/FeedNetwork.swift, Services/Feeds/FeedCenter.swift, Views/Feeds/FeedArrivals.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/FeedTestSupport.swift.
- The system's media session.
  - Owner: NowPlayingService.shared, which in a process hosting tests takes no part in it (installsSystemIntegrations(in:)): it registers no MediaPlayer command targets, tells the system's Now Playing center nothing, and starts no CoreAudio device listener, though it still routes the commands handed to it among its readers. The test host is TextMouth.app itself, with the bundle identifier of the developer's own copy, and macOS sends the AirPods and the media keys to the app that last said it was playing, so a suite whose readers play silent test audio could otherwise take the developer's AirPods presses. MediaCommandPlaybackTests and NowPlayingOwnershipTests give their readers a coordinator of their own (IntegrationWorld.openReader(nowPlaying:)) and see what the system would be told through a writer of their own, and RecordedSoundEffects (TextMouthProductionTests/IntegrationTestSupport.swift) records the sounds a reader asks for and plays none, for the tests of which sounds a reader makes.
  - Files: Services/NowPlayingService.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/IntegrationTestSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/MediaCommandPlaybackTests.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/NowPlayingOwnershipTests.swift.
- The microphone.
  - Owner: AudioInputPermission, the one place TextMouth asks macOS for the microphone. Its system answer never shows macOS's prompt in a process hosting tests: it answers no instead, unless TEXTMOUTH_MICROPHONE is 1, which the opt-in real-microphone test (RecordingEndToEndTests) needs. Every other recording test hands the recorder an answer of its own (FakeMicrophonePermission) and a capture backend that writes CAF files of generated sound (FakeCaptureBackend), so the suite needs neither a microphone nor the permission.
  - Files: Services/AudioInputPermission.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/RecordingTestSupport.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/RecordingEndToEndTests.swift.
- Reader windows a test leaves open.
  - Owner: ReaderWindowLeakCheck, registered by the test bundle's principal class, TextMouthTestBundle. It notes the reader windows open when each test starts, then closes any new one after the test's teardown blocks and fails the test.
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/HostedTestObservers.swift.
- A data directory a test leaves elsewhere.
  - Owner: DataDirectoryLeakCheck, which TextMouthTestBundle registers too. A move a test publishes takes the whole process's data directory with it, so a test moves it only into a folder from makeDataDirectoryMoveDestination(_:), which puts the data directory, config.json's copy of its path, and its preferences back when the test ends. The check notes the data directory as each test starts, and once the test and its teardown blocks have run, puts back any other and fails the test, naming where it was left.
  - Files: TextMouthProductionTests/HostedTestObservers.swift, TextMouthProductionTests/StorageGenerationTestSupport.swift.
- Build slots.
  - Owner: the build-slot wrapper, .claude/skills/agent-bbs/scripts/build-slot-with-bbs.sh, which runs each build while holding one of three machine-wide slots: directories made atomically with mkdir, released on exit, and reclaimed once they are old and their owner is gone. BUILD_SLOT_ROOT moves the slots elsewhere. Scripts/check.sh uses such a wrapper when TEXTMOUTH_BUILD_SLOT names one.
  - Files: .claude/skills/agent-bbs/scripts/build-slot-with-bbs.sh, Scripts/check.sh.

## Reading a book: the path from library row to spoken audio

A book opens from its row in the Library window: VoiceOver's default action on the row, the row's Open command, or the Open Selected Book shortcut calls LibraryView.openBook(_:), which, at the Reading List, first asks the book's reader to read it from the list (reading from the reading list, above), and then ContentView's onOpenBook, which asks SwiftUI for the reader window whose value is the book's ID. A book already open comes forward instead of opening twice. Requests from elsewhere, such as Finder or a finished book continuing to the next, go through LibraryWindowRouter to the same scene. The scene builds a BookReaderWindowView, which looks the book up in LibraryViewModel and creates the window's ReaderViewModel with a copy of it. When the view appears it marks the book opened, and its window accessor gives the reader its window and registers the window with the router.

The new reader loads at once, on a background queue. It first asks the book storage where the library lives. Anywhere but a cloud location it reads the book in the same step, as it always has. In a cloud location it reads the book's cloud state too, and a book with files only in the cloud downloads first, in the reader window; the book is then read with dataless files left in the cloud, an audiobook after it is cloned into its working copy (getting a book from the cloud, above). A book being edited in the user's own editor, a text book or a transcribed audiobook, first has any edit saved there checked (settlePendingEdit(of:): edited text, above): one taken is read as the book's text, and one that can't be taken stops a text book's load, which says why, and leaves out an audiobook's transcript text. Reading calls BookContentAnalysisService.load, asking for a fresh text cache when the book records an older cache format than MarkdownCacheService.currentCacheVersion, and passes this Mac's ReadingTextCache: a large book whose kept text matches is read from it instead of content.md, and a parsed one is kept in the background. For a text book, loading writes content.md if it is missing; otherwise it checks the cache against its fingerprints, rebuilding it when the source or the cache has changed, reads it, and uses the content index saved in bookInfo.json when that index validates and lines up with the chapters, or indexes the chapters afresh. For an audiobook, loading uses a validated saved index, or else reads the audio through AudioBookParser, waiting for it through AsyncBridge, into chapters whose placeholder text stands for time at ten characters a second. A transcribed audiobook then has its transcript text laid over those chapters, for the text area to show while the audio plays (TranscriptTextLoader.load: transcripts, above). An audiobook's tags are read as well, but not an EPUB's package metadata: showEPUBMetadata() shows it from the book's row (Book.keptEPUBMetadata) when the row keeps every field, and otherwise reads it off the main thread once the book shows, through LibraryBookInfoReader.system's read, which never downloads, laying the book's edits over it as the book is when the read ends, and keeps it on the row and in the book's record (Book.keepEPUBMetadata(readFromFile:)), giving only the fields the row lacks the file's values, so an edit stands and later opens read nothing. Back on the main actor, the reader publishes the content and its headings, lays the book's own metadata edits over what the files hold, taking them from its copy of the book as it is by then, brings the saved position into range, commits a new or changed index through LibraryViewModel.updateContentIndex, saves the position, and claims a waiting autoplay request, if one is there. If the library moved the book while it loaded, the reader finds its chapter files at the new place, and a load that failed because of the move starts again from there. After claiming a waiting autoplay request, the reader prepares the opened chapter's speech off the main thread (prepareOpenedChapterSpeech).

Play starts nothing while the book is loading or while Where Am I? is speaking. For a text book, the reader first prepares what it will say, or takes what it prepared before from the same inputs (prepared speech, above). The chapter's text goes through the playback pipeline (ChapterSpeechInputs.prepare()): skipped sections are removed, runs of a repeated symbol are rewritten (RepeatedSymbolRule, which runs before the pauses because their periods and commas would lengthen runs of those symbols), punctuation pauses are added, and the substitution rules are applied, while a ComposedOffsetMap keeps track of where every spoken character came from. A position anywhere inside a rewritten run maps into the words that replaced it, and back. When nothing speakable remains, the book ends at once through its end-of-book behavior. Otherwise the reader claims the media controls through its NowPlayingSession, plays the play sound, records when playback started (for rewind on resume), starts a listening session in ReadingStatsService, resumes the sleep timer's countdown if this reader owns a paused one, and starts speaking.

Speaking begins a new play of the chapter (ChapterPlay), which gives the speech service a finish callback made for that play, and hands the spoken text and the starting offset to SpeechService.speak, which starts the synthesizer speaking directly at once, from a short first chunk, and renders the same text into an AudioEngineService in the background, through SpeechRenderCoordinator, for the next natural break. The reader then pre-renders the next speakable chapter, recording what that render was asked for as its signature and carrying that chapter's speech to its start; a chapter too long to render ahead is not rendered, and only its speech is prepared, off the main actor. As each word is spoken, onWordSpoken moves the cursor back through the offset map to the original text. The reader refreshes its view at most every 0.12 seconds, gives the library's rows and summary bar its cursor about once a second, through LiveReadingPositions, and saves a durable checkpoint every 30 seconds, into the book's record at once and into library.json within five minutes.

When a chapter's speech finishes, the callback made for its play ignores the end of a play the reader has already left, a later play having begun. Otherwise it saves the position and starts the next speakable chapter after the one that played, as a new play, unless the reader's own sleep timer stops playback at this chapter's end, where the next chapter begins the next heading, or the book ends (expireAtChapterEnd(endingTheHeading:endingTheBook:by:)): then the reader stops there instead, its cursor waiting at the next chapter's start. If the look-ahead holds that chapter's audio, rendered with the signature its speech has now, the engine plays it at once and the chapter after it is pre-rendered; otherwise the chapter is spoken from its current text. When no speakable chapter remains, handleEndOfBook stops, starts the book again, or continues to the next book in the folder, as the book's end-of-book behavior says and the reader's sleep timer allows, or, for a book read from the reading list, goes where the list's options say (finishBookReadFromReadingList). Every end but a loop's calls finishAtTheEnd(replayingAFinishedEnd:) (P07), which tells ReadingStatsService.markBookFinished, unless the end replayed a finished end, writes one BOOK_FINISHED event line, counted, counted already, or a replay, and calls restAtEnd(), which saves the cursor at endOfBookPlace, the last chapter at its full length, so the place's global offset is the book's total, beginning a new playback generation only when that moves it to another chapter. Book.restsAtEnd, a saved place at or past the book's total, with no field of its own, and the reader's cursorRestsAtEnd, its live counterpart, then read the book as finished. A book that neither loops back to its beginning nor hands over to the next book rests finished at its end (hasFinishedBook) and keeps its Now Playing session, so a backward skip that reaches its reader, from the AirPods, the media keys, or the keyboard, plays it again from where the skip lands, while no other book plays (Now Playing and media keys, above); performSkip marks that play replaysFinishedEnd, which the end it reaches takes and a jump or a stop clears, and a forward skip from the end stays there. play() on a book whose cursor rests at its end calls playFromTheEnd(), which repeats the end's choice without playing the end: Stop says EndOfBookWording.finished(_:notes:sleepTimerExpired:) alone, EndOfBookBehavior.stopAndPlayStartsOver, which stops at the end as Stop does, and loop play the book from its beginning through playFromTheBeginning(announcing:) ("Starting again: Dune", or "Restarting: Dune"), and Continue to Next Book and the reading list go on through continueToNextBookInFolder and followReadingListPastThisBook. An audiobook's finished play reports no position (AudioPositionObserverPolicy's hasFinished, from AudioPlaybackService.finishedPlayGeneration), so the player's last tick never moves the cursor off the end.

An audiobook takes the other branch at Play: playAudioChapter begins a new play and loads the chapter's file into AudioPlaybackService, from the book's working copy when the reader has one, playing it from the cursor's time, counted from the chapter's start within the file when the chapter is a time range, at the book's speed. A segmented chapter plays its clips as one chain instead, from the clip that holds the cursor's time. In a cloud location without a working copy, a chapter whose file is only in the cloud downloads first (chapterFilesReadyToPlay), and plays once it is here. The service reports the end of each play once, and the reader moves on from the chapter that played, as it does for speech. A file that cannot be played stops playback where it was, and the reader shows an alert with Retry.

Pausing first ends a Where Am I? announcement, leaving its book paused, then records when playback stopped, pauses the sleep timer's countdown if this reader owns the timer, ends the listening session (crediting its last slice), and saves the position. Closing the window unregisters it from the router and closes the reader, which stops waiting for any download, stops playback, saves the position, releases its Now Playing session, stops the sleep timer if this reader owns it, deletes its working copy off the main thread, stops watching the library's updates to its book, and lets go of everything that grows with the book (a closed reader, above). The book's library row and its bookInfo.json then hold the last checkpoint, and LibraryPersistenceService writes both within half a second, library.json too, since unregistering the book brought its write forward from the checkpoint's deadline, or at the latest when the app quits.

## Shared helpers

These small types each own one rule that several parts of the app follow; use them rather than writing the rule again.

- LibraryWindowRouter (Services/LibraryWindowRouter.swift): decides which window takes each app-wide action.
- WindowCommandRouting (Views/Library/LibraryView.swift): decides which of several open windows takes a menu command or keyboard shortcut posted as a notification: a shortcut goes to the window it was pressed in, a menu command to the key window, and a window not attached yet takes either. Every Library window asks it through LibraryView's takesCommand(_:), and a reader, which counts its own sheets as itself, asks it too, as does an audio editor window's model for the editor's own shortcuts.
- LibraryBookUpdates (ViewModels/LibraryBookUpdates.swift): how open readers learn that the library changed their book or is moving its files.
- ReaderRequestStore (Services/ReaderRequestStore.swift): the one store of requests that wait for a book's reader, Continue to Next Book's (ReaderAutoplayRequests) and the reading list's (ReadingListOpenRequests): one a book, claimed once, thrown away after 30 seconds unless the book's reader holds them while the book downloads, and dropped when the open they wait for is refused (ReaderOpenRefusal).
- LiveReadingPositions (ViewModels/LiveReadingPositions.swift): where readers are between checkpoints. A live position goes there, never into the library, which takes a reader's position only at its checkpoints.
- ReaderSnapshot and SheetOverOneReader (Views/Reader/ReaderSnapshot.swift): what a view shows of its reader, republished only when it changes, and sheets that compare equal while they show the same reader. A view over a playing reader observes a snapshot, never the whole reader.
- TextCacheFingerprint (Models/TextCacheFingerprint.swift): what a text cache was written from, checked by size and date before any file is read.
- MarkdownCacheService.chapterMarker(title:) (Services/MarkdownCacheService.swift): the line that starts a chapter in content.md, which EPUB extraction and a transcript's text both write.
- TranscriptText (Models/TranscriptTextIndex.swift): a transcribed audiobook's text with its timing, sentence by sentence: the sentence heard at a time, the place in the text for a time and the time for a place, and so the reader's audio cursor converted both ways. Search in Book, Where Am I?, and anything else that follows the narration in the text should use it rather than converting time again.
- TranscriptSentence.rewrite(as:) (Models/Transcript.swift): a transcript's sentence given new text, keeping its time in the audio and dropping its words' timings, which no longer fit. An edit in the user's editor (TranscriptEditing.apply) and a cleanup (TranscriptCleanup) both rewrite sentences through it.
- TranscriptAccuracy (Services/TranscriptAccuracy.swift): a transcript aligned word by word with the text that was read aloud, its words compared as the edit alignment compares them (TranscriptEditAlignment.key(of:)), with every apostrophe read as one; the differences sorted into the kinds of mistake a recognizer makes, its confidence weighed against them, and the corrections that would have been right in every place they apply. It is in the app rather than the tests so that later work, such as learning rules from the user's own edits, can use it.
- RowNoteBadge (Views/Library/BookCloudNote.swift): the outlined badge a library row's notes are drawn in, its cloud notes and its transcription note, with a picture hidden from VoiceOver, which only repeats the words.
- PersistenceFailureCenter (Services/PersistenceFailureCenter.swift): where every failed save is reported and settled.
- ImportStaging (Services/ImportStaging.swift): builds a package out of sight and publishes it with one rename.
- FileImportService.namesManagedPackage(_:) (Services/FileImportService.swift): whether a row names a package the library manages, by the package's name and folder, the one check that the deletion guard, the first launch's move of feed downloads, Move to Library…, and the return of a gone feed's downloads share; and Book.FileLocation.packageDirectory(inDataDirectory:) (Models/Book.swift): where a row's package is, the one package rule, which Book.packageDirectory(inDataDirectory:) and the audio editor's AudioBookPlace follow. FileImportService.isManagedPackageName(_:) and isSymbolicLink(_:) are the check's parts, which a feed item's own check shares (FeedItemPackage.isPackageName(_:) and packageForRemoval(of:in:)); TextMouthPaths.bookInfoFileName and contentMarkdownFileName name a package's record and text; and LibraryListCache.index(of:) gives each book's place in a list by ID, the first of an ID winning, for the list cache and the feed rows' lookup (FeedItemRowLookup) alike.
- AudioEditHistoryStore and AudioEditPackage (Services/AudioEditing/): the one way to change a book's audio edit history, under one lock for the whole process, and where a package keeps that history, its originals, and its takes, with the one check that a path a history records stays inside the package.
- DataDirectoryMigrator and DataDirectoryLocation (Services/DataDirectoryMigrator.swift): copy a whole data directory out of sight and publish it with one rename, and take a location as the real folder its links lead to.
- StorageBoundary and StorageGeneration (Services/StorageGeneration.swift): where the data directory is now, and the boundary that keeps the work writing into it from running across a move's publication.
- StorageDestinationAssessment, StorageChoice, and StorageWording (Services/StorageDestinationAssessment.swift): what a folder chosen for the library can become, judged from metadata alone, and the words that offer it.
- DataDirectoryMoveProgress and DataDirectoryMoveWording (ViewModels/DataDirectoryMoveProgress.swift): a move under way, with its Cancel, as every Library window's sheet, Storage settings, and VoiceOver present it.
- OffMainWork (Services/StorageDestinationAssessment.swift): runs blocking work, such as reading or copying a folder that can wait on a drive or the network, on a background dispatch queue, and hands back its result without holding the main actor.
- ArchiveExtraction, ArchiveBudget, ArchiveEntryReader, and IndexedArchive (Services/ArchiveExtraction.swift, Services/ArchiveBudget.swift, Parsers/ArchiveEntryReader.swift): bounded, cancellable ZIP extraction, the one way to read an archive entry, and the one way to find many entries.
- ImportScratchSpace (Services/ImportScratchSpace.swift): where imports stage packages and extract archives, on this Mac or beside the library.
- SettingsPolicy (Models/SettingsPolicy.swift): the table of every validated preference.
- StoredPreference and PreferenceObserver (Models/StoredPreference.swift): one definition of each preference's key and stored form, and notice of its changes.
- KeyBinding.keyNames and KeyBinding.accessibleDisplayString (Models/KeyboardShortcut.swift): the one table of named keys, with each key's words, what the screen shows, and config.json's name, and a shortcut in words, single or double, which the Shortcuts tab, its announcements, the audio editor's hints, and config.json all read. Say or write a key through them rather than naming it again.
- VoiceOverAnnouncer (Services/VoiceOverAnnouncer.swift): every VoiceOver announcement, recorded instead under tests. announceAfterSheetCloses(_:from:), and AnnouncementAfterSheet for a sheet's owner, say what a sheet's work did VoiceOverAnnouncer.afterSheetDelay, 0.6 seconds, after the sheet closes, so the closing never cuts it off (REV11 F6): a subscription, the Move sheet's note of the items it left, Move to Library…, and the Remove question's Delete Download use them, and the feed's own confirmations, Unsubscribe's, Delete Episode's, and Delete Feed's, wait the same way through afterSheetCloses(_:), as does the feed downloads section's Cancel, which moves VoiceOver's focus to the list first and speaks after it (REV13 items 1 and 3).
- focusing(_:first:_:) (Views/Transcription/TranscriptionSheet.swift): marks a sheet's control that takes keyboard focus while the sheet's focus is on it, and that VoiceOver moves to once when it becomes the sheet's first control; the Transcribe Audio sheet and the Clean Up sheet both mark theirs with it.
- VoiceCatalog (Services/VoiceCatalog.swift): the installed voices, listed once off the main thread and again when the system's voices change. Offer its list rather than asking the system for the voices.
- SpeechMarkupGuard (Services/SpeechMarkupGuard.swift): makes tag-shaped text, and the NUL character, harmless to the speech engine at the same UTF-16 length, so an offset into the guarded text is an offset into the original. Every utterance made from a book's text, or quoting a book, as Where Am I? and Speak Info do, goes through it. neutralizedStrictly(_:) silences every bracket, tag-shaped or not, and neutralized(_:strictlyWithin:) does so within a range of a text, for the rest of a chunk the engine refused; neither touches the ampersand.
- TextMouthRuntime (App/TextMouthRuntime.swift): the one switch that keeps hosted tests away from the user's data, and the sandbox it gives each test process instead, deleted when the process is done with it.
- ViewBodyCounter (Views/ViewBodyCounter.swift): how many times a view's body ran, counted in Debug builds for the tests that check what a change redraws.
- DatalessFiles (Models/CloudStorageContract.swift): whether a file's content is only in the cloud, and running work that must never download with dataless files left there.
- LibraryIndexQuarantine (Services/LibraryIndexQuarantine.swift): keeps a file beside itself, never changing the original, under a name marker of the caller's own, .unreadable- for the stores and transcript.json and .edited- for Edit Text's copies of content.md, using an earlier copy of the same bytes again; SavedFile.preserveUnusable reaches it with dataless files left in the cloud.
- SavedFile (Services/SavedFile.swift): reads a small file that TextMouth reads whole and replaces whole, one another Mac writes too or one of this Mac's own, with dataless files left in the cloud, telling a missing file, one only in the cloud, and one that can't be read apart, and preserves one that can't be used before anything replaces it. SavedFile.renameReplacing(_:_:) is the one rename(2) helper, which puts a file in place of another in one step: the log writer's rotation, the reading-text cache, and a filtered audio render's result use it.
- MachineLocalPaths (Models/CloudStorageContract.swift): this Mac's own folders, which never sync: its Logs folder, the working copies, import staging, archive extraction, the takes being recorded (MachineLocalPaths.recordings, in Services/RecordingTake.swift), the feeds' downloads waiting to be imported (MachineLocalPaths.feedDownloads, in Services/Feeds/FeedStore.swift), and, in its Caches folder, the kept reading text, the feeds' fetch details and resume data, audio editing's scratch folders (AudioEditScratch, in Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditComposition.swift), and the audio editor's clips (AudioEditCaches.clipsRoot, in Services/AudioEditing/AudioEditCaches.swift).
- NetworkReachability (Services/NetworkReachability.swift): whether this Mac is online, from one NWPathMonitor, or a test's answer.
- MaintenanceFileAccess (Services/MaintenanceFileAccess.swift): how maintenance asks whether a book's files are in the cloud, and reads them without downloading.
- DiagnosticLog (Services/DiagnosticLog.swift): what a log line may say, and which lines are written.
- LogWriter and TextMouthPaths.logDestination (Services/LogWriter.swift, Services/TextMouthPaths.swift): the one writer of log files, and the one place that decides each line's folder.
- AsyncBridge (Services/AsyncBridge.swift): lets synchronous parsing and analysis wait for async work, such as reading audio through AVFoundation. The work runs at the waiter's priority or .medium, whichever is higher (waitPriority(for:)), so no thread waits on work of lower priority (#1581).
- AudioSegment and AudioClipChain (Models/AudioSegment.swift, Services/AudioPlaybackService.swift): a segmented chapter's clips, never an empty list, and the one conversion between a time in the chapter and a time in a clip's file.
- TimeLeftEstimate and WorkTiming (Models/TimeLeftEstimate.swift): what the finished items of a run took, each by its size where that tells its time, and the time the items still to come should take, rounded as it is said: the one estimate the import overlay and the feed downloads section share, worded by ImportProgressWording.timeLeft(_:).
- PlaceClock (Models/PlaceClock.swift): the clock that names a book's places by times, an audiobook's recording or a text book's words at its speed, and finds the place a time names (P08): Go to Time, the Headings sheet's Time In, and Copy Reading Position's place count on it (the reader, above). PlaceClock.end is its last chapter at its full length, at the clock's length; span(from:to:) adds a span chapter by chapter, so whole chapters last exactly their durations, where subtracting two times carries the rounding of the running total before them (the Headings sheet's Durations).
- TimeEntry (Models/TimeEntry.swift): reads a time typed or pasted into one field as people write it, "1:02:03", "62:03", "3723", or "12:04.3", for the audio editor's Go to Time and Insert Silence and the reader's Go to Time (JumpInputValidator.time(_:length:count:)); it moved from the audio editor, where it was AudioEditorTimeEntry, with P08.
- ProgressObserver (Views/ContentView.swift): a view that observes one progress object alone and draws what its caller makes of it, so the window around it, and the library model, are never redrawn for the progress; the text cache's overlay and Storage rows, the import overlay, and the feed downloads section (FeedDownloadProgressObserver, since the feed follow-ups after FR14) use it.
- FeedDownloadWait.networkWait (Services/Feeds/FeedDownloadManager.swift): the one wording of a wait for a network, "downloads once this Mac is on a network without data limits", which an item's row says as its reason and a download refused on a network with data limits says as its message (FR14, REV13 item 4).
- Book.hasOwnSettings (Models/Book+OwnSettings.swift): whether a book keeps a setting of its own, a voice, speed, or pitch, skipped headings, its end-of-book choice, or a skip unit, the one rule by which a feed item's package is kept once its file goes (FeedItemPackage.holdsListenerState) and by which Delete Download and the library's Remove name its "own settings" (FeedWording.KeptParts).
